## Complete Agent Configuration & LLM Provider Integration Report

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's the complete picture of how the ADK-Go agent is configured and how LLM providers are wired in:

### 1. **ADK-Go Version & Dependencies**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/go.mod`

The project uses:
- **ADK-Go v0.5.0** (`google.golang.org/adk v0.5.0`)
- **google.golang.org/genai v1.40.0** (Google's generative AI client library)
- **No direct OpenAI dependency** in the official `go.mod` - OpenAI is integrated via a custom wrapper

### 2. **LLM Provider Configuration**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/model.go`

The `LLMConfig` struct centralizes provider configuration:

```go
type LLMConfig struct {
	Provider string // "gemini" or "openai"
	Model    string // e.g. "gemini-2.5-flash", "gpt-5.2"
	
	// Gemini
	GoogleAPIKey string
	
	// OpenAI
	OpenAIAPIKey  string
	OpenAIBaseURL string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
}
```

**NewLLMModel function** (lines 27-47) implements provider selection:
- **Gemini path** (default): Uses `google.golang.org/adk/model/gemini.NewModel()` with `genai.ClientConfig`
- **OpenAI path**: Uses custom `internal/agent/openai.NewModel()` wrapper
- Validates that required API keys are present for each provider
- Returns `model.LLM` interface (ADK-standard)

### 3. **Custom OpenAI Integration**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go` (400 lines)

The project implements a full ADK-compatible OpenAI wrapper:

**Key Components:**
- **openaiModel struct** (lines 25-30) - Implements `model.LLM` interface required by ADK
- **NewModel()** factory (lines 32-47) - Creates OpenAI model instances
  - Validates API key presence
  - Defaults BaseURL to `https://api.openai.com/v1`
  - Returns `model.LLM` type for seamless ADK integration
  
**Request/Response Translation:**
- **GenerateContent()** (lines 54-63) - Converts ADK `model.LLMRequest` to OpenAI chat completion format
- **convertContents()** (lines 197-251) - Translates ADK `genai.Content` messages to OpenAI chat messages
- **convertTools()** (lines 274-297) - Converts ADK tool definitions to OpenAI function format
- **convertResponse()** (lines 328-375) - Translates OpenAI responses back to ADK `model.LLMResponse`

**Tool Call Handling:**
- Properly serializes/deserializes function call arguments as JSON
- Merges adjacent assistant tool calls (required by OpenAI API spec)
- Maps finish reasons between OpenAI and ADK formats

**No Streaming:** Current implementation returns single non-streaming response (line 143: `Stream: false`)

### 4. **Agent Architecture & Provider-Specific Logic**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go`

**BuildAgent() function** (lines 77-145) implements two distinct architectures:

**For OpenAI (lines 100-107):**
- **Flat architecture** - All function tools passed directly to root agent
- Uses `llmagent.New()` with Tools field set to projected-scoped function tools
- Reason: OpenAI doesn't support the ADK agenttool sub-agent schema

**For Gemini (lines 110-144) [DEFAULT]:**
- **Sub-agent architecture** - Required because Gemini can't mix grounding tools with function tools on same agent
- **Data sub-agent**: Handles database queries for releases/context sources (function tools)
- **Search sub-agent**: Handles Google Search grounding via `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}`
- **Root agent**: Orchestrates both sub-agents via `agenttool.New()`

**Orchestrator Validation** (lines 187-201):
- Checks that appropriate API key is configured for selected provider
- Returns error if key is missing, allowing graceful degradation

### 5. **Environment Variables Configuration**

**Files:** 
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go` (lines 66-77)
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/agent/main.go` (lines 62-73)

**Provider selection:**
```go
llmProvider := envOr("LLM_PROVIDER", "gemini")  // default: gemini
llmModelDefault := "gemini-2.5-flash"
if llmProvider == "openai" {
	llmModelDefault = "gpt-5.2"
}
```

**Full environment variable set:**

| Variable | Default | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `LLM_PROVIDER` | `gemini` | Provider selection: "gemini" or "openai" |
| `LLM_MODEL` | Varies by provider | Model name (e.g., "gemini-2.5-flash", "gpt-5.2") |
| `GOOGLE_API_KEY` | (empty) | Gemini/Vertex API key - required if `LLM_PROVIDER=gemini` |
| `OPENAI_API_KEY` | (empty) | OpenAI API key - required if `LLM_PROVIDER=openai` |
| `OPENAI_BASE_URL` | `https://api.openai.com/v1` | OpenAI-compatible API base (supports local/proxy endpoints) |

### 6. **Agent Tools & Data Access**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/tools.go`

The agent uses three ADK function tools:
1. **get_releases** - Fetch paginated release list for project
2. **get_release_detail** - Get full release details by ID
3. **list_context_sources** - List runbooks/documentation for project

**AgentDataStore interface** (lines 15-19) - Abstraction for tool data access:
- `ListReleasesByProject()`
- `GetRelease()`
- `ListContextSources()`

All tools are built once per agent run and bound to project scope via `projectID`.

### 7. **Agent Execution Flow**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go`

**RunAgent() lifecycle** (lines 212-241):
1. Mark run as "running"
2. Load project config (including custom agent prompts)
3. Build ADK agent via BuildAgent() with LLM config
4. Create in-memory session via `session.InMemoryService()`
5. Create runner with logging plugin for trace visibility
6. Execute agent with prompt (via `r.Run()`)
7. Parse response as JSON semantic report
8. Persist SemanticRelease and link to agent run
9. Send project-level notifications
10. Mark run as "completed" or "failed"

### 8. **Worker Integration**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/worker.go`

**AgentWorker** - River job processor that:
- Dequeues `AgentJobArgs` from River queue
- Loads agent run from database
- Checks `WaitForAllSources` rule (if enabled, waits for all sources to have target version)
- Delegates to `Orchestrator.RunAgent()`
- Supports snoozing for up to ~1 hour if sources not ready

### 9. **Registration in Main**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go`

**Initialization sequence** (lines 63-85):
1. Parse LLM config from environment variables
2. Create orchestrator: `agentpkg.NewOrchestrator(pgStore, llmConfig)`
3. If orchestrator creation succeeds, register agent worker with River
4. If it fails (due to missing API key), log warning and skip registration
5. Agent jobs remain queued until server is restarted with valid credentials

**Graceful degradation:** If `GOOGLE_API_KEY` (for Gemini) or `OPENAI_API_KEY` (for OpenAI) is missing, the agent worker simply isn't registered; notifications and ingestion continue normally.

### 10. **Dev Entrypoint**

**File:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/agent/main.go`

Standalone binary for interactive agent debugging:
- Takes `--project-id` flag
- Loads project from database
- Builds agent with same LLM config as server
- Launches ADK interactive development UI via `full.NewLauncher()`
- Uses same `BuildAgent()` function as production runtime

---

## Summary

The agent configuration is **cleanly abstracted** with:
- **Single source of truth** for LLM provider logic in `model.go`
- **Provider parity** - Both Gemini and OpenAI pluggable via `model.LLM` interface
- **Different architectures** required by each provider (sub-agent pattern for Gemini, flat for OpenAI)
- **Graceful degradation** - Missing API keys don't crash the system, just disable agent processing
- **Custom OpenAI wrapper** - Full ADK-compatible implementation with proper request/response translation
- **Centralized configuration** - All env vars read in main.go and cmd/agent/main.go, passed through `LLMConfig`

The design allows runtime provider switching without code changes—just set environment variables and restart.

## Research Findings: ADK-Go OpenAI Support

### 1. Does ADK-Go have an official `model/openai` package or similar?

**No.** ADK-Go v0.5.0 (the latest release as of 2026-02-20) has exactly one model implementation:

- `google.golang.org/adk/model/gemini` -- the Gemini/Vertex AI backend

The `model/` directory on the `main` branch of `google/adk-go` still only contains `model/gemini/`. There is no `model/openai`, `model/litellm`, or any other non-Google provider.

Confirmed by examining the installed module at:
`/Users/pc/go/pkg/mod/google.golang.org/adk@v0.5.0/model/`

And the current `main` branch tree via `gh api`.

**Zero** files in ADK-Go v0.5.0 mention "openai" anywhere in the source code.

### 2. What model backends does ADK-Go support natively?

ADK-Go natively supports **only Gemini** (both via Gemini API and Vertex AI). The architecture is:

- **`model.LLM` interface** (`/Users/pc/go/pkg/mod/google.golang.org/adk@v0.5.0/model/llm.go`): Defines the abstract interface with `Name()` and `GenerateContent()` methods.
- **`model/gemini.NewModel()`** (`/Users/pc/go/pkg/mod/google.golang.org/adk@v0.5.0/model/gemini/gemini.go`): The only official implementation. It wraps `google.golang.org/genai.Client`.
- **`internal/llminternal/googlellm`** (`/Users/pc/go/pkg/mod/google.golang.org/adk@v0.5.0/internal/llminternal/googlellm/variant.go`): Contains Gemini-specific helpers like `IsGeminiModel()`, `IsGemini25OrLower()`, `GetGoogleLLMVariant()` used by the runner to enable model-specific behaviors.

Gemini-specific tools (`geminitool` package) like `GoogleSearch` use Gemini-native API features (`genai.Tool{GoogleSearch: ...}`) that are server-side executed and have no equivalent in the OpenAI API.

### 3. Can `agenttool` (sub-agent tools) work with OpenAI?

**Technically yes, but with caveats.** After examining the `agenttool` implementation at `/Users/pc/go/pkg/mod/google.golang.org/adk@v0.5.0/tool/agenttool/agent_tool.go`, the mechanism is as follows:

The `agenttool` works by:
1. **Exposing a `FunctionDeclaration`** (lines 85-115) -- it generates a standard `genai.FunctionDeclaration` with the sub-agent's name, description, and an input schema (defaults to `{type: "OBJECT", properties: {request: {type: "STRING"}}, required: ["request"]}`).
2. **Adding it as a `FunctionDeclaration`** via `ProcessRequest()` (lines 253-287) -- it appends the declaration to `req.Config.Tools[].FunctionDeclarations`, which is the same `genai.Tool` structure used by regular function tools.
3. **Executing the sub-agent** via `Run()` (lines 120-249) -- when the LLM calls the function, the tool spins up a new runner with a new session and delegates to the child agent.

The key insight: **`agenttool` ultimately reduces to a standard function call.** It uses `genai.FunctionDeclaration` with a JSON Schema parameter definition -- this is the exact same format your custom OpenAI wrapper at `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go` already translates via `convertTools()` (lines 275-297).

The comment in your orchestrator at `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` (lines 96-99):

```go
// For Gemini: use sub-agent pattern (required because Gemini can't mix
// grounding tools like GoogleSearch with function tools on the same agent).
// For OpenAI: flat architecture — give function tools directly to root agent
// (OpenAI doesn't support the agenttool schema).
```

**This comment is not strictly accurate about the schema limitation.** The `agenttool` schema is a standard `genai.FunctionDeclaration`, which your OpenAI wrapper already knows how to convert to OpenAI's `tools[].function` format. The actual constraint is that:

- The `agenttool.Declaration()` uses `genai.Schema` with `Type: "OBJECT"` (uppercase), which your `schemaToMap()` function passes through as-is. OpenAI expects lowercase `"object"` in JSON Schema. This could cause issues depending on how strictly the OpenAI API validates the type field.
- The deeper issue is that the **search sub-agent** uses `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` which is a Gemini-proprietary server-side tool with no OpenAI equivalent. So even if `agenttool` itself works, the search sub-agent cannot function with OpenAI.

### Status of Official OpenAI Support

There are **three open PRs** attempting to add OpenAI support, **none merged**:

| PR | Title | Status | Notes |
|----|-------|--------|-------|
| [#242](https://github.com/google/adk-go/pull/242) | Add support for the OpenAI API | Open | Has merge conflicts, focuses on Responses API, basic support without tool calling initially |
| [#314](https://github.com/google/adk-go/pull/314) | Create OpenAI adapter | Open | Minimal description |
| [#342](https://github.com/google/adk-go/pull/342) | feat: add support for OpenAI-compatible third-party provider | Open | Most active (21 comments). Tool calling and agent tools reportedly working. Streaming with tool calls has known issues. Comments confirm it requires changes across other layers for full streaming support |

And **two open feature request issues**:
- [#596](https://github.com/google/adk-go/issues/596) -- "Add Support for OpenAI-Compatible Providers" (most recent)
- [#341](https://github.com/google/adk-go/issues/341) -- "Support Third-Party Providers Using OpenAI-Compatible Format"

A maintainer comment from `@hyangah` on issue #341 notes concern about long-term maintainability:
> *"my biggest concern is whether the code will be maintainable by this repo maintainers for long term. If the REST API changes in the future in a subtle way, or if the OpenAI is moving towards Responses or other directions..."*

### Community Alternative

The third-party adapter [`byebyebruce/adk-go-openai`](https://github.com/byebyebruce/adk-go-openai) exists as a community solution. It wraps the `sashabaranov/go-openai` client to implement `model.LLM`, supports function tool calling, and works with the ADK-Go launcher. However, sub-agent support is not explicitly demonstrated.

### Summary for Your Project

1. **Your custom wrapper** at `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go` is the correct approach for now -- there is no official alternative.
2. **`agenttool` could technically work with OpenAI** since it reduces to standard function calls. The real blocker is that the search sub-agent uses `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` which is Gemini-only. You could use `agenttool` with a data sub-agent for OpenAI if you skip the search sub-agent.
3. **No official OpenAI support is planned for any imminent release** -- the PRs have been open for months with no signs of being merged soon.

Here's a comprehensive summary of my research:

## Current State

Your project already has a working dual-provider setup:

- **`internal/agent/model.go`** — `LLMConfig` struct + `NewLLMModel()` factory that returns `model.LLM` for either provider
- **`internal/agent/openai/openai.go`** — Custom ADK-compatible OpenAI wrapper (~400 lines) that translates between ADK's `genai` types and OpenAI chat completion format
- **`internal/agent/orchestrator.go`** `BuildAgent()` — Two different architectures based on provider

## The Key Architectural Difference

| | Gemini | OpenAI |
|---|---|---|
| **Agent structure** | Sub-agent pattern via `agenttool.New()` (root + data sub-agent + search sub-agent) | Flat — all function tools on root agent |
| **Search** | `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` (Gemini server-side grounding) | Not available |
| **Tool calling** | `genai.FunctionDeclaration` | Translated to `tools[].function` by custom wrapper |

## Can You Reuse the Same Agent Flow?

**Mostly yes, with one blocker:**

1. **`agenttool` (sub-agents) could work with OpenAI** — Under the hood, `agenttool` generates standard `genai.FunctionDeclaration` entries, which your OpenAI wrapper already knows how to convert. The comment in `orchestrator.go:96-99` saying "OpenAI doesn't support the agenttool schema" is **not strictly accurate**. You could potentially use `agenttool` with OpenAI.

2. **The real blocker is `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}`** — This is a Gemini-proprietary server-side tool with no OpenAI equivalent. The sub-agent pattern exists in your Gemini flow specifically because Gemini can't mix grounding tools with function tools on the same agent. OpenAI doesn't have this constraint.

3. **Minor issue: schema type casing** — `agenttool.Declaration()` uses `genai.Schema` with `Type: "OBJECT"` (uppercase). OpenAI expects lowercase `"object"`. Your `schemaToMap()` may need to lowercase the type field.

## Options for Unification

**Option A: Skip search sub-agent for OpenAI, use `agenttool` for data sub-agent**
- Both providers use `agenttool` pattern, but OpenAI skips the Google Search sub-agent
- Gives a more consistent architecture while respecting provider limitations
- Could add an OpenAI-compatible search tool (e.g., Tavily, Brave Search API) as a replacement

**Option B: Use flat architecture for both**
- Remove the sub-agent pattern from Gemini too
- Requires separating `geminitool.GoogleSearch` from function tools (Gemini doesn't allow mixing)
- May not work well for Gemini without rearchitecting

**Option C: Keep current split (status quo)**
- Already works, minimal risk
- Duplicated architecture logic in `BuildAgent()`

## ADK-Go Official OpenAI Support

**There is none yet.** ADK-Go v0.5.0 only ships `model/gemini`. Three PRs are open ([#242](https://github.com/google/adk-go/pull/242), [#314](https://github.com/google/adk-go/pull/314), [#342](https://github.com/google/adk-go/pull/342)) but none merged. Your custom wrapper is the correct approach for now.

    27→// NewLLMModel creates a model.LLM based on the configured provider.
    28→func NewLLMModel(ctx context.Context, cfg LLMConfig) (model.LLM, error) {
    29→	switch cfg.Provider {
    30→	case "gemini", "":
    31→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
    32→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is required for gemini provider")
    33→		}
    34→		return gemini.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, &genai.ClientConfig{
    35→			APIKey: cfg.GoogleAPIKey,
    36→		})
    37→
    38→	case "openai":
    39→		return oaimodel.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, oaimodel.Config{
    40→			APIKey:  cfg.OpenAIAPIKey,
    41→			BaseURL: cfg.OpenAIBaseURL,
    42→		})
    43→
    44→	default:
    45→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unknown LLM provider: %q (supported: gemini, openai)", cfg.Provider)
    46→	}
    47→}
    48→

     1→package agent

     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"encoding/json"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"log/slog"
     8→	"net/http"
     9→	"strings"

    27→
    28→// OrchestratorStore defines all data access methods required by the agent
    29→// orchestrator to load project configuration, run the agent, and persist
    30→// the resulting semantic release.
    31→type OrchestratorStore interface {
    32→	AgentDataStore
    33→	GetProject(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Project, error)
    34→	GetAgentRun(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.AgentRun, error)
    35→	UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx context.Context, id, status string) error
    36→	CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error
    37→	UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx context.Context, id string, semanticReleaseID string) error
    38→	ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx context.Context, projectID string) ([]models.Subscription, error)
    39→	GetChannel(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.NotificationChannel, error)
    40→	ListSourcesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.Source, int, error)
    41→	HasReleaseForVersion(ctx context.Context, sourceID, version string) (bool, error)
    42→}
    43→
    44→const DefaultInstruction = `You are a release intelligence agent analyzing version {{VERSION}} of a software project.
    45→
    46→Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check this version across all available sources.
    47→
    48→Use the available tools to:
    49→1. Fetch releases and find the one matching {{VERSION}} from each source.
    50→2. Inspect release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads) for {{VERSION}} only.
    51→3. Check binary/image availability directly from the source data.
    52→4. Review the project's context sources (runbooks, documentation) for relevant background.
    53→5. Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources
    54→   (e.g., community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption stats, known issues).
    55→
    56→CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.
    57→Do not include any explanation, commentary, or markdown formatting — just the raw JSON.
    58→
    59→The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
    60→{
    61→  "subject": "Ready to Deploy: <Project> <Version> (<Risk Summary>)",
    62→  "risk_level": "CRITICAL|HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW",
    63→  "risk_reason": "Why this risk level (e.g., 'Hard Fork detected in Discord #announcements')",
    64→  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"],
    65→  "changelog_summary": "One-line summary of key changes (e.g., 'Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000')",
    66→  "availability": "GA|RC|Beta",
    67→  "adoption": "Percentage or recommendation (e.g., '12% of network updated (Wait recommended if not urgent)')",
    68→  "urgency": "Critical|High|Medium|Low",
    69→  "recommendation": "Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation for the SRE team",
    70→  "download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"],
    71→  "download_links": ["https://github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/releases/tag/v1.10.15"]
    72→}`
    73→
    74→// BuildAgent creates the ADK-Go LLM agent for a given project. This is used
    75→// by both the production orchestrator and the dev entrypoint to ensure the
    76→// same agent configuration is used everywhere.
    77→func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {
    78→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
    79→	if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
    80→		instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
    81→	}
    82→	// Substitute version placeholder
    83→	instruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", version)
    84→
    85→	llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig)
    86→	if err != nil {
    87→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create LLM model: %w", err)
    88→	}
    89→
    90→	// Create project-scoped function tools.
    91→	functionTools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)
    92→	if err != nil {
    93→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create agent tools: %w", err)
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	// For Gemini: use sub-agent pattern (required because Gemini can't mix
    97→	// grounding tools like GoogleSearch with function tools on the same agent).
    98→	// For OpenAI: flat architecture — give function tools directly to root agent
    99→	// (OpenAI doesn't support the agenttool schema).
   100→	if llmConfig.Provider == "openai" {
   101→		return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   102→			Name:        "release_analyst",
   103→			Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   104→			Model:       llmModel,
   105→			Instruction: instruction,
   106→			Tools:       functionTools,
   107→		})
   108→	}
   109→
   110→	// Gemini path: sub-agent architecture.
   111→
   112→	// Data sub-agent: handles DB queries for releases and context sources.
   113→	dataAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   114→		Name:        "data_agent",
   115→		Description: "Query project releases and context sources from the database. Use this to fetch release lists, release details, and context sources like runbooks and documentation.",
   116→		Model:       llmModel,
   117→		Tools:       functionTools,
   118→	})
   119→	if err != nil {
   120→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create data sub-agent: %w", err)
   121→	}
   122→
   123→	// Search sub-agent: Google Search grounding.
   124→	searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   125→		Name:        "search_agent",
   126→		Description: "Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues.",
   127→		Model:       llmModel,
   128→		Tools:       []tool.Tool{geminitool.GoogleSearch{}},
   129→	})
   130→	if err != nil {
   131→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create search sub-agent: %w", err)
   132→	}
   133→
   134→	// Root agent orchestrates data lookup and web search via sub-agents.
   135→	return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   136→		Name:        "release_analyst",
   137→		Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   138→		Model:       llmModel,
   139→		Instruction: instruction,
   140→		Tools: []tool.Tool{
   141→			agenttool.New(dataAgent, nil),
   142→			agenttool.New(searchAgent, nil),
   143→		},
   144→	})
   145→}
   146→
   147→// checkAllSourcesReady returns true if every source in the project has a
   148→// release matching the target version.
   149→func (o *Orchestrator) checkAllSourcesReady(ctx context.Context, projectID, version string) (bool, error) {
   150→	// Use a high limit to fetch all sources; projects rarely exceed a handful.
   151→	sources, _, err := o.store.ListSourcesByProject(ctx, projectID, 1, 1000)
   152→	if err != nil {
   153→		return false, fmt.Errorf("list sources: %w", err)
   154→	}
   155→	if len(sources) == 0 {
   156→		return true, nil
   157→	}
   158→
   159→	for _, src := range sources {
   160→		has, err := o.store.HasReleaseForVersion(ctx, src.ID, version)
   161→		if err != nil {
   162→			return false, fmt.Errorf("check source %s: %w", src.ID, err)
   163→		}
   164→		if !has {
   165→			slog.Info("agent: source not ready for version",
   166→				"project_id", projectID,
   167→				"source_id", src.ID,
   168→				"version", version,
   169→			)
   170→			return false, nil
   171→		}
   172→	}
   173→	return true, nil
   174→}
   175→
   176→// Orchestrator manages the lifecycle of an agent run: loading project config,
   177→// creating an ADK-Go agent with project-specific tools and instructions,
   178→// running the agent, and persisting the result as a semantic release.
   179→type Orchestrator struct {
   180→	store     OrchestratorStore
   181→	llmConfig LLMConfig
   182→}
   183→
   184→// NewOrchestrator creates a new Orchestrator. It validates that the provided
   185→// LLMConfig has the required API key for the configured provider. If the key
   186→// is missing, it returns an error so the caller can degrade gracefully.
   187→func NewOrchestrator(store OrchestratorStore, cfg LLMConfig) (*Orchestrator, error) {
   188→	switch cfg.Provider {
   189→	case "openai":
   190→		if cfg.OpenAIAPIKey == "" {
   191→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("OPENAI_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   192→		}
   193→	default: // gemini
   194→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
   195→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   196→		}
   197→	}
   198→	return &Orchestrator{
   199→		store:     store,
   200→		llmConfig: cfg,
   201→	}, nil
   202→}
   203→
   204→// RunAgent executes the full agent lifecycle for a given agent run:
   205→//  1. Marks the run as "running".
   206→//  2. Loads the project and builds the agent prompt.
   207→//  3. Creates an ADK-Go LLM agent with project-scoped tools.
   208→//  4. Runs the agent and captures its final text response.
   209→//  5. Parses the response into a SemanticReport.
   210→//  6. Creates a SemanticRelease and links it to the agent run.
   211→//  7. Marks the run as "completed" (or "failed" on error).
   212→func (o *Orchestrator) RunAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) error {
   213→	// Mark as running.
   214→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "running"); err != nil {
   215→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to running: %w", err)
   216→	}
   217→
   218→	// Execute the agent; capture any error to mark the run as failed.
   219→	result, err := o.executeAgent(ctx, run)
   220→	if err != nil {
   221→		slog.Error("agent run failed", "run_id", run.ID, "err", err)
   222→		// Best-effort: mark the run as failed.
   223→		if statusErr := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "failed"); statusErr != nil {
   224→			slog.Error("agent: failed to mark run as failed",
   225→				"run_id", run.ID, "status_err", statusErr)
   226→		}
   227→		return err
   228→	}
   229→
   230→	// Link the semantic release to the agent run and mark completed.
   231→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx, run.ID, result.semanticReleaseID); err != nil {
   232→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run result: %w", err)
   233→	}
   234→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "completed"); err != nil {
   235→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to completed: %w", err)
   236→	}
   237→
   238→	// Send project-level notifications (best-effort; errors are logged, not returned).
   239→	o.sendProjectNotifications(ctx, run, result)
   240→
   241→	return nil
   242→}
   243→
   244→// agentResult holds the output of a successful agent execution, used to
   245→// construct notifications after the semantic release is persisted.
   246→type agentResult struct {
   247→	semanticReleaseID string
   248→	version           string
   249→	reportText        string
   250→	projectName       string
   251→}
   252→
   253→// executeAgent performs the actual LLM agent interaction and returns the
   254→// agentResult containing the semantic release ID and metadata for notifications.
   255→func (o *Orchestrator) executeAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) (*agentResult, error) {
   256→	// Load project.
   257→	slog.Info("agent: loading project", "run_id", run.ID, "project_id", run.ProjectID)
   258→	project, err := o.store.GetProject(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   259→	if err != nil {
   260→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("get project: %w", err)
   261→	}
   262→	slog.Info("agent: project loaded", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name)
   263→
   264→	// Extract target version from the agent run.
   265→	version := run.Version
   266→	if version == "" {
   267→		// Fallback: parse from trigger "auto:version:v1.10.15"
   268→		if strings.HasPrefix(run.Trigger, "auto:version:") {
   269→			version = strings.TrimPrefix(run.Trigger, "auto:version:")
   270→		}
   271→	}
   272→
   273→	// Build the agent using the shared constructor.
   274→	slog.Info("agent: building agent", "run_id", run.ID,
   275→		"provider", o.llmConfig.Provider, "model", o.llmConfig.Model,
   276→		"version", version)
   277→	agentInstance, err := BuildAgent(ctx, o.store, project, o.llmConfig, version)
   278→	if err != nil {
   279→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("build agent: %w", err)
   280→	}
   281→
   282→	// Create in-memory session service and a new session.
   283→	sessionService := session.InMemoryService()
   284→	createResp, err := sessionService.Create(ctx, &session.CreateRequest{
   285→		AppName: "changelogue",
   286→		UserID:  "system",
   287→	})
   288→	if err != nil {
   289→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create session: %w", err)
   290→	}
   291→	sess := createResp.Session
   292→
   293→	// Create the runner with logging plugin for agent trace visibility.
   294→	r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
   295→		AppName:        "changelogue",
   296→		Agent:          agentInstance,
   297→		SessionService: sessionService,
   298→		PluginConfig: runner.PluginConfig{
   299→			Plugins:      []*plugin.Plugin{loggingplugin.MustNew("agent_trace")},
   300→			CloseTimeout: 5 * time.Second,
   301→		},
   302→	})
   303→	if err != nil {
   304→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create runner: %w", err)
   305→	}
   306→
   307→	// Run the agent with a prompt requesting analysis.
   308→	slog.Info("agent: starting LLM run", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name, "version", version)
   309→	var userPrompt string
   310→	if version != "" {
   311→		userPrompt = fmt.Sprintf("Analyze version %s for this project. Cross-check all sources and produce a semantic release report.", version)
   312→	} else {
   313→		userPrompt = "Analyze the recent releases for this project and produce a semantic release report."
   314→	}
   315→	userMsg := genai.NewContentFromText(userPrompt, "user")
   316→
   317→	var finalText string
   318→	eventCount := 0
   319→	for event, err := range r.Run(ctx, "system", sess.ID(), userMsg, agent.RunConfig{}) {
   320→		if err != nil {
   321→			slog.Error("agent: run event error",
   322→				"run_id", run.ID,
   323→				"event_count", eventCount,
   324→				"err", err,
   325→			)
   326→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent run event error: %w", err)
   327→		}
   328→		eventCount++
   329→		if event != nil && event.IsFinalResponse() && event.Content != nil {
   330→			for _, part := range event.Content.Parts {
   331→				if part.Text != "" {
   332→					finalText += part.Text
   333→				}
   334→			}
   335→		}
   336→	}
   337→	slog.Info("agent: LLM run finished",
   338→		"run_id", run.ID,
   339→		"event_count", eventCount,
   340→		"output_length", len(finalText),
   341→	)
   342→
   343→	if finalText == "" {
   344→		slog.Error("agent: produced no output",
   345→			"run_id", run.ID,
   346→			"project_id", run.ProjectID,
   347→			"event_count", eventCount,
   348→		)
   349→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent produced no output")
   350→	}
   351→
   352→	// Parse the agent's response into a SemanticReport.
   353→	report, err := parseReport(finalText)
   354→	if err != nil {
   355→		slog.Warn("agent output was not valid JSON report, storing raw",
   356→			"run_id", run.ID, "parse_err", err, "raw_output", finalText)
   357→		// Fall back to storing the raw text as the summary.
   358→		report = &models.SemanticReport{
   359→			Summary: finalText,
   360→		}
   361→	}
   362→
   363→	reportJSON, err := json.Marshal(report)
   364→	if err != nil {
   365→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("marshal report: %w", err)
   366→	}
   367→
   368→	// Gather release IDs for the semantic_release_sources join table.
   369→	releases, _, err := o.store.ListReleasesByProject(ctx, run.ProjectID, 1, 50)
   370→	if err != nil {
   371→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("list releases for semantic release: %w", err)
   372→	}
   373→	releaseIDs := make([]string, 0, len(releases))
   374→	for _, r := range releases {
   375→		releaseIDs = append(releaseIDs, r.ID)
   376→	}
   377→
   378→	// Use the target version for the semantic release.
   379→	srVersion := version
   380→	if srVersion == "" {
   381→		srVersion = "unknown"
   382→		if len(releases) > 0 {
   383→			srVersion = releases[0].Version
   384→		}
   385→	}
   386→
   387→	now := time.Now()
   388→	sr := &models.SemanticRelease{
   389→		ProjectID:   run.ProjectID,
   390→		Version:     srVersion,
   391→		Report:      reportJSON,
   392→		Status:      "completed",
   393→		CompletedAt: &now,
   394→	}
   395→
   396→	if err := o.store.CreateSemanticRelease(ctx, sr, releaseIDs); err != nil {
   397→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create semantic release: %w", err)
   398→	}
   399→
   400→	slog.Info("agent run produced semantic release",
   401→		"run_id", run.ID,
   402→		"semantic_release_id", sr.ID,
   403→		"version", sr.Version,
   404→	)
   405→
   406→	return &agentResult{
   407→		semanticReleaseID: sr.ID,
   408→		version:           srVersion,
   409→		reportText:        finalText,
   410→		projectName:       project.Name,
   411→	}, nil
   412→}
   413→
   414→// defaultSenders returns a map of channel type to Sender for the supported
   415→// notification channel types (webhook, slack, discord).
   416→func defaultSenders() map[string]routing.Sender {
   417→	httpClient := &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}
   418→	return map[string]routing.Sender{
   419→		"webhook": &routing.WebhookSender{Client: httpClient},
   420→		"slack":   &routing.SlackSender{Client: httpClient},
   421→		"discord": &routing.DiscordSender{Client: httpClient},
   422→	}
   423→}
   424→
   425→// sendProjectNotifications looks up project-level subscriptions and sends a
   426→// notification to each channel with the semantic release report. This is
   427→// best-effort: errors are logged but do not propagate to the caller.
   428→func (o *Orchestrator) sendProjectNotifications(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun, result *agentResult) {
   429→	subs, err := o.store.ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   430→	if err != nil {
   431→		slog.Error("list project subscriptions for notification",
   432→			"project_id", run.ProjectID, "err", err)
   433→		return
   434→	}
   435→	if len(subs) == 0 {
   436→		slog.Debug("agent: no project subscriptions configured",
   437→			"project_id", run.ProjectID)
   438→		return
   439→	}
   440→
   441→	senders := defaultSenders()
   442→
   443→	msg := routing.Notification{
   444→		Title:   fmt.Sprintf("Semantic Release Report: %s %s", result.projectName, result.version),
   445→		Body:    result.reportText,
   446→		Version: result.version,
   447→	}
   448→
   449→	for _, sub := range subs {
   450→		ch, err := o.store.GetChannel(ctx, sub.ChannelID)
   451→		if err != nil {
   452→			slog.Error("get channel for project notification",
   453→				"channel_id", sub.ChannelID, "err", err)
   454→			continue
   455→		}
   456→
   457→		sender, ok := senders[ch.Type]
   458→		if !ok {
   459→			slog.Warn("unknown channel type for project notification", "type", ch.Type)
   460→			continue
   461→		}
   462→
   463→		if err := sender.Send(ctx, ch, msg); err != nil {
   464→			slog.Error("send project notification failed",
   465→				"channel", ch.Name, "type", ch.Type, "err", err)
   466→		} else {
   467→			slog.Info("project notification sent",
   468→				"channel", ch.Name, "project_id", run.ProjectID, "version", result.version)
   469→		}
   470→	}
   471→}
   472→
   473→// parseReport attempts to parse the agent's text output as a SemanticReport JSON.
   474→// It handles cases where the agent wraps the JSON in markdown code blocks.
   475→func parseReport(text string) (*models.SemanticReport, error) {
   476→	// Strip markdown code fences if present.
   477→	cleaned := strings.TrimSpace(text)
   478→	if strings.HasPrefix(cleaned, "```") {
   479→		// Remove opening fence (with optional language tag).
   480→		if idx := strings.Index(cleaned, "\n"); idx != -1 {
   481→			cleaned = cleaned[idx+1:]
   482→		}
   483→		// Remove closing fence.
   484→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(cleaned, "```"); idx != -1 {
   485→			cleaned = cleaned[:idx]
   486→		}
   487→		cleaned = strings.TrimSpace(cleaned)
   488→	}
   489→
   490→	var report models.SemanticReport
   491→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(cleaned), &report); err != nil {
   492→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parse report JSON: %w", err)
   493→	}
   494→
   495→	if report.Subject == "" && report.Summary == "" {
   496→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("report is missing required 'subject' or 'summary' field")
   497→	}
   498→
   499→	return &report, nil
   500→}

     1→package agent
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→
     7→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
     8→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool/functiontool"
     9→
    10→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/models"
    11→)
    12→
    13→// AgentDataStore defines the data access methods that agent tools use to
    14→// research releases and context sources for a given project.
    15→type AgentDataStore interface {
    16→	ListReleasesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.Release, int, error)
    17→	GetRelease(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Release, error)
    18→	ListContextSources(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.ContextSource, int, error)
    19→}
    20→
    21→// --- Tool input/output types ---
    22→
    23→// GetReleasesInput is the input for the get_releases tool.
    24→type GetReleasesInput struct {
    25→	Page    int `json:"page,omitempty"`
    26→	PerPage int `json:"per_page,omitempty"`
    27→}
    28→
    29→// ReleaseItem is a single release in the tool output.
    30→type ReleaseItem struct {
    31→	ID         string `json:"id"`
    32→	SourceID   string `json:"source_id"`
    33→	Version    string `json:"version"`
    34→	RawData    any    `json:"raw_data,omitempty"`
    35→	ReleasedAt string `json:"released_at,omitempty"`
    36→	CreatedAt  string `json:"created_at"`
    37→}
    38→
    39→// GetReleasesOutput is the output for the get_releases tool.
    40→type GetReleasesOutput struct {
    41→	Releases []ReleaseItem `json:"releases"`
    42→	Total    int           `json:"total"`
    43→	Page     int           `json:"page"`
    44→	PerPage  int           `json:"per_page"`
    45→}
    46→
    47→// GetReleaseDetailInput is the input for the get_release_detail tool.
    48→type GetReleaseDetailInput struct {
    49→	ReleaseID string `json:"release_id"`
    50→}
    51→
    52→// GetReleaseDetailOutput is the output for the get_release_detail tool.
    53→type GetReleaseDetailOutput struct {
    54→	ID         string `json:"id"`
    55→	SourceID   string `json:"source_id"`
    56→	Version    string `json:"version"`
    57→	RawData    any    `json:"raw_data,omitempty"`
    58→	ReleasedAt string `json:"released_at,omitempty"`
    59→	CreatedAt  string `json:"created_at"`
    60→}
    61→
    62→// ListContextSourcesInput is the input for the list_context_sources tool.
    63→type ListContextSourcesInput struct {
    64→	Page    int `json:"page,omitempty"`
    65→	PerPage int `json:"per_page,omitempty"`
    66→}
    67→
    68→// ContextSourceItem is a single context source in the tool output.
    69→type ContextSourceItem struct {
    70→	ID     string `json:"id"`
    71→	Type   string `json:"type"`
    72→	Name   string `json:"name"`
    73→	Config any    `json:"config"`
    74→}
    75→
    76→// ListContextSourcesOutput is the output for the list_context_sources tool.
    77→type ListContextSourcesOutput struct {
    78→	Sources []ContextSourceItem `json:"sources"`
    79→	Total   int                 `json:"total"`
    80→	Page    int                 `json:"page"`
    81→	PerPage int                 `json:"per_page"`
    82→}
    83→
    84→// toolFactory holds the store reference and project ID scoped to a single
    85→// agent run. Tools created from this factory are bound to a specific project.
    86→type toolFactory struct {
    87→	store     AgentDataStore
    88→	projectID string
    89→}
    90→
    91→// NewTools creates the ADK function tools for agent research, scoped to a
    92→// specific project. The returned tools use the provided store to query
    93→// releases and context sources belonging to projectID.
    94→func NewTools(store AgentDataStore, projectID string) ([]tool.Tool, error) {
    95→	f := &toolFactory{store: store, projectID: projectID}
    96→
    97→	getReleases, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
    98→		Name:        "get_releases",
    99→		Description: "Fetch recent releases for the current project. Returns a paginated list of releases with version, raw data, and timestamps. Use this to understand what new versions have been published.",
   100→	}, f.getReleases)
   101→	if err != nil {
   102→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create get_releases tool: %w", err)
   103→	}
   104→
   105→	getReleaseDetail, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
   106→		Name:        "get_release_detail",
   107→		Description: "Get detailed information for a specific release by its ID. Returns the full raw data payload from the upstream provider. Use this to inspect changelogs, commit lists, or other release metadata.",
   108→	}, f.getReleaseDetail)
   109→	if err != nil {
   110→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create get_release_detail tool: %w", err)
   111→	}
   112→
   113→	listContextSources, err := functiontool.New(functiontool.Config{
   114→		Name:        "list_context_sources",
   115→		Description: "List the context sources configured for this project. Context sources are references to runbooks, documentation, monitoring dashboards, and other background materials that help you produce richer analysis.",
   116→	}, f.listContextSources)
   117→	if err != nil {
   118→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create list_context_sources tool: %w", err)
   119→	}
   120→
   121→	return []tool.Tool{getReleases, getReleaseDetail, listContextSources}, nil
   122→}
   123→
   124→// getReleases is the handler for the get_releases tool.
   125→func (f *toolFactory) getReleases(ctx tool.Context, input GetReleasesInput) (GetReleasesOutput, error) {
   126→	page := input.Page
   127→	if page < 1 {
   128→		page = 1
   129→	}
   130→	perPage := input.PerPage
   131→	if perPage < 1 || perPage > 50 {
   132→		perPage = 20
   133→	}
   134→
   135→	releases, total, err := f.store.ListReleasesByProject(ctx, f.projectID, page, perPage)
   136→	if err != nil {
   137→		return GetReleasesOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("list releases: %w", err)
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	items := make([]ReleaseItem, 0, len(releases))
   141→	for _, r := range releases {
   142→		item := ReleaseItem{
   143→			ID:        r.ID,
   144→			SourceID:  r.SourceID,
   145→			Version:   r.Version,
   146→			RawData:   r.RawData,
   147→			CreatedAt: r.CreatedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z"),
   148→		}
   149→		if r.ReleasedAt != nil {
   150→			item.ReleasedAt = r.ReleasedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z")
   151→		}
   152→		items = append(items, item)
   153→	}
   154→
   155→	return GetReleasesOutput{
   156→		Releases: items,
   157→		Total:    total,
   158→		Page:     page,
   159→		PerPage:  perPage,
   160→	}, nil
   161→}
   162→
   163→// getReleaseDetail is the handler for the get_release_detail tool.
   164→func (f *toolFactory) getReleaseDetail(ctx tool.Context, input GetReleaseDetailInput) (GetReleaseDetailOutput, error) {
   165→	if input.ReleaseID == "" {
   166→		return GetReleaseDetailOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("release_id is required")
   167→	}
   168→
   169→	release, err := f.store.GetRelease(ctx, input.ReleaseID)
   170→	if err != nil {
   171→		return GetReleaseDetailOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("get release: %w", err)
   172→	}
   173→
   174→	out := GetReleaseDetailOutput{
   175→		ID:        release.ID,
   176→		SourceID:  release.SourceID,
   177→		Version:   release.Version,
   178→		RawData:   release.RawData,
   179→		CreatedAt: release.CreatedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z"),
   180→	}
   181→	if release.ReleasedAt != nil {
   182→		out.ReleasedAt = release.ReleasedAt.Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05Z")
   183→	}
   184→
   185→	return out, nil
   186→}
   187→
   188→// listContextSources is the handler for the list_context_sources tool.
   189→func (f *toolFactory) listContextSources(ctx tool.Context, input ListContextSourcesInput) (ListContextSourcesOutput, error) {
   190→	page := input.Page
   191→	if page < 1 {
   192→		page = 1
   193→	}
   194→	perPage := input.PerPage
   195→	if perPage < 1 || perPage > 50 {
   196→		perPage = 20
   197→	}
   198→
   199→	sources, total, err := f.store.ListContextSources(ctx, f.projectID, page, perPage)
   200→	if err != nil {
   201→		return ListContextSourcesOutput{}, fmt.Errorf("list context sources: %w", err)
   202→	}
   203→
   204→	items := make([]ContextSourceItem, 0, len(sources))
   205→	for _, s := range sources {
   206→		items = append(items, ContextSourceItem{
   207→			ID:     s.ID,
   208→			Type:   s.Type,
   209→			Name:   s.Name,
   210→			Config: s.Config,
   211→		})
   212→	}
   213→
   214→	return ListContextSourcesOutput{
   215→		Sources: items,
   216→		Total:   total,
   217→		Page:    page,
   218→		PerPage: perPage,
   219→	}, nil
   220→}
   221→

     1→package openai
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"bytes"
     5→	"context"
     6→	"encoding/json"
     7→	"fmt"
     8→	"io"
     9→	"iter"
    10→	"net/http"
    11→
    12→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
    13→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    14→)
    15→
    16→const defaultBaseURL = "https://api.openai.com/v1"
    17→
    18→// Config holds connection parameters for an OpenAI-compatible API.
    19→type Config struct {
    20→	APIKey  string
    21→	BaseURL string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
    22→}
    23→
    24→// openaiModel implements model.LLM for OpenAI-compatible chat completion APIs.
    25→type openaiModel struct {
    26→	name    string
    27→	apiKey  string
    28→	baseURL string
    29→	client  *http.Client
    30→}
    31→
    32→// NewModel creates an OpenAI-backed model.LLM.
    33→func NewModel(_ context.Context, modelName string, cfg Config) (model.LLM, error) {
    34→	if cfg.APIKey == "" {
    35→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API key is required")
    36→	}
    37→	baseURL := cfg.BaseURL
    38→	if baseURL == "" {
    39→		baseURL = defaultBaseURL
    40→	}
    41→	return &openaiModel{
    42→		name:    modelName,
    43→		apiKey:  cfg.APIKey,
    44→		baseURL: baseURL,
    45→		client:  &http.Client{},
    46→	}, nil
    47→}
    48→
    49→func (m *openaiModel) Name() string { return m.name }
    50→
    51→// GenerateContent converts the ADK request to an OpenAI chat completion request,
    52→// calls the API, and converts the response back to an LLMResponse.
    53→// Streaming is not implemented; the iterator always yields a single response.
    54→func (m *openaiModel) GenerateContent(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest, _ bool) iter.Seq2[*model.LLMResponse, error] {
    55→	return func(yield func(*model.LLMResponse, error) bool) {
    56→		resp, err := m.doRequest(ctx, req)
    57→		if err != nil {
    58→			yield(nil, err)
    59→			return
    60→		}
    61→		yield(resp, nil)
    62→	}
    63→}
    64→
    65→// Close is a no-op; the HTTP client has no persistent state.
    66→func (m *openaiModel) Close() {}
    67→
    68→// --- OpenAI API types ---
    69→
    70→type chatRequest struct {
    71→	Model       string        `json:"model"`
    72→	Messages    []chatMessage `json:"messages"`
    73→	Tools       []chatTool    `json:"tools,omitempty"`
    74→	Temperature *float32      `json:"temperature,omitempty"`
    75→	TopP        *float32      `json:"top_p,omitempty"`
    76→	MaxTokens   int32         `json:"max_tokens,omitempty"`
    77→	Stop        []string      `json:"stop,omitempty"`
    78→	Stream      bool          `json:"stream"`
    79→}
    80→
    81→type chatMessage struct {
    82→	Role       string     `json:"role"`
    83→	Content    string     `json:"content,omitempty"`
    84→	ToolCalls  []toolCall `json:"tool_calls,omitempty"`
    85→	ToolCallID string     `json:"tool_call_id,omitempty"`
    86→}
    87→
    88→type toolCall struct {
    89→	ID       string       `json:"id"`
    90→	Type     string       `json:"type"`
    91→	Function functionCall `json:"function"`
    92→}
    93→
    94→type functionCall struct {
    95→	Name      string `json:"name"`
    96→	Arguments string `json:"arguments"`
    97→}
    98→
    99→type chatTool struct {
   100→	Type     string       `json:"type"`
   101→	Function chatFunction `json:"function"`
   102→}
   103→
   104→type chatFunction struct {
   105→	Name        string `json:"name"`
   106→	Description string `json:"description,omitempty"`
   107→	Parameters  any    `json:"parameters,omitempty"`
   108→}
   109→
   110→type chatResponse struct {
   111→	Choices []chatChoice `json:"choices"`
   112→	Usage   *chatUsage   `json:"usage,omitempty"`
   113→	Error   *apiError    `json:"error,omitempty"`
   114→}
   115→
   116→type chatChoice struct {
   117→	Message      chatMessage `json:"message"`
   118→	FinishReason string      `json:"finish_reason"`
   119→}
   120→
   121→type chatUsage struct {
   122→	PromptTokens     int32 `json:"prompt_tokens"`
   123→	CompletionTokens int32 `json:"completion_tokens"`
   124→	TotalTokens      int32 `json:"total_tokens"`
   125→}
   126→
   127→type apiError struct {
   128→	Message string `json:"message"`
   129→	Type    string `json:"type"`
   130→	Code    string `json:"code"`
   131→}
   132→
   133→// --- request/response conversion ---
   134→
   135→func (m *openaiModel) doRequest(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) (*model.LLMResponse, error) {
   136→	msgs := convertContents(req)
   137→	tools := convertTools(req)
   138→
   139→	chatReq := chatRequest{
   140→		Model:    m.name,
   141→		Messages: msgs,
   142→		Tools:    tools,
   143→		Stream:   false,
   144→	}
   145→
   146→	if req.Config != nil {
   147→		chatReq.Temperature = req.Config.Temperature
   148→		chatReq.TopP = req.Config.TopP
   149→		if req.Config.MaxOutputTokens > 0 {
   150→			chatReq.MaxTokens = req.Config.MaxOutputTokens
   151→		}
   152→		if len(req.Config.StopSequences) > 0 {
   153→			chatReq.Stop = req.Config.StopSequences
   154→		}
   155→	}
   156→
   157→	body, err := json.Marshal(chatReq)
   158→	if err != nil {
   159→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: marshal request: %w", err)
   160→	}
   161→
   162→	httpReq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, m.baseURL+"/chat/completions", bytes.NewReader(body))
   163→	if err != nil {
   164→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: create http request: %w", err)
   165→	}
   166→	httpReq.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
   167→	httpReq.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+m.apiKey)
   168→
   169→	httpResp, err := m.client.Do(httpReq)
   170→	if err != nil {
   171→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: http request: %w", err)
   172→	}
   173→	defer httpResp.Body.Close()
   174→
   175→	respBody, err := io.ReadAll(httpResp.Body)
   176→	if err != nil {
   177→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: read response: %w", err)
   178→	}
   179→
   180→	if httpResp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
   181→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API returned status %d: %s", httpResp.StatusCode, string(respBody))
   182→	}
   183→
   184→	var chatResp chatResponse
   185→	if err := json.Unmarshal(respBody, &chatResp); err != nil {
   186→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: unmarshal response: %w", err)
   187→	}
   188→
   189→	if chatResp.Error != nil {
   190→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API error: %s (type=%s, code=%s)",
   191→			chatResp.Error.Message, chatResp.Error.Type, chatResp.Error.Code)
   192→	}
   193→
   194→	return convertResponse(&chatResp), nil
   195→}
   196→
   197→// convertContents transforms ADK genai.Content messages into OpenAI chat messages.
   198→func convertContents(req *model.LLMRequest) []chatMessage {
   199→	var msgs []chatMessage
   200→
   201→	// System instruction.
   202→	if req.Config != nil && req.Config.SystemInstruction != nil {
   203→		var text string
   204→		for _, p := range req.Config.SystemInstruction.Parts {
   205→			if p.Text != "" {
   206→				text += p.Text
   207→			}
   208→		}
   209→		if text != "" {
   210→			msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{Role: "system", Content: text})
   211→		}
   212→	}
   213→
   214→	// Conversation contents.
   215→	for _, c := range req.Contents {
   216→		role := mapRole(c.Role)
   217→
   218→		// Check each part: could be text, function_call, or function_response.
   219→		for _, p := range c.Parts {
   220→			switch {
   221→			case p.FunctionCall != nil:
   222→				argsJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionCall.Args)
   223→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{
   224→					Role: "assistant",
   225→					ToolCalls: []toolCall{{
   226→						ID:   p.FunctionCall.ID,
   227→						Type: "function",
   228→						Function: functionCall{
   229→							Name:      p.FunctionCall.Name,
   230→							Arguments: string(argsJSON),
   231→						},
   232→					}},
   233→				})
   234→			case p.FunctionResponse != nil:
   235→				respJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionResponse.Response)
   236→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{
   237→					Role:       "tool",
   238→					Content:    string(respJSON),
   239→					ToolCallID: p.FunctionResponse.ID,
   240→				})
   241→			case p.Text != "":
   242→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{Role: role, Content: p.Text})
   243→			}
   244→		}
   245→	}
   246→
   247→	// Merge consecutive assistant messages with tool_calls.
   248→	msgs = mergeAssistantToolCalls(msgs)
   249→
   250→	return msgs
   251→}
   252→
   253→// mergeAssistantToolCalls combines adjacent assistant messages that each have
   254→// tool_calls into a single message, as OpenAI expects multiple tool_calls in
   255→// one message rather than separate messages.
   256→func mergeAssistantToolCalls(msgs []chatMessage) []chatMessage {
   257→	if len(msgs) == 0 {
   258→		return msgs
   259→	}
   260→	var merged []chatMessage
   261→	for _, msg := range msgs {
   262→		if msg.Role == "assistant" && len(msg.ToolCalls) > 0 && len(merged) > 0 {
   263→			prev := &merged[len(merged)-1]
   264→			if prev.Role == "assistant" && len(prev.ToolCalls) > 0 {
   265→				prev.ToolCalls = append(prev.ToolCalls, msg.ToolCalls...)
   266→				continue
   267→			}
   268→		}
   269→		merged = append(merged, msg)
   270→	}
   271→	return merged
   272→}
   273→
   274→// convertTools transforms ADK genai.Tool definitions into OpenAI tool format.
   275→func convertTools(req *model.LLMRequest) []chatTool {
   276→	if req.Config == nil || len(req.Config.Tools) == 0 {
   277→		return nil
   278→	}
   279→
   280→	var tools []chatTool
   281→	for _, t := range req.Config.Tools {
   282→		for _, fd := range t.FunctionDeclarations {
   283→			ct := chatTool{
   284→				Type: "function",
   285→				Function: chatFunction{
   286→					Name:        fd.Name,
   287→					Description: fd.Description,
   288→				},
   289→			}
   290→			if fd.Parameters != nil {
   291→				ct.Function.Parameters = schemaToMap(fd.Parameters)
   292→			}
   293→			tools = append(tools, ct)
   294→		}
   295→	}
   296→	return tools
   297→}
   298→
   299→// schemaToMap converts a genai.Schema to a JSON-serialisable map that matches
   300→// the OpenAI function parameters format (JSON Schema).
   301→func schemaToMap(s *genai.Schema) map[string]any {
   302→	m := map[string]any{}
   303→	if s.Type != "" {
   304→		m["type"] = string(s.Type)
   305→	}
   306→	if s.Description != "" {
   307→		m["description"] = s.Description
   308→	}
   309→	if len(s.Enum) > 0 {
   310→		m["enum"] = s.Enum
   311→	}
   312→	if s.Items != nil {
   313→		m["items"] = schemaToMap(s.Items)
   314→	}
   315→	if len(s.Properties) > 0 {
   316→		props := map[string]any{}
   317→		for k, v := range s.Properties {
   318→			props[k] = schemaToMap(v)
   319→		}
   320→		m["properties"] = props
   321→	}
   322→	if len(s.Required) > 0 {
   323→		m["required"] = s.Required
   324→	}
   325→	return m
   326→}
   327→
   328→// convertResponse transforms an OpenAI chat completion response into an ADK LLMResponse.
   329→func convertResponse(resp *chatResponse) *model.LLMResponse {
   330→	llmResp := &model.LLMResponse{
   331→		TurnComplete: true,
   332→	}
   333→
   334→	if len(resp.Choices) == 0 {
   335→		llmResp.Content = genai.NewContentFromText("", "model")
   336→		return llmResp
   337→	}
   338→
   339→	choice := resp.Choices[0]
   340→	llmResp.FinishReason = mapFinishReason(choice.FinishReason)
   341→
   342→	content := &genai.Content{Role: "model"}
   343→
   344→	// Text content.
   345→	if choice.Message.Content != "" {
   346→		content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{Text: choice.Message.Content})
   347→	}
   348→
   349→	// Tool calls.
   350→	for _, tc := range choice.Message.ToolCalls {
   351→		var args map[string]any
   352→		if tc.Function.Arguments != "" {
   353→			_ = json.Unmarshal([]byte(tc.Function.Arguments), &args)
   354→		}
   355→		content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{
   356→			FunctionCall: &genai.FunctionCall{
   357→				ID:   tc.ID,
   358→				Name: tc.Function.Name,
   359→				Args: args,
   360→			},
   361→		})
   362→	}
   363→
   364→	llmResp.Content = content
   365→
   366→	if resp.Usage != nil {
   367→		llmResp.UsageMetadata = &genai.GenerateContentResponseUsageMetadata{
   368→			PromptTokenCount:     resp.Usage.PromptTokens,
   369→			CandidatesTokenCount: resp.Usage.CompletionTokens,
   370→			TotalTokenCount:      resp.Usage.TotalTokens,
   371→		}
   372→	}
   373→
   374→	return llmResp
   375→}
   376→
   377→func mapRole(role string) string {
   378→	switch role {
   379→	case "model":
   380→		return "assistant"
   381→	default:
   382→		return role
   383→	}
   384→}
   385→
   386→func mapFinishReason(reason string) genai.FinishReason {
   387→	switch reason {
   388→	case "stop":
   389→		return genai.FinishReasonStop
   390→	case "length":
   391→		return genai.FinishReasonMaxTokens
   392→	case "tool_calls":
   393→		return genai.FinishReasonStop
   394→	case "content_filter":
   395→		return genai.FinishReasonSafety
   396→	default:
   397→		return genai.FinishReasonOther
   398→	}
   399→}
   400→

    15→// Package agenttool provides a tool that allows an agent to call another agent.
    16→// This enables composition of agents, which can be useful for scenarios where
    17→// different types of `genai` tools cannot be used together.
    18→package agenttool
    19→
    20→import (
    21→	"encoding/json"
    22→	"fmt"
    23→	"strings"
    24→
    25→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    26→
    27→	"google.golang.org/adk/agent"
    28→	"google.golang.org/adk/artifact"
    29→	"google.golang.org/adk/internal/llminternal"
    30→	"google.golang.org/adk/internal/utils"
    31→	"google.golang.org/adk/memory"
    32→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
    33→	"google.golang.org/adk/runner"
    34→	"google.golang.org/adk/session"
    35→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
    36→)
    37→
    38→// agentTool implements a tool that allows an agent to call another agent.
    39→type agentTool struct {
    40→	agent             agent.Agent
    41→	skipSummarization bool
    42→}
    43→
    44→// Config holds the configuration for an agent tool.
    45→type Config struct {
    46→	// SkipSummarization, if true, will cause the agent to skip summarization
    47→	// after the sub-agent finishes execution.
    48→	SkipSummarization bool
    49→}
    50→
    51→// New creates a new agent tool.
    52→// If cfg is nil, skipSummarization defaults to false.
    53→func New(agent agent.Agent, cfg *Config) tool.Tool {
    54→	if cfg == nil {
    55→		return &agentTool{
    56→			agent:             agent,
    57→			skipSummarization: false,
    58→		}
    59→	}
    60→	return &agentTool{
    61→		agent:             agent,
    62→		skipSummarization: cfg.SkipSummarization,
    63→	}
    64→}
    65→
    66→// Name implements tool.Tool.
    67→func (t *agentTool) Name() string {
    68→	return t.agent.Name()
    69→}
    70→
    71→// Description implements tool.Tool.
    72→func (t *agentTool) Description() string {
    73→	return t.agent.Description()
    74→}
    75→
    76→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
    77→func (t *agentTool) IsLongRunning() bool {
    78→	return false
    79→}
    80→
    81→// Declaration returns the function declaration for the wrapped agent.
    82→// It generates a function declaration based on the agent's input schema.
    83→// If the agent does not have an input schema, a default schema with a
    84→// "request" string parameter is used.
    85→func (t *agentTool) Declaration() *genai.FunctionDeclaration {
    86→	decl := &genai.FunctionDeclaration{
    87→		Name:        t.Name(),
    88→		Description: t.Description(),
    89→	}
    90→
    91→	var agentInputSchema *genai.Schema
    92→	llmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
    93→	if ok && llmAgent != nil {
    94→		// TODO - understand what build_function_declaration does in python and apply if needed.
    95→		internalLlmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
    96→		if !ok {
    97→			return nil
    98→		}
    99→		agentInputSchema = llminternal.Reveal(internalLlmAgent).InputSchema
   100→	}
   101→
   102→	if agentInputSchema != nil {
   103→		decl.Parameters = agentInputSchema
   104→	} else {
   105→		decl.Parameters = &genai.Schema{
   106→			Type: "OBJECT",
   107→			Properties: map[string]*genai.Schema{
   108→				"request": {Type: "STRING"},
   109→			},
   110→			Required: []string{"request"},
   111→		}
   112→	}
   113→	// TODO - understand how _api_variant affects response type.
   114→	return decl
   115→}
   116→
   117→// Run executes the wrapped agent with the provided arguments.
   118→// It creates a new session for the sub-agent, runs the agent, and returns
   119→// the final result.
   120→func (t *agentTool) Run(toolCtx tool.Context, args any) (map[string]any, error) {
   121→	margs, ok := args.(map[string]any)
   122→	if !ok {
   123→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("agentTool expects map[string]any arguments, got %T", args)
   124→	}
   125→
   126→	if t.skipSummarization {
   127→		if actions := toolCtx.Actions(); actions != nil {
   128→			actions.SkipSummarization = true
   129→		}
   130→	}
   131→
   132→	var agentInputSchema *genai.Schema
   133→	llmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
   134→	isLllmAgent := (ok && llmAgent != nil)
   135→	if isLllmAgent {
   136→		internalLlmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
   137→		if !ok {
   138→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("internal error: failed to convert to llm agent")
   139→		}
   140→		agentInputSchema = llminternal.Reveal(internalLlmAgent).InputSchema
   141→	}
   142→
   143→	var content *genai.Content
   144→	var err error
   145→	if agentInputSchema != nil {
   146→		if err = utils.ValidateMapOnSchema(margs, agentInputSchema, true); err != nil {
   147→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("argument validation failed for agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   148→		}
   149→		jsonData, err := json.Marshal(margs)
   150→		if err != nil {
   151→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error serializing tool arguments for agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   152→		}
   153→		content = genai.NewContentFromText(string(jsonData), genai.RoleUser)
   154→	} else {
   155→		input, ok := margs["request"]
   156→		if !ok {
   157→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("missing required argument 'request' for agent %s", t.agent.Name())
   158→		}
   159→		inputText, ok := input.(string)
   160→		if !ok {
   161→			// Try to convert to string if not already one
   162→			inputText = fmt.Sprint(input)
   163→		}
   164→		content = genai.NewContentFromText(inputText, genai.RoleUser)
   165→	}
   166→
   167→	sessionService := session.InMemoryService()
   168→
   169→	r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
   170→		AppName:        t.agent.Name(),
   171→		Agent:          t.agent,
   172→		SessionService: sessionService,
   173→		// TODO - use forwarding_artifact_service as in python.
   174→		ArtifactService: artifact.InMemoryService(),
   175→		MemoryService:   memory.InMemoryService(),
   176→	})
   177→	if err != nil {
   178→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create runner")
   179→	}
   180→
   181→	stateMap := make(map[string]any)
   182→
   183→	for k, v := range toolCtx.State().All() {
   184→		// Filter out adk internal states.
   185→		if !strings.HasPrefix(k, "_adk") {
   186→			stateMap[k] = v
   187→		}
   188→	}
   189→
   190→	subSession, err := sessionService.Create(toolCtx, &session.CreateRequest{
   191→		AppName: t.agent.Name(),
   192→		UserID:  toolCtx.UserID(),
   193→		State:   stateMap,
   194→	})
   195→	if err != nil {
   196→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create session for sub-agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   197→	}
   198→
   199→	// TODO(dpasiukevich): verify agent loop termination.
   200→	eventCh := r.Run(toolCtx, subSession.Session.UserID(), subSession.Session.ID(), content, agent.RunConfig{
   201→		StreamingMode: agent.StreamingModeSSE,
   202→	})
   203→
   204→	var lastEvent *session.Event
   205→	for event, err := range eventCh {
   206→		if err != nil {
   207→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error during execution of sub-agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   208→		}
   209→		if event.ErrorCode != "" || event.ErrorMessage != "" {
   210→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("error from sub-agent %q (code: %q, message: %q)", t.agent.Name(), event.ErrorCode, event.ErrorMessage)
   211→		}
   212→		if event.LLMResponse.Content != nil {
   213→			lastEvent = event
   214→		}
   215→	}
   216→
   217→	if lastEvent == nil {
   218→		return map[string]any{}, nil
   219→	}
   220→
   221→	lastContent := lastEvent.LLMResponse.Content
   222→	var textParts []string
   223→	for _, part := range lastContent.Parts {
   224→		if part != nil && part.Text != "" {
   225→			textParts = append(textParts, part.Text)
   226→		}
   227→	}
   228→	outputText := strings.Join(textParts, "\n")
   229→
   230→	if outputText == "" {
   231→		return map[string]any{}, nil
   232→	}
   233→	if isLllmAgent {
   234→		internalLlmAgent, ok := t.agent.(llminternal.Agent)
   235→		if !ok {
   236→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("internal error: failed to convert to llm agent")
   237→		}
   238→		if agentOutputSchema := llminternal.Reveal(internalLlmAgent).OutputSchema; agentOutputSchema != nil {
   239→			// Assuming schemautils.ValidateOutputSchema parses the JSON string outputText
   240→			// and validates it against the agentOutputSchema, returning a map[string]any.
   241→			parsedOutput, err := utils.ValidateOutputSchema(outputText, agentOutputSchema)
   242→			if err != nil {
   243→				return nil, fmt.Errorf("output validation failed for sub-agent %s: %w", t.agent.Name(), err)
   244→			}
   245→			return parsedOutput, nil
   246→		}
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	return map[string]any{"result": outputText}, nil
   250→}
   251→
   252→// ProcessRequest adds the agent tool's function declaration to the LLM request.
   253→func (t *agentTool) ProcessRequest(ctx tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
   254→	// TODO extract this function somewhere else, simillar operations are done for
   255→	// other tools with function declaration.
   256→	if req.Tools == nil {
   257→		req.Tools = make(map[string]any)
   258→	}
   259→
   260→	name := t.Name()
   261→	if _, ok := req.Tools[name]; ok {
   262→		return fmt.Errorf("duplicate tool: %q", name)
   263→	}
   264→	req.Tools[name] = t
   265→
   266→	if req.Config == nil {
   267→		req.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}
   268→	}
   269→	if decl := t.Declaration(); decl == nil {
   270→		return nil
   271→	}
   272→	var funcTool *genai.Tool
   273→	for _, tool := range req.Config.Tools {
   274→		if tool != nil && tool.FunctionDeclarations != nil {
   275→			funcTool = tool
   276→			break
   277→		}
   278→	}
   279→	if funcTool == nil {
   280→		req.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, &genai.Tool{
   281→			FunctionDeclarations: []*genai.FunctionDeclaration{t.Declaration()},
   282→		})
   283→	} else {
   284→		funcTool.FunctionDeclarations = append(funcTool.FunctionDeclarations, t.Declaration())
   285→	}
   286→	return nil
   287→}
   288→

### POST /v1/chat/completions - Web Search

Source: https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/tools-web-search_api-mode=chat

Create a chat completion request with web search enabled. The model will retrieve information from the web before generating a response. Supported models include gpt-4o-search-preview, gpt-4o-mini-search-preview, and gpt-5-search-api.

```APIDOC
## POST /v1/chat/completions

### Description
Create a chat completion with web search enabled. The model retrieves current information from the web before responding to queries.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions

### Parameters

#### Request Body
- **model** (string) - Required - One of: `gpt-4o-search-preview`, `gpt-4o-mini-search-preview`, or `gpt-5-search-api`
- **web_search_options** (object) - Required - Configuration object for web search (can be empty {})
- **messages** (array) - Required - Array of message objects with role and content
  - **role** (string) - Required - Role of the message sender (e.g., "user", "assistant")
  - **content** (string) - Required - Content of the message

### Request Example
```
{
  "model": "gpt-4o-search-preview",
  "web_search_options": {},
  "messages": [
    {
      "role": "user",
      "content": "What was a positive news story from today?"
    }
  ]
}
```

### Response

#### Success Response (200)
- **choices** (array) - Array of completion choices
  - **index** (integer) - Index of the choice
  - **message** (object) - The assistant's message
    - **role** (string) - Role of the message ("assistant")
    - **content** (string) - The model response with inline citations
    - **refusal** (string|null) - Refusal message if applicable
    - **annotations** (array) - List of citation annotations
      - **type** (string) - Type of annotation (e.g., "url_citation")
      - **url_citation** (object) - Citation details
        - **url** (string) - URL of the cited source
        - **title** (string) - Title of the cited source
        - **start_index** (integer) - Start character index in response
        - **end_index** (integer) - End character index in response
  - **finish_reason** (string) - Reason for completion (e.g., "stop")

#### Response Example
```
[
  {
    "index": 0,
    "message": {
      "role": "assistant",
      "content": "the model response is here...",
      "refusal": null,
      "annotations": [
        {
          "type": "url_citation",
          "url_citation": {
            "end_index": 985,
            "start_index": 764,
            "title": "Page title...",
            "url": "https://..."
          }
        }
      ]
    },
    "finish_reason": "stop"
  }
]
```

### Code Examples

#### JavaScript
```javascript
import OpenAI from "openai";
const client = new OpenAI();

const completion = await client.chat.completions.create({
  model: "gpt-4o-search-preview",
  web_search_options: {},
  messages: [{
    "role": "user",
    "content": "What was a positive news story from today?"
  }]
});

console.log(completion.choices[0].message.content);
```

#### Python
```python
from openai import OpenAI
client = OpenAI()

completion = client.chat.completions.create(
  model="gpt-4o-search-preview",
  web_search_options={},
  messages=[
    {
      "role": "user",
      "content": "What was a positive news story from today?"
    }
  ]
)

print(completion.choices[0].message.content)
```

#### cURL
```bash
curl -X POST "https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $OPENAI_API_KEY" \
  -H "Content-type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "model": "gpt-4o-search-preview",
    "web_search_options": {},
    "messages": [{
      "role": "user",
      "content": "What was a positive news story from today?"
    }]
  }'
```

### Notes
- Response includes inline citations for URLs found in web search results
- Citations must be displayed as clearly visible and clickable in user interfaces
- The `annotations` array contains `url_citation` objects with URL, title, and character indices
- By default, the model response includes inline citations for referenced sources
```

### Implement Custom Web Search Tool with Chat Completions API

Source: https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/responses-vs-chat-completions_api-mode=responses

Creates a custom web search function for use with OpenAI's Chat Completions API. The function queries an external search API and returns results. This approach requires manual tool implementation since Chat Completions doesn't support native tools natively.

```javascript
async function web_search(query) {
    const fetch = (await import('node-fetch')).default;
    const res = await fetch(`https://api.example.com/search?q=${query}`);
    const data = await res.json();
    return data.results;
}

const completion = await client.chat.completions.create({
  model: 'gpt-5',
  messages: [
    { role: 'system', content: 'You are a helpful assistant.' },
    { role: 'user', content: 'Who is the current president of France?' }
  ],
  functions: [
    {
      name: 'web_search',
      description: 'Search the web for information',
      parameters: {
        type: 'object',
        properties: { query: { type: 'string' } },
        required: ['query']
      }
    }
  ]
});
```

```python
import requests

def web_search(query):
    r = requests.get(f"https://api.example.com/search?q={query}")
    return r.json().get("results", [])

completion = client.chat.completions.create(
    model="gpt-5",
    messages=[
        {"role": "system", "content": "You are a helpful assistant."},
        {"role": "user", "content": "Who is the current president of France?"}
    ],
    functions=[
        {
            "name": "web_search",
            "description": "Search the web for information",
            "parameters": {
                "type": "object",
                "properties": {"query": {"type": "string"}},
                "required": ["query"]
            }
        }
    ]
)
```

### Web Search Tool Implementation

Source: https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/responses-vs-chat-completions_api-mode=responses

Implement custom web search functionality as a tool function that can be called by the model. This example shows how to define and use a web search tool with the Chat Completions API. The function fetches search results from an external API when invoked.

```APIDOC
## Custom Web Search Tool Function

### Description
Implement a web search tool function that the model can invoke. This function makes HTTP requests to a search API and returns results.

### JavaScript Implementation
```javascript
async function web_search(query) {
    const fetch = (await import('node-fetch')).default;
    const res = await fetch(`https://api.example.com/search?q=${query}`);
    const data = await res.json();
    return data.results;
}
```

### Python Implementation
```python
import requests

def web_search(query):
    r = requests.get(f"https://api.example.com/search?q={query}")
    return r.json().get("results", [])
```

### cURL Implementation
```bash
curl https://api.example.com/search \
  -G \
  --data-urlencode "q=your+search+term" \
  --data-urlencode "key=$SEARCH_API_KEY"
```

### Function Parameters
- **query** (string) - The search query string to look up

### Returns
- **results** (array) - Array of search result objects from the API
```

Enabling web search allows the model to provide more current and factually grounded responses by incorporating real-time information from web sources.
