### Enable Web Search Tool in OpenAI Responses API

Source: https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/tools-web-search_api-mode=responses

This snippet demonstrates how to configure the OpenAI Responses API to utilize the `web_search` tool. By including `{"type": "web_search"}` within the `tools` array of the API request, the model gains the ability to perform web searches to answer the provided input prompt. This functionality allows models to access current information from the internet, enhancing the relevance and accuracy of their responses.

```javascript
import OpenAI from "openai";
const client = new OpenAI();

const response = await client.responses.create({
    model: "gpt-5",
    tools: [
        { type: "web_search" }
    ],
    input: "What was a positive news story from today?"
});

console.log(response.output_text);
```

```python
from openai import OpenAI
client = OpenAI()

response = client.responses.create(
    model="gpt-5",
    tools=[{"type": "web_search"}],
    input="What was a positive news story from today?"
)

print(response.output_text);
```

```curl
curl "https://api.openai.com/v1/responses" \
    -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $OPENAI_API_KEY" \
    -d '{
        "model": "gpt-5",
        "tools": [{"type": "web_search"}],
        "input": "what was a positive news story from today?"
    }'
```

```csharp
using OpenAI.Responses;

string key = Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable("OPENAI_API_KEY")!;
OpenAIResponseClient client = new(model: "gpt-5", apiKey: key);

ResponseCreationOptions options = new();
options.Tools.Add(ResponseTool.CreateWebSearchTool());

OpenAIResponse response = (OpenAIResponse)client.CreateResponse([
    ResponseItem.CreateUserMessageItem([
        ResponseContentPart.CreateInputTextPart("What was a positive news story from today?")
    ])
], options);

Console.WriteLine(response.GetOutputText());
```

### POST /responses - Web Search Tool (Native)

Source: https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/responses-vs-chat-completions

Create a response using the newer Responses API with native web search tool support. This simplified endpoint uses OpenAI's native tools without requiring custom function definitions.

```APIDOC
## POST /responses

### Description
Create a response using the Responses API with native web search tool integration. This is the recommended approach for new applications, providing simplified tool configuration and improved reasoning capabilities.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/responses

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **model** (string) - Required - The model identifier (e.g., 'gpt-5')
- **input** (string) - Required - The user input or question
- **tools** (array) - Optional - Array of tool objects to enable
  - **type** (string) - Tool type (e.g., 'web_search')

const answer = await client.responses.create({

- **output_text** (string) - The generated response text
- **model** (string) - Model used for generation
- **usage** (object) - Token usage information
  - **input_tokens** (integer) - Tokens used in input
  - **output_tokens** (integer) - Tokens used in output

### Response Example
```json
{
  "id": "resp-123",
  "output_text": "The current president of France is Emmanuel Macron. He has been in office since May 2017 and was re-elected in 2022.",
  "model": "gpt-5",
  "usage": {
    "input_tokens": 15,
    "output_tokens": 35
  }
}

### POST /v1/responses - Web Search Tool

Source: https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/tools-web-search_api-mode=responses

Create a response with web search enabled using the Responses API. The model can choose to search the web based on the input prompt. This endpoint supports three types of web search: non-reasoning for quick lookups, agentic search with reasoning models for complex workflows, and deep research for extended investigations.

```APIDOC
## POST /v1/responses

### Description
Create a response with web search capability enabled. The model will have access to web search tools and can choose to search the web based on the input prompt to provide up-to-date information with sourced citations.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
https://api.openai.com/v1/responses

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **model** (string) - Required - The model to use (e.g., "gpt-5", "o3-deep-research", "o4-mini-deep-research")
- **tools** (array) - Required - Array of tools to enable. Include {"type": "web_search"} to enable web search
- **input** (string) - Required - The user's query or prompt for the model

### Request Example
```json
{
  "model": "gpt-5",
  "tools": [
    {"type": "web_search"}
  ],
  "input": "What was a positive news story from today?"
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **output_text** (string) - The model's response based on web search results with sourced citations
- **model** (string) - The model used for the response
- **tools_used** (array) - Array of tools that were utilized by the model

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "output_text": "[Model response with web search results and citations]",
  "model": "gpt-5",
  "tools_used": ["web_search"]
}
```

### Web Search Types
1. **Non-reasoning web search** - Fast method for quick lookups. Model sends query to web search tool and returns top results without internal planning.
2. **Agentic search with reasoning models** - Model actively manages search process, analyzes results, and decides whether to continue searching. Better for complex workflows.
3. **Deep research** - Specialized agent-driven method for in-depth investigations. Can tap into hundreds of sources and run for several minutes. Best used with background mode.

### Code Examples

#### JavaScript
```javascript
import OpenAI from "openai";
const client = new OpenAI();

const response = await client.responses.create({
    model: "gpt-5",
    tools: [
        { type: "web_search" },
    ],
    input: "What was a positive news story from today?",
});

console.log(response.output_text);
```

#### Python
```python
from openai import OpenAI
client = OpenAI()

response = client.responses.create(
    model="gpt-5",
    tools=[{"type": "web_search"}],
    input="What was a positive news story from today?"
)

print(response.output_text)
```

#### cURL
```bash
curl "https://api.openai.com/v1/responses" \
    -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $OPENAI_API_KEY" \
    -d '{
        "model": "gpt-5",
        "tools": [{"type": "web_search"}],
        "input": "what was a positive news story from today?"
    }'
```

#### C#
```csharp
using OpenAI.Responses;

string key = Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable("OPENAI_API_KEY")!;
OpenAIResponseClient client = new(model: "gpt-5", apiKey: key);

ResponseCreationOptions options = new();
options.Tools.Add(ResponseTool.CreateWebSearchTool());

OpenAIResponse response = (OpenAIResponse)client.CreateResponse([
    ResponseItem.CreateUserMessageItem([
        ResponseContentPart.CreateInputTextPart("What was a positive news story from today?"),
    ]),
], options);

Console.WriteLine(response.GetOutputText());
```
```

Now I have the full picture. Key finding: OpenAI's web search in Chat Completions API uses `web_search_options: {}` as a top-level parameter (not a tool in the `tools` array). This is similar to Gemini's `GoogleSearch` grounding — it's a model-level feature, not a function call. This actually simplifies the design significantly.

Now I have enough context to propose approaches.

> AGENT

Here are my proposed approaches — all three unify the agent architecture so both providers use `agenttool`, but they differ in how OpenAI web search is wired in.

---

## Approach A: Mirror Gemini's pattern exactly — search as a sub-agent (Recommended)

Both providers get the same 3-agent structure: root → data sub-agent + search sub-agent.

- **Gemini search sub-agent** uses `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` (as today)
- **OpenAI search sub-agent** uses `web_search_options: {}` in the chat completion request (model-level parameter, same idea as Gemini grounding)

The OpenAI wrapper already translates `genai.Tool` entries. We'd add support for recognizing a `GoogleSearch`-equivalent marker in the request config and emitting `web_search_options: {}` in the HTTP body instead.

**Trade-offs:**
- Maximum code reuse — `BuildAgent()` becomes one path with a provider-specific search tool swap
- `web_search_options` only works with specific OpenAI models (`gpt-4o-search-preview`, `gpt-5-search-api`) — forces model selection for search sub-agent
- The search sub-agent would need its own model instance (search-capable model), different from the main model

## Approach B: OpenAI search as a function tool (no sub-agent)

Same 3-agent structure, but the OpenAI search sub-agent uses a custom `functiontool` that calls an external search API (Tavily, Brave, SerpAPI).

**Trade-offs:**
- No model restrictions — any OpenAI model works
- Adds an external API dependency + API key
- OpenAI doesn't control the search; the tool returns raw results for the LLM to interpret

## Approach C: Search via model config, not sub-agent

Instead of a search sub-agent, both providers enable search as a model-level config. Gemini gets `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` as a tool on the root agent. OpenAI gets `web_search_options` on the root agent's requests.

Use `agenttool` only for the data sub-agent (both providers). No search sub-agent at all.

**Trade-offs:**
- Simplest architecture (root + 1 data sub-agent)
- Gemini can't mix grounding tools with function tools on the same agent — meaning the root agent can't have both `agenttool` (data sub-agent) and `GoogleSearch`. **This doesn't work for Gemini.**

---

**My recommendation: Approach A.** It mirrors the existing Gemini pattern exactly and uses OpenAI's native capability. The search model constraint is manageable — you already configure models per provider. The search sub-agent would just use a search-capable model while the root and data agents use the configured `LLM_MODEL`.

Does this approach work for you, or would you prefer a different direction?

> DEVELOPER

Option A

> AGENT

Good. Let me now present the design section by section.

## Section 1: Unified `BuildAgent()` Architecture

The core change: `BuildAgent()` becomes a single code path for both providers. The only difference is which search tool the search sub-agent gets.

```
Root Agent (release_analyst)
├── Data Sub-Agent (data_agent) — agenttool
│   └── functiontools: get_releases, get_release_detail, list_context_sources
└── Search Sub-Agent (search_agent) — agenttool
    └── Gemini: geminitool.GoogleSearch{}
        OpenAI: openaitool.WebSearch{}  ← new
```

`BuildAgent()` stops branching on provider. Instead, it builds a `searchTool` based on provider and passes it to the same sub-agent structure.

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Section 2: OpenAI Web Search Implementation

OpenAI's Chat Completions web search uses `web_search_options: {}` as a top-level request parameter — not a tool in the `tools` array. This is analogous to how Gemini's `GoogleSearch` is a server-side grounding feature rather than a function call.

**New type: `openaitool.WebSearch{}`**

A struct in `internal/agent/openai/` that implements ADK's `tool.Tool` interface, mirroring how `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` works. It:

1. Returns a `nil` FunctionDeclaration (no function schema — it's not a callable tool)
2. In `ProcessRequest()`, sets a marker on the request that the OpenAI wrapper can detect
3. The OpenAI wrapper's `convertTools()` / `doRequest()` recognizes this marker and emits `"web_search_options": {}` in the HTTP body

**Model constraint:** `web_search_options` only works with search-capable models (`gpt-4o-search-preview`, `gpt-4o-mini-search-preview`, `gpt-5-search-api`). The search sub-agent needs its own model instance with a search-capable model name. This means `LLMConfig` gets a new optional field:

```go
type LLMConfig struct {
    // ... existing fields ...
    OpenAISearchModel string // e.g. "gpt-5-search-api", defaults to "gpt-5-search-api"
}
```

**Schema casing fix:** `schemaToMap()` in the OpenAI wrapper lowercases the `Type` field so `agenttool`'s `"OBJECT"` / `"STRING"` schemas become `"object"` / `"string"`.

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Section 3: OpenAI Wrapper Changes

Three changes to `internal/agent/openai/openai.go`:

**1. Schema type lowercasing in `schemaToMap()`**

```go
if s.Type != "" {
    m["type"] = strings.ToLower(string(s.Type))
}
```

**2. Web search options support in `chatRequest`**

Add a `WebSearchOptions` field to the request struct:

```go
type chatRequest struct {
    // ... existing fields ...
    WebSearchOptions *webSearchOptions `json:"web_search_options,omitempty"`
}

type webSearchOptions struct{} // empty object — OpenAI just needs `{}`
```

**3. Detect web search marker in `doRequest()`**

Before building the HTTP request, check if any `genai.Tool` in the request config has `GoogleSearch` set (or our custom marker). If found, set `chatReq.WebSearchOptions = &webSearchOptions{}` and skip that tool entry in `convertTools()`.

This keeps the OpenAI wrapper self-contained — no changes needed to ADK internals.

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Section 4: New `openaitool` Package & WebSearch Tool

New file: `internal/agent/openai/websearch.go`

A minimal `tool.Tool` implementation:

```go
type WebSearch struct{}

func (WebSearch) Name() string                    { return "web_search" }
func (WebSearch) Description() string             { return "Search the web..." }
func (WebSearch) IsLongRunning() bool              { return false }
func (WebSearch) Declaration() *genai.FunctionDeclaration { return nil }
```

**`ProcessRequest()`** — the key method. Instead of adding a FunctionDeclaration, it adds a special `genai.Tool` entry with a marker that the OpenAI wrapper can detect. Two options for the marker:

- **Option 1:** Reuse `genai.Tool{GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{}}` — the same field Gemini uses. The OpenAI wrapper detects `GoogleSearch != nil` and translates it to `web_search_options`.
- **Option 2:** Use a convention like storing a sentinel in `req.Tools` map that `doRequest()` checks.

Option 1 is cleaner — `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` already sets `genai.Tool{GoogleSearch: ...}`, and our OpenAI wrapper just needs to handle it the same way. The `WebSearch` tool would do the same thing `geminitool.GoogleSearch` does internally.

**`Run()`** — returns an error. This tool is never "called" as a function — the model uses web search natively and includes results inline. The `agenttool` runner handles the sub-agent flow; search happens inside the LLM call itself.

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

## Section 5: Environment Variables & Config

One new env var:

| Variable | Default | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` | `gpt-5-search-api` | Search-capable model for OpenAI search sub-agent |

`LLMConfig` gets one new field:

```go
type LLMConfig struct {
    // ... existing ...
    OpenAISearchModel string
}
```

Wired in `cmd/server/main.go` and `cmd/agent/main.go`:

```go
openaiSearchModel := envOr("OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL", "gpt-5-search-api")
```

The search sub-agent creates its own `model.LLM` instance via `NewLLMModel()` with the search model name. The root and data agents continue using `LLM_MODEL`.

For Gemini, this field is ignored — all agents use the same model (Gemini handles search grounding server-side on any model).

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Section 6: Test Strategy

**Unit tests:**

1. **`schemaToMap()` lowercasing** — verify `"OBJECT"` → `"object"`, `"STRING"` → `"string"` in the output map
2. **`convertTools()` with GoogleSearch marker** — verify it emits `web_search_options` and skips the marker tool from the `tools` array
3. **`WebSearch.ProcessRequest()`** — verify it adds the correct `genai.Tool{GoogleSearch: ...}` entry to the request config
4. **`WebSearch.Declaration()`** — verify it returns `nil`

**Integration test (manual):**

- Run the agent dev entrypoint (`cmd/agent`) with `LLM_PROVIDER=openai` and verify:
  - Data sub-agent tool calls work (agenttool round-trip)
  - Search sub-agent triggers web search (visible in response annotations)
  - Final semantic report is produced correctly

No new external dependencies. No database schema changes.

Does this section look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

Good. The full design is approved. Let me write the design doc and commit it.

     1→package agent
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"fmt"
     6→
     7→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
     8→	"google.golang.org/adk/model/gemini"
     9→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    10→
    11→	oaimodel "github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/agent/openai"
    12→)
    13→
    14→// LLMConfig holds the configuration for creating an LLM model.
    15→type LLMConfig struct {
    16→	Provider string // "gemini" or "openai"
    17→	Model    string // e.g. "gemini-2.5-flash", "gpt-5.2"
    18→
    19→	// Gemini
    20→	GoogleAPIKey string
    21→
    22→	// OpenAI
    23→	OpenAIAPIKey  string
    24→	OpenAIBaseURL string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
    25→}
    26→
    27→// NewLLMModel creates a model.LLM based on the configured provider.
    28→func NewLLMModel(ctx context.Context, cfg LLMConfig) (model.LLM, error) {
    29→	switch cfg.Provider {
    30→	case "gemini", "":
    31→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
    32→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is required for gemini provider")
    33→		}
    34→		return gemini.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, &genai.ClientConfig{
    35→			APIKey: cfg.GoogleAPIKey,
    36→		})
    37→
    38→	case "openai":
    39→		return oaimodel.NewModel(ctx, cfg.Model, oaimodel.Config{
    40→			APIKey:  cfg.OpenAIAPIKey,
    41→			BaseURL: cfg.OpenAIBaseURL,
    42→		})
    43→
    44→	default:
    45→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unknown LLM provider: %q (supported: gemini, openai)", cfg.Provider)
    46→	}
    47→}
    48→

     1→package openai
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"bytes"
     5→	"context"
     6→	"encoding/json"
     7→	"fmt"
     8→	"io"
     9→	"iter"
    10→	"net/http"
    11→
    12→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
    13→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    14→)
    15→
    16→const defaultBaseURL = "https://api.openai.com/v1"
    17→
    18→// Config holds connection parameters for an OpenAI-compatible API.
    19→type Config struct {
    20→	APIKey  string
    21→	BaseURL string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
    22→}
    23→
    24→// openaiModel implements model.LLM for OpenAI-compatible chat completion APIs.
    25→type openaiModel struct {
    26→	name    string
    27→	apiKey  string
    28→	baseURL string
    29→	client  *http.Client
    30→}
    31→
    32→// NewModel creates an OpenAI-backed model.LLM.
    33→func NewModel(_ context.Context, modelName string, cfg Config) (model.LLM, error) {
    34→	if cfg.APIKey == "" {
    35→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API key is required")
    36→	}
    37→	baseURL := cfg.BaseURL
    38→	if baseURL == "" {
    39→		baseURL = defaultBaseURL
    40→	}
    41→	return &openaiModel{
    42→		name:    modelName,
    43→		apiKey:  cfg.APIKey,
    44→		baseURL: baseURL,
    45→		client:  &http.Client{},
    46→	}, nil
    47→}
    48→
    49→func (m *openaiModel) Name() string { return m.name }
    50→
    51→// GenerateContent converts the ADK request to an OpenAI chat completion request,
    52→// calls the API, and converts the response back to an LLMResponse.
    53→// Streaming is not implemented; the iterator always yields a single response.
    54→func (m *openaiModel) GenerateContent(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest, _ bool) iter.Seq2[*model.LLMResponse, error] {
    55→	return func(yield func(*model.LLMResponse, error) bool) {
    56→		resp, err := m.doRequest(ctx, req)
    57→		if err != nil {
    58→			yield(nil, err)
    59→			return
    60→		}
    61→		yield(resp, nil)
    62→	}
    63→}
    64→
    65→// Close is a no-op; the HTTP client has no persistent state.
    66→func (m *openaiModel) Close() {}
    67→
    68→// --- OpenAI API types ---
    69→
    70→type chatRequest struct {
    71→	Model       string        `json:"model"`
    72→	Messages    []chatMessage `json:"messages"`
    73→	Tools       []chatTool    `json:"tools,omitempty"`
    74→	Temperature *float32      `json:"temperature,omitempty"`
    75→	TopP        *float32      `json:"top_p,omitempty"`
    76→	MaxTokens   int32         `json:"max_tokens,omitempty"`
    77→	Stop        []string      `json:"stop,omitempty"`
    78→	Stream      bool          `json:"stream"`
    79→}
    80→
    81→type chatMessage struct {
    82→	Role       string     `json:"role"`
    83→	Content    string     `json:"content,omitempty"`
    84→	ToolCalls  []toolCall `json:"tool_calls,omitempty"`
    85→	ToolCallID string     `json:"tool_call_id,omitempty"`
    86→}
    87→
    88→type toolCall struct {
    89→	ID       string       `json:"id"`
    90→	Type     string       `json:"type"`
    91→	Function functionCall `json:"function"`
    92→}
    93→
    94→type functionCall struct {
    95→	Name      string `json:"name"`
    96→	Arguments string `json:"arguments"`
    97→}
    98→
    99→type chatTool struct {
   100→	Type     string       `json:"type"`
   101→	Function chatFunction `json:"function"`
   102→}
   103→
   104→type chatFunction struct {
   105→	Name        string `json:"name"`
   106→	Description string `json:"description,omitempty"`
   107→	Parameters  any    `json:"parameters,omitempty"`
   108→}
   109→
   110→type chatResponse struct {
   111→	Choices []chatChoice `json:"choices"`
   112→	Usage   *chatUsage   `json:"usage,omitempty"`
   113→	Error   *apiError    `json:"error,omitempty"`
   114→}
   115→
   116→type chatChoice struct {
   117→	Message      chatMessage `json:"message"`
   118→	FinishReason string      `json:"finish_reason"`
   119→}
   120→
   121→type chatUsage struct {
   122→	PromptTokens     int32 `json:"prompt_tokens"`
   123→	CompletionTokens int32 `json:"completion_tokens"`
   124→	TotalTokens      int32 `json:"total_tokens"`
   125→}
   126→
   127→type apiError struct {
   128→	Message string `json:"message"`
   129→	Type    string `json:"type"`
   130→	Code    string `json:"code"`
   131→}
   132→
   133→// --- request/response conversion ---
   134→
   135→func (m *openaiModel) doRequest(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) (*model.LLMResponse, error) {
   136→	msgs := convertContents(req)
   137→	tools := convertTools(req)
   138→
   139→	chatReq := chatRequest{
   140→		Model:    m.name,
   141→		Messages: msgs,
   142→		Tools:    tools,
   143→		Stream:   false,
   144→	}
   145→
   146→	if req.Config != nil {
   147→		chatReq.Temperature = req.Config.Temperature
   148→		chatReq.TopP = req.Config.TopP
   149→		if req.Config.MaxOutputTokens > 0 {
   150→			chatReq.MaxTokens = req.Config.MaxOutputTokens
   151→		}
   152→		if len(req.Config.StopSequences) > 0 {
   153→			chatReq.Stop = req.Config.StopSequences
   154→		}
   155→	}
   156→
   157→	body, err := json.Marshal(chatReq)
   158→	if err != nil {
   159→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: marshal request: %w", err)
   160→	}
   161→
   162→	httpReq, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, m.baseURL+"/chat/completions", bytes.NewReader(body))
   163→	if err != nil {
   164→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: create http request: %w", err)
   165→	}
   166→	httpReq.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
   167→	httpReq.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+m.apiKey)
   168→
   169→	httpResp, err := m.client.Do(httpReq)
   170→	if err != nil {
   171→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: http request: %w", err)
   172→	}
   173→	defer httpResp.Body.Close()
   174→
   175→	respBody, err := io.ReadAll(httpResp.Body)
   176→	if err != nil {
   177→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: read response: %w", err)
   178→	}
   179→
   180→	if httpResp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
   181→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API returned status %d: %s", httpResp.StatusCode, string(respBody))
   182→	}
   183→
   184→	var chatResp chatResponse
   185→	if err := json.Unmarshal(respBody, &chatResp); err != nil {
   186→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: unmarshal response: %w", err)
   187→	}
   188→
   189→	if chatResp.Error != nil {
   190→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API error: %s (type=%s, code=%s)",
   191→			chatResp.Error.Message, chatResp.Error.Type, chatResp.Error.Code)
   192→	}
   193→
   194→	return convertResponse(&chatResp), nil
   195→}
   196→
   197→// convertContents transforms ADK genai.Content messages into OpenAI chat messages.
   198→func convertContents(req *model.LLMRequest) []chatMessage {
   199→	var msgs []chatMessage
   200→
   201→	// System instruction.
   202→	if req.Config != nil && req.Config.SystemInstruction != nil {
   203→		var text string
   204→		for _, p := range req.Config.SystemInstruction.Parts {
   205→			if p.Text != "" {
   206→				text += p.Text
   207→			}
   208→		}
   209→		if text != "" {
   210→			msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{Role: "system", Content: text})
   211→		}
   212→	}
   213→
   214→	// Conversation contents.
   215→	for _, c := range req.Contents {
   216→		role := mapRole(c.Role)
   217→
   218→		// Check each part: could be text, function_call, or function_response.
   219→		for _, p := range c.Parts {
   220→			switch {
   221→			case p.FunctionCall != nil:
   222→				argsJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionCall.Args)
   223→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{
   224→					Role: "assistant",
   225→					ToolCalls: []toolCall{{
   226→						ID:   p.FunctionCall.ID,
   227→						Type: "function",
   228→						Function: functionCall{
   229→							Name:      p.FunctionCall.Name,
   230→							Arguments: string(argsJSON),
   231→						},
   232→					}},
   233→				})
   234→			case p.FunctionResponse != nil:
   235→				respJSON, _ := json.Marshal(p.FunctionResponse.Response)
   236→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{
   237→					Role:       "tool",
   238→					Content:    string(respJSON),
   239→					ToolCallID: p.FunctionResponse.ID,
   240→				})
   241→			case p.Text != "":
   242→				msgs = append(msgs, chatMessage{Role: role, Content: p.Text})
   243→			}
   244→		}
   245→	}
   246→
   247→	// Merge consecutive assistant messages with tool_calls.
   248→	msgs = mergeAssistantToolCalls(msgs)
   249→
   250→	return msgs
   251→}
   252→
   253→// mergeAssistantToolCalls combines adjacent assistant messages that each have
   254→// tool_calls into a single message, as OpenAI expects multiple tool_calls in
   255→// one message rather than separate messages.
   256→func mergeAssistantToolCalls(msgs []chatMessage) []chatMessage {
   257→	if len(msgs) == 0 {
   258→		return msgs
   259→	}
   260→	var merged []chatMessage
   261→	for _, msg := range msgs {
   262→		if msg.Role == "assistant" && len(msg.ToolCalls) > 0 && len(merged) > 0 {
   263→			prev := &merged[len(merged)-1]
   264→			if prev.Role == "assistant" && len(prev.ToolCalls) > 0 {
   265→				prev.ToolCalls = append(prev.ToolCalls, msg.ToolCalls...)
   266→				continue
   267→			}
   268→		}
   269→		merged = append(merged, msg)
   270→	}
   271→	return merged
   272→}
   273→
   274→// convertTools transforms ADK genai.Tool definitions into OpenAI tool format.
   275→func convertTools(req *model.LLMRequest) []chatTool {
   276→	if req.Config == nil || len(req.Config.Tools) == 0 {
   277→		return nil
   278→	}
   279→
   280→	var tools []chatTool
   281→	for _, t := range req.Config.Tools {
   282→		for _, fd := range t.FunctionDeclarations {
   283→			ct := chatTool{
   284→				Type: "function",
   285→				Function: chatFunction{
   286→					Name:        fd.Name,
   287→					Description: fd.Description,
   288→				},
   289→			}
   290→			if fd.Parameters != nil {
   291→				ct.Function.Parameters = schemaToMap(fd.Parameters)
   292→			}
   293→			tools = append(tools, ct)
   294→		}
   295→	}
   296→	return tools
   297→}
   298→
   299→// schemaToMap converts a genai.Schema to a JSON-serialisable map that matches
   300→// the OpenAI function parameters format (JSON Schema).
   301→func schemaToMap(s *genai.Schema) map[string]any {
   302→	m := map[string]any{}
   303→	if s.Type != "" {
   304→		m["type"] = string(s.Type)
   305→	}
   306→	if s.Description != "" {
   307→		m["description"] = s.Description
   308→	}
   309→	if len(s.Enum) > 0 {
   310→		m["enum"] = s.Enum
   311→	}
   312→	if s.Items != nil {
   313→		m["items"] = schemaToMap(s.Items)
   314→	}
   315→	if len(s.Properties) > 0 {
   316→		props := map[string]any{}
   317→		for k, v := range s.Properties {
   318→			props[k] = schemaToMap(v)
   319→		}
   320→		m["properties"] = props
   321→	}
   322→	if len(s.Required) > 0 {
   323→		m["required"] = s.Required
   324→	}
   325→	return m
   326→}
   327→
   328→// convertResponse transforms an OpenAI chat completion response into an ADK LLMResponse.
   329→func convertResponse(resp *chatResponse) *model.LLMResponse {
   330→	llmResp := &model.LLMResponse{
   331→		TurnComplete: true,
   332→	}
   333→
   334→	if len(resp.Choices) == 0 {
   335→		llmResp.Content = genai.NewContentFromText("", "model")
   336→		return llmResp
   337→	}
   338→
   339→	choice := resp.Choices[0]
   340→	llmResp.FinishReason = mapFinishReason(choice.FinishReason)
   341→
   342→	content := &genai.Content{Role: "model"}
   343→
   344→	// Text content.
   345→	if choice.Message.Content != "" {
   346→		content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{Text: choice.Message.Content})
   347→	}
   348→
   349→	// Tool calls.
   350→	for _, tc := range choice.Message.ToolCalls {
   351→		var args map[string]any
   352→		if tc.Function.Arguments != "" {
   353→			_ = json.Unmarshal([]byte(tc.Function.Arguments), &args)
   354→		}
   355→		content.Parts = append(content.Parts, &genai.Part{
   356→			FunctionCall: &genai.FunctionCall{
   357→				ID:   tc.ID,
   358→				Name: tc.Function.Name,
   359→				Args: args,
   360→			},
   361→		})
   362→	}
   363→
   364→	llmResp.Content = content
   365→
   366→	if resp.Usage != nil {
   367→		llmResp.UsageMetadata = &genai.GenerateContentResponseUsageMetadata{
   368→			PromptTokenCount:     resp.Usage.PromptTokens,
   369→			CandidatesTokenCount: resp.Usage.CompletionTokens,
   370→			TotalTokenCount:      resp.Usage.TotalTokens,
   371→		}
   372→	}
   373→
   374→	return llmResp
   375→}
   376→
   377→func mapRole(role string) string {
   378→	switch role {
   379→	case "model":
   380→		return "assistant"
   381→	default:
   382→		return role
   383→	}
   384→}
   385→
   386→func mapFinishReason(reason string) genai.FinishReason {
   387→	switch reason {
   388→	case "stop":
   389→		return genai.FinishReasonStop
   390→	case "length":
   391→		return genai.FinishReasonMaxTokens
   392→	case "tool_calls":
   393→		return genai.FinishReasonStop
   394→	case "content_filter":
   395→		return genai.FinishReasonSafety
   396→	default:
   397→		return genai.FinishReasonOther
   398→	}
   399→}
   400→

     3→import (

    28→// OrchestratorStore defines all data access methods required by the agent
    29→// orchestrator to load project configuration, run the agent, and persist
    30→// the resulting semantic release.
    31→type OrchestratorStore interface {
    32→	AgentDataStore
    33→	GetProject(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Project, error)
    34→	GetAgentRun(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.AgentRun, error)
    35→	UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx context.Context, id, status string) error
    36→	CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error
    37→	UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx context.Context, id string, semanticReleaseID string) error
    38→	ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx context.Context, projectID string) ([]models.Subscription, error)
    39→	GetChannel(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.NotificationChannel, error)
    40→	ListSourcesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.Source, int, error)
    41→	HasReleaseForVersion(ctx context.Context, sourceID, version string) (bool, error)
    42→}
    43→
    44→const DefaultInstruction = `You are a release intelligence agent analyzing version {{VERSION}} of a software project.
    45→
    46→Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check this version across all available sources.
    47→
    48→Use the available tools to:
    49→1. Fetch releases and find the one matching {{VERSION}} from each source.
    50→2. Inspect release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads) for {{VERSION}} only.
    51→3. Check binary/image availability directly from the source data.
    52→4. Review the project's context sources (runbooks, documentation) for relevant background.
    53→5. Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources
    54→   (e.g., community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption stats, known issues).
    55→
    56→CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.
    57→Do not include any explanation, commentary, or markdown formatting — just the raw JSON.
    58→
    59→For download_links: prefer direct binary/artifact URLs for specific platforms (e.g., linux-amd64, darwin-arm64, windows-amd64 .tar.gz/.zip files) over generic release pages. Include the general release page URL as well, but prioritize direct download links when available.
    60→
    61→The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
    62→{
    63→  "subject": "Ready to Deploy: <Project> <Version> (<Risk Summary>)",
    64→  "risk_level": "CRITICAL|HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW",
    65→  "risk_reason": "Why this risk level (e.g., 'Hard Fork detected in Discord #announcements')",
    66→  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"],
    67→  "changelog_summary": "One-line summary of key changes (e.g., 'Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000')",
    68→  "availability": "GA|RC|Beta",
    69→  "adoption": "Percentage or recommendation (e.g., '12% of network updated (Wait recommended if not urgent)')",
    70→  "urgency": "Critical|High|Medium|Low",
    71→  "recommendation": "Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation for the SRE team",
    72→  "download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"],
    73→  "download_links": ["https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-linux-amd64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-darwin-arm64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/releases/tag/v1.10.15"]
    74→}`
    75→
    76→// BuildAgent creates the ADK-Go LLM agent for a given project. This is used
    77→// by both the production orchestrator and the dev entrypoint to ensure the
    78→// same agent configuration is used everywhere.
    79→func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {
    80→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
    81→	if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
    82→		instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
    83→	}
    84→	// Substitute version placeholder
    85→	instruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", version)
    86→
    87→	llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig)
    88→	if err != nil {
    89→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create LLM model: %w", err)
    90→	}
    91→
    92→	// Create project-scoped function tools.
    93→	functionTools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)
    94→	if err != nil {
    95→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create agent tools: %w", err)
    96→	}
    97→
    98→	// For Gemini: use sub-agent pattern (required because Gemini can't mix
    99→	// grounding tools like GoogleSearch with function tools on the same agent).
   100→	// For OpenAI: flat architecture — give function tools directly to root agent
   101→	// (OpenAI doesn't support the agenttool schema).
   102→	if llmConfig.Provider == "openai" {
   103→		return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   104→			Name:        "release_analyst",
   105→			Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   106→			Model:       llmModel,
   107→			Instruction: instruction,
   108→			Tools:       functionTools,
   109→		})
   110→	}
   111→
   112→	// Gemini path: sub-agent architecture.
   113→
   114→	// Data sub-agent: handles DB queries for releases and context sources.
   115→	dataAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   116→		Name:        "data_agent",
   117→		Description: "Query project releases and context sources from the database. Use this to fetch release lists, release details, and context sources like runbooks and documentation.",
   118→		Model:       llmModel,
   119→		Tools:       functionTools,
   120→	})
   121→	if err != nil {
   122→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create data sub-agent: %w", err)
   123→	}
   124→
   125→	// Search sub-agent: Google Search grounding.
   126→	searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   127→		Name:        "search_agent",
   128→		Description: "Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues.",
   129→		Model:       llmModel,
   130→		Tools:       []tool.Tool{geminitool.GoogleSearch{}},
   131→	})
   132→	if err != nil {
   133→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create search sub-agent: %w", err)
   134→	}
   135→
   136→	// Root agent orchestrates data lookup and web search via sub-agents.
   137→	return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   138→		Name:        "release_analyst",
   139→		Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   140→		Model:       llmModel,
   141→		Instruction: instruction,
   142→		Tools: []tool.Tool{
   143→			agenttool.New(dataAgent, nil),
   144→			agenttool.New(searchAgent, nil),
   145→		},
   146→	})
   147→}
   148→
   149→// checkAllSourcesReady returns true if every source in the project has a
   150→// release matching the target version.
   151→func (o *Orchestrator) checkAllSourcesReady(ctx context.Context, projectID, version string) (bool, error) {
   152→	// Use a high limit to fetch all sources; projects rarely exceed a handful.
   153→	sources, _, err := o.store.ListSourcesByProject(ctx, projectID, 1, 1000)
   154→	if err != nil {
   155→		return false, fmt.Errorf("list sources: %w", err)
   156→	}
   157→	if len(sources) == 0 {
   158→		return true, nil
   159→	}
   160→
   161→	for _, src := range sources {
   162→		has, err := o.store.HasReleaseForVersion(ctx, src.ID, version)
   163→		if err != nil {
   164→			return false, fmt.Errorf("check source %s: %w", src.ID, err)
   165→		}
   166→		if !has {
   167→			slog.Info("agent: source not ready for version",
   168→				"project_id", projectID,
   169→				"source_id", src.ID,
   170→				"version", version,
   171→			)
   172→			return false, nil
   173→		}
   174→	}
   175→	return true, nil
   176→}
   177→
   178→// Orchestrator manages the lifecycle of an agent run: loading project config,
   179→// creating an ADK-Go agent with project-specific tools and instructions,
   180→// running the agent, and persisting the result as a semantic release.
   181→type Orchestrator struct {
   182→	store     OrchestratorStore
   183→	llmConfig LLMConfig
   184→}
   185→
   186→// NewOrchestrator creates a new Orchestrator. It validates that the provided
   187→// LLMConfig has the required API key for the configured provider. If the key
   188→// is missing, it returns an error so the caller can degrade gracefully.
   189→func NewOrchestrator(store OrchestratorStore, cfg LLMConfig) (*Orchestrator, error) {
   190→	switch cfg.Provider {
   191→	case "openai":
   192→		if cfg.OpenAIAPIKey == "" {
   193→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("OPENAI_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   194→		}
   195→	default: // gemini
   196→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
   197→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   198→		}
   199→	}
   200→	return &Orchestrator{
   201→		store:     store,
   202→		llmConfig: cfg,
   203→	}, nil
   204→}
   205→
   206→// RunAgent executes the full agent lifecycle for a given agent run:
   207→//  1. Marks the run as "running".
   208→//  2. Loads the project and builds the agent prompt.
   209→//  3. Creates an ADK-Go LLM agent with project-scoped tools.
   210→//  4. Runs the agent and captures its final text response.
   211→//  5. Parses the response into a SemanticReport.
   212→//  6. Creates a SemanticRelease and links it to the agent run.
   213→//  7. Marks the run as "completed" (or "failed" on error).
   214→func (o *Orchestrator) RunAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) error {
   215→	// Mark as running.
   216→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "running"); err != nil {
   217→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to running: %w", err)
   218→	}
   219→
   220→	// Execute the agent; capture any error to mark the run as failed.
   221→	result, err := o.executeAgent(ctx, run)
   222→	if err != nil {
   223→		slog.Error("agent run failed", "run_id", run.ID, "err", err)
   224→		// Best-effort: mark the run as failed.
   225→		if statusErr := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "failed"); statusErr != nil {
   226→			slog.Error("agent: failed to mark run as failed",
   227→				"run_id", run.ID, "status_err", statusErr)
   228→		}
   229→		return err
   230→	}
   231→
   232→	// Link the semantic release to the agent run and mark completed.
   233→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx, run.ID, result.semanticReleaseID); err != nil {
   234→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run result: %w", err)
   235→	}
   236→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "completed"); err != nil {
   237→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to completed: %w", err)
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	// Send project-level notifications (best-effort; errors are logged, not returned).
   241→	o.sendProjectNotifications(ctx, run, result)
   242→
   243→	return nil
   244→}
   245→
   246→// agentResult holds the output of a successful agent execution, used to
   247→// construct notifications after the semantic release is persisted.
   248→type agentResult struct {
   249→	semanticReleaseID string
   250→	version           string
   251→	reportText        string
   252→	projectName       string
   253→}
   254→
   255→// executeAgent performs the actual LLM agent interaction and returns the
   256→// agentResult containing the semantic release ID and metadata for notifications.
   257→func (o *Orchestrator) executeAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) (*agentResult, error) {
   258→	// Load project.
   259→	slog.Info("agent: loading project", "run_id", run.ID, "project_id", run.ProjectID)
   260→	project, err := o.store.GetProject(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   261→	if err != nil {
   262→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("get project: %w", err)
   263→	}
   264→	slog.Info("agent: project loaded", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name)
   265→
   266→	// Extract target version from the agent run.
   267→	version := run.Version
   268→	if version == "" {
   269→		// Fallback: parse from trigger "auto:version:v1.10.15"
   270→		if strings.HasPrefix(run.Trigger, "auto:version:") {
   271→			version = strings.TrimPrefix(run.Trigger, "auto:version:")
   272→		}
   273→	}
   274→
   275→	// Build the agent using the shared constructor.
   276→	slog.Info("agent: building agent", "run_id", run.ID,
   277→		"provider", o.llmConfig.Provider, "model", o.llmConfig.Model,
   278→		"version", version)
   279→	agentInstance, err := BuildAgent(ctx, o.store, project, o.llmConfig, version)
   280→	if err != nil {
   281→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("build agent: %w", err)
   282→	}
   283→
   284→	// Create in-memory session service and a new session.
   285→	sessionService := session.InMemoryService()
   286→	createResp, err := sessionService.Create(ctx, &session.CreateRequest{
   287→		AppName: "changelogue",
   288→		UserID:  "system",
   289→	})
   290→	if err != nil {
   291→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create session: %w", err)
   292→	}
   293→	sess := createResp.Session
   294→
   295→	// Create the runner with logging plugin for agent trace visibility.
   296→	r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
   297→		AppName:        "changelogue",
   298→		Agent:          agentInstance,
   299→		SessionService: sessionService,
   300→		PluginConfig: runner.PluginConfig{
   301→			Plugins:      []*plugin.Plugin{loggingplugin.MustNew("agent_trace")},
   302→			CloseTimeout: 5 * time.Second,
   303→		},
   304→	})
   305→	if err != nil {
   306→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create runner: %w", err)
   307→	}
   308→
   309→	// Run the agent with a prompt requesting analysis.
   310→	slog.Info("agent: starting LLM run", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name, "version", version)
   311→	var userPrompt string
   312→	if version != "" {
   313→		userPrompt = fmt.Sprintf("Analyze version %s for this project. Cross-check all sources and produce a semantic release report.", version)
   314→	} else {
   315→		userPrompt = "Analyze the recent releases for this project and produce a semantic release report."
   316→	}
   317→	userMsg := genai.NewContentFromText(userPrompt, "user")
   318→
   319→	var finalText string
   320→	eventCount := 0
   321→	for event, err := range r.Run(ctx, "system", sess.ID(), userMsg, agent.RunConfig{}) {
   322→		if err != nil {
   323→			slog.Error("agent: run event error",
   324→				"run_id", run.ID,
   325→				"event_count", eventCount,
   326→				"err", err,
   327→			)
   328→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent run event error: %w", err)
   329→		}
   330→		eventCount++
   331→		if event != nil && event.IsFinalResponse() && event.Content != nil {
   332→			for _, part := range event.Content.Parts {
   333→				if part.Text != "" {
   334→					finalText += part.Text
   335→				}
   336→			}
   337→		}
   338→	}
   339→	slog.Info("agent: LLM run finished",
   340→		"run_id", run.ID,
   341→		"event_count", eventCount,
   342→		"output_length", len(finalText),
   343→	)
   344→
   345→	if finalText == "" {
   346→		slog.Error("agent: produced no output",
   347→			"run_id", run.ID,
   348→			"project_id", run.ProjectID,
   349→			"event_count", eventCount,
   350→		)
   351→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent produced no output")
   352→	}
   353→
   354→	// Parse the agent's response into a SemanticReport.
   355→	report, err := parseReport(finalText)
   356→	if err != nil {
   357→		slog.Warn("agent output was not valid JSON report, storing raw",
   358→			"run_id", run.ID, "parse_err", err, "raw_output", finalText)
   359→		// Fall back to storing the raw text as the summary.
   360→		report = &models.SemanticReport{
   361→			Summary: finalText,
   362→		}
   363→	}
   364→
   365→	reportJSON, err := json.Marshal(report)
   366→	if err != nil {
   367→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("marshal report: %w", err)
   368→	}
   369→
   370→	// Gather release IDs for the semantic_release_sources join table.
   371→	// Only link releases that match the target version.
   372→	releases, _, err := o.store.ListReleasesByProject(ctx, run.ProjectID, 1, 200)
   373→	if err != nil {
   374→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("list releases for semantic release: %w", err)
   375→	}
   376→	releaseIDs := make([]string, 0, len(releases))
   377→	for _, r := range releases {
   378→		if r.Version == version {
   379→			releaseIDs = append(releaseIDs, r.ID)
   380→		}
   381→	}
   382→
   383→	// Use the target version for the semantic release.
   384→	srVersion := version
   385→	if srVersion == "" {
   386→		srVersion = "unknown"
   387→		if len(releases) > 0 {
   388→			srVersion = releases[0].Version
   389→		}
   390→	}
   391→
   392→	now := time.Now()
   393→	sr := &models.SemanticRelease{
   394→		ProjectID:   run.ProjectID,
   395→		Version:     srVersion,
   396→		Report:      reportJSON,
   397→		Status:      "completed",
   398→		CompletedAt: &now,
   399→	}
   400→
   401→	if err := o.store.CreateSemanticRelease(ctx, sr, releaseIDs); err != nil {
   402→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create semantic release: %w", err)
   403→	}
   404→
   405→	slog.Info("agent run produced semantic release",
   406→		"run_id", run.ID,
   407→		"semantic_release_id", sr.ID,
   408→		"version", sr.Version,
   409→	)
   410→
   411→	return &agentResult{
   412→		semanticReleaseID: sr.ID,
   413→		version:           srVersion,
   414→		reportText:        finalText,
   415→		projectName:       project.Name,
   416→	}, nil
   417→}
   418→
   419→// defaultSenders returns a map of channel type to Sender for the supported
   420→// notification channel types (webhook, slack, discord).
   421→func defaultSenders() map[string]routing.Sender {
   422→	httpClient := &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}
   423→	return map[string]routing.Sender{
   424→		"webhook": &routing.WebhookSender{Client: httpClient},
   425→		"slack":   &routing.SlackSender{Client: httpClient},
   426→		"discord": &routing.DiscordSender{Client: httpClient},
   427→	}
   428→}
   429→
   430→// sendProjectNotifications looks up project-level subscriptions and sends a
   431→// notification to each channel with the semantic release report. This is
   432→// best-effort: errors are logged but do not propagate to the caller.
   433→func (o *Orchestrator) sendProjectNotifications(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun, result *agentResult) {
   434→	subs, err := o.store.ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   435→	if err != nil {
   436→		slog.Error("list project subscriptions for notification",
   437→			"project_id", run.ProjectID, "err", err)
   438→		return
   439→	}
   440→	if len(subs) == 0 {
   441→		slog.Debug("agent: no project subscriptions configured",
   442→			"project_id", run.ProjectID)
   443→		return
   444→	}
   445→
   446→	senders := defaultSenders()
   447→
   448→	msg := routing.Notification{
   449→		Title:   fmt.Sprintf("Semantic Release Report: %s %s", result.projectName, result.version),
   450→		Body:    result.reportText,
   451→		Version: result.version,
   452→	}
   453→
   454→	for _, sub := range subs {
   455→		ch, err := o.store.GetChannel(ctx, sub.ChannelID)
   456→		if err != nil {
   457→			slog.Error("get channel for project notification",
   458→				"channel_id", sub.ChannelID, "err", err)
   459→			continue
   460→		}
   461→
   462→		sender, ok := senders[ch.Type]
   463→		if !ok {
   464→			slog.Warn("unknown channel type for project notification", "type", ch.Type)
   465→			continue
   466→		}
   467→
   468→		if err := sender.Send(ctx, ch, msg); err != nil {
   469→			slog.Error("send project notification failed",
   470→				"channel", ch.Name, "type", ch.Type, "err", err)
   471→		} else {
   472→			slog.Info("project notification sent",
   473→				"channel", ch.Name, "project_id", run.ProjectID, "version", result.version)
   474→		}
   475→	}
   476→}
   477→
   478→// parseReport attempts to parse the agent's text output as a SemanticReport JSON.
   479→// It handles cases where the agent wraps the JSON in markdown code blocks.
   480→func parseReport(text string) (*models.SemanticReport, error) {
   481→	// Strip markdown code fences if present.
   482→	cleaned := strings.TrimSpace(text)
   483→	if strings.HasPrefix(cleaned, "```") {
   484→		// Remove opening fence (with optional language tag).
   485→		if idx := strings.Index(cleaned, "\n"); idx != -1 {
   486→			cleaned = cleaned[idx+1:]
   487→		}
   488→		// Remove closing fence.
   489→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(cleaned, "```"); idx != -1 {
   490→			cleaned = cleaned[:idx]
   491→		}
   492→		cleaned = strings.TrimSpace(cleaned)
   493→	}
   494→
   495→	var report models.SemanticReport
   496→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(cleaned), &report); err != nil {
   497→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parse report JSON: %w", err)
   498→	}
   499→
   500→	if report.Subject == "" && report.Summary == "" {
   501→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("report is missing required 'subject' or 'summary' field")
   502→	}
   503→
   504→	return &report, nil
   505→}
   506→

<system-reminder>

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"log/slog"
     6→	"net/http"
     7→	"os"
     8→	"os/signal"
     9→	"time"
    10→
    11→	"github.com/riverqueue/river"
    12→	agentpkg "github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/agent"
    13→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/api"
    14→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/db"
    15→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/ingestion"
    16→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/queue"
    17→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/routing"
    18→)
    19→
    20→func main() {
    21→	// Configure log level from LOG_LEVEL env (debug, info, warn, error).
    22→	logLevel := new(slog.LevelVar) // defaults to Info
    23→	switch os.Getenv("LOG_LEVEL") {
    24→	case "debug":
    25→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelDebug)
    26→	case "warn":
    27→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelWarn)
    28→	case "error":
    29→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelError)
    30→	}
    31→	slog.SetDefault(slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(os.Stderr, &slog.HandlerOptions{Level: logLevel})))
    32→
    33→	ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), os.Interrupt)
    34→	defer stop()
    35→
    36→	dbURL := envOr("DATABASE_URL", "postgres://localhost:5432/changelogue?sslmode=disable")
    37→	addr := envOr("LISTEN_ADDR", ":8080")
    38→	noAuth := os.Getenv("NO_AUTH") == "true"
    39→
    40→	// Database
    41→	pool, err := db.NewPool(ctx, dbURL)
    42→	if err != nil {
    43→		slog.Error("database connection failed", "err", err)
    44→		os.Exit(1)
    45→	}
    46→	defer pool.Close()
    47→
    48→	if err := db.RunMigrations(ctx, pool); err != nil {
    49→		slog.Error("migrations failed", "err", err)
    50→		os.Exit(1)
    51→	}
    52→
    53→	// API & routing store (created before River so we can register workers)
    54→	// The river client is set to nil initially; TriggerAgentRun (which needs it)
    55→	// won't be called until the server is up and the client is set below.
    56→	pgStore := api.NewPgStore(pool, nil)
    57→
    58→	// Register River workers
    59→	workers := river.NewWorkers()
    60→	notifyWorker := routing.NewNotifyWorker(pgStore)
    61→	river.AddWorker(workers, notifyWorker)
    62→
    63→	// Agent worker: requires an LLM API key. If unavailable, agent jobs
    64→	// will remain in the queue until the key is configured and the server
    65→	// is restarted.
    66→	llmProvider := envOr("LLM_PROVIDER", "gemini")
    67→	llmModelDefault := "gemini-2.5-flash"
    68→	if llmProvider == "openai" {
    69→		llmModelDefault = "gpt-5.2"
    70→	}
    71→	llmConfig := agentpkg.LLMConfig{
    72→		Provider:      llmProvider,
    73→		Model:         envOr("LLM_MODEL", llmModelDefault),
    74→		GoogleAPIKey:  os.Getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY"),
    75→		OpenAIAPIKey:  os.Getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"),
    76→		OpenAIBaseURL: os.Getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL"),
    77→	}
    78→	agentOrchestrator, err := agentpkg.NewOrchestrator(pgStore, llmConfig)
    79→	if err != nil {
    80→		slog.Warn("agent orchestrator not available — agent jobs will not be processed", "err", err)
    81→	} else {
    82→		agentWorker := agentpkg.NewAgentWorker(agentOrchestrator, pgStore)
    83→		river.AddWorker(workers, agentWorker)
    84→		slog.Info("agent worker registered")
    85→	}
    86→
    87→	riverClient, err := queue.NewRiverClient(pool, workers)
    88→	if err != nil {
    89→		slog.Error("river client failed", "err", err)
    90→		os.Exit(1)
    91→	}
    92→
    93→	// Now that the river client exists, set it on the existing pgStore so that
    94→	// all holders (including NotifyWorker) can enqueue agent jobs.
    95→	pgStore.SetRiverClient(riverClient)
    96→
    97→	if err := riverClient.Start(ctx); err != nil {
    98→		slog.Error("river start failed", "err", err)
    99→		os.Exit(1)
   100→	}
   101→
   102→	// Ingestion layer
   103→	ingestionStore := ingestion.NewPgStore(pool, riverClient)
   104→	svc := ingestion.NewService(ingestionStore)
   105→
   106→	loader := ingestion.NewSourceLoader(pool, http.DefaultClient)
   107→	orch := ingestion.NewOrchestrator(svc, loader, pool, 5*time.Minute)
   108→
   109→	broadcaster := api.NewBroadcaster()
   110→
   111→	mux := http.NewServeMux()
   112→
   113→	// Register all API v1 routes
   114→	api.RegisterRoutes(mux, api.Dependencies{
   115→		DB:                    pool,
   116→		ProjectsStore:         pgStore,
   117→		ReleasesStore:         pgStore,
   118→		SubscriptionsStore:    pgStore,
   119→		SourcesStore:          pgStore,
   120→		ChannelsStore:         pgStore,
   121→		ContextSourcesStore:   pgStore,
   122→		SemanticReleasesStore: pgStore,
   123→		AgentStore:            pgStore,
   124→		KeyStore:              pgStore,
   125→		HealthChecker:         pgStore,
   126→		Broadcaster:           broadcaster,
   127→		NoAuth:                noAuth,
   128→		IngestionService:      svc,
   129→		HTTPClient:            http.DefaultClient,
   130→	})
   131→
   132→	srv := &http.Server{Addr: addr, Handler: api.CORS(mux)}
   133→
   134→	// Start polling in background
   135→	go orch.Run(ctx)
   136→
   137→	// Start PostgreSQL LISTEN/NOTIFY → SSE bridge
   138→	go api.ListenForNotifications(ctx, pool, broadcaster)
   139→
   140→	// Start HTTP server
   141→	go func() {
   142→		slog.Info("server starting", "addr", srv.Addr)
   143→		if err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != http.ErrServerClosed {
   144→			slog.Error("server error", "err", err)
   145→		}
   146→	}()
   147→
   148→	<-ctx.Done()
   149→	slog.Info("shutting down")
   150→
   151→	shutdownCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
   152→	defer cancel()
   153→	riverClient.Stop(shutdownCtx)
   154→	srv.Shutdown(shutdownCtx)
   155→}
   156→
   157→func envOr(key, fallback string) string {
   158→	if v := os.Getenv(key); v != "" {
   159→		return v
   160→	}
   161→	return fallback
   162→}
   163→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

     1→package main
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"flag"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"log"
     8→	"log/slog"
     9→	"os"
    10→
    11→	"google.golang.org/adk/agent"
    12→	"google.golang.org/adk/cmd/launcher"
    13→	"google.golang.org/adk/cmd/launcher/full"
    14→
    15→	agentpkg "github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/agent"
    16→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/api"
    17→	"github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/db"
    18→)
    19→
    20→func main() {
    21→	projectID := flag.String("project-id", "", "Project ID to scope the agent to (required)")
    22→	flag.Parse()
    23→
    24→	// Configure log level from LOG_LEVEL env (debug, info, warn, error).
    25→	logLevel := new(slog.LevelVar) // defaults to Info
    26→	switch os.Getenv("LOG_LEVEL") {
    27→	case "debug":
    28→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelDebug)
    29→	case "warn":
    30→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelWarn)
    31→	case "error":
    32→		logLevel.Set(slog.LevelError)
    33→	}
    34→	slog.SetDefault(slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(os.Stderr, &slog.HandlerOptions{Level: logLevel})))
    35→
    36→	if *projectID == "" {
    37→		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "error: --project-id is required")
    38→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "usage: go run ./cmd/agent --project-id=<uuid> [web api webui]\n")
    39→		os.Exit(1)
    40→	}
    41→
    42→	ctx := context.Background()
    43→
    44→	// Database
    45→	dbURL := envOr("DATABASE_URL", "postgres://localhost:5432/changelogue?sslmode=disable")
    46→	pool, err := db.NewPool(ctx, dbURL)
    47→	if err != nil {
    48→		log.Fatalf("database connection failed: %v", err)
    49→	}
    50→	defer pool.Close()
    51→
    52→	store := api.NewPgStore(pool, nil)
    53→
    54→	// Validate project exists
    55→	project, err := store.GetProject(ctx, *projectID)
    56→	if err != nil {
    57→		log.Fatalf("project %q not found: %v", *projectID, err)
    58→	}
    59→	slog.Info("loaded project", "id", project.ID, "name", project.Name)
    60→
    61→	// LLM config (same env vars as server)
    62→	llmProvider := envOr("LLM_PROVIDER", "gemini")
    63→	llmModelDefault := "gemini-2.5-flash"
    64→	if llmProvider == "openai" {
    65→		llmModelDefault = "gpt-5.2"
    66→	}
    67→	llmConfig := agentpkg.LLMConfig{
    68→		Provider:      llmProvider,
    69→		Model:         envOr("LLM_MODEL", llmModelDefault),
    70→		GoogleAPIKey:  os.Getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY"),
    71→		OpenAIAPIKey:  os.Getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"),
    72→		OpenAIBaseURL: os.Getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL"),
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	agentInstance, err := agentpkg.BuildAgent(ctx, store, project, llmConfig, "")
    76→	if err != nil {
    77→		log.Fatalf("build agent: %v", err)
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	// Launch with ADK-Web UI
    81→	config := &launcher.Config{
    82→		AgentLoader: agent.NewSingleLoader(agentInstance),
    83→	}
    84→
    85→	l := full.NewLauncher()
    86→	if err := l.Execute(ctx, config, flag.Args()); err != nil {
    87→		log.Fatalf("launcher failed: %v\n\n%s", err, l.CommandLineSyntax())
    88→	}
    89→}
    90→
    91→func envOr(key, fallback string) string {
    92→	if v := os.Getenv(key); v != "" {
    93→		return v
    94→	}
    95→	return fallback
    96→}
    97→

    97→	}

    99→	f := &toolFactory{store: store, projectID: "proj-1"}
   100→

   129→	t.Run("custom pagination", func(t *testing.T) {

    15→// --- mock orchestrator store ---
    16→
    17→type mockOrchestratorStore struct {
    18→	mockDataStore // embeds AgentDataStore methods
    19→
    20→	project               *models.Project
    21→	agentRun              *models.AgentRun
    22→	projectSubscriptions  []models.Subscription
    23→	channels              map[string]*models.NotificationChannel
    24→	semanticRelease       *models.SemanticRelease
    25→	statusUpdates         []statusUpdate
    26→	agentRunResult        *agentRunResult
    27→	createSemanticReleaseErr error
    28→	sources               []models.Source
    29→	hasReleaseMap         map[string]bool
    30→
    31→	mu  sync.Mutex
    32→	err error
    33→}
    34→
    35→type statusUpdate struct {

    38→}

    45→func (m *mockOrchestratorStore) GetProject(_ context.Context, id string) (*models.Project, error) {
    46→	if m.err != nil {
    47→		return nil, m.err
    48→	}
    49→	if m.project != nil && m.project.ID == id {
    50→		return m.project, nil
    51→	}
    52→	return nil, errors.New("project not found")
    53→}
    54→
    55→func (m *mockOrchestratorStore) GetAgentRun(_ context.Context, id string) (*models.AgentRun, error) {

    58→	}
    59→	if m.agentRun != nil && m.agentRun.ID == id {
    60→		return m.agentRun, nil
    61→	}
    62→	return nil, errors.New("agent run not found")
    63→}

    65→func (m *mockOrchestratorStore) UpdateAgentRunStatus(_ context.Context, id, status string) error {
    66→	m.mu.Lock()
    67→	defer m.mu.Unlock()
    68→	m.statusUpdates = append(m.statusUpdates, statusUpdate{ID: id, Status: status})
    69→	return nil
    70→}

    79→}

   117→// --- mock sender for testing ---
   118→
   119→type mockNotifySender struct {
   120→	mu      sync.Mutex
   121→	sent    []routing.Notification
   122→	sendErr error
   123→}
   124→
   125→func (m *mockNotifySender) Send(_ context.Context, _ *models.NotificationChannel, msg routing.Notification) error {
   126→	if m.sendErr != nil {
   127→		return m.sendErr
   128→	}
   129→	m.mu.Lock()
   130→	defer m.mu.Unlock()
   131→	m.sent = append(m.sent, msg)
   132→	return nil
   133→}
   134→
   135→func (m *mockNotifySender) sentCount() int {
   136→	m.mu.Lock()
   137→	defer m.mu.Unlock()
   138→	return len(m.sent)
   139→}

   141→// --- tests ---
   142→
   143→func TestSendProjectNotifications_WithSubscriptions(t *testing.T) {
   144→	projectID := "proj-1"
   145→	channelID := "ch-1"
   146→
   147→	store := &mockOrchestratorStore{
   148→		project: &models.Project{ID: projectID, Name: "My Project"},
   149→		projectSubscriptions: []models.Subscription{
   150→			{ID: "sub-1", ChannelID: channelID, Type: "project", ProjectID: &projectID},
   151→		},
   152→		channels: map[string]*models.NotificationChannel{
   153→			channelID: {
   154→				ID:     channelID,
   155→				Name:   "test-webhook",
   156→				Type:   "webhook",
   157→				Config: json.RawMessage(`{"url":"http://example.com/hook"}`),
   158→			},
   159→		},
   160→	}
   161→
   162→	orch := &Orchestrator{store: store, llmConfig: LLMConfig{GoogleAPIKey: "test-key"}}
   163→
   164→	run := &models.AgentRun{ID: "run-1", ProjectID: projectID}
   165→	result := &agentResult{
   166→		semanticReleaseID: "sr-1",
   167→		version:           "v2.0.0",
   168→		reportText:        "Major release with breaking changes.",
   169→		projectName:       "My Project",
   170→	}
   171→
   172→	// sendProjectNotifications calls defaultSenders() which creates real HTTP
   173→	// senders. To test without making HTTP calls, we test that the store
   174→	// methods are called correctly. The real HTTP send will fail (no server),
   175→	// but that is logged and not returned as an error.
   176→	//
   177→	// To properly unit test the send path, we verify that
   178→	// ListProjectSubscriptions and GetChannel are called by checking that the
   179→	// method does not panic and the store state is accessed.
   180→	orch.sendProjectNotifications(context.Background(), run, result)
   181→
   182→	// Verify ListProjectSubscriptions was called (implicitly — if it returned
   183→	// subscriptions, GetChannel must have been called for the channel).
   184→	// The send itself will fail because there's no HTTP server, but that's
   185→	// expected and logged. The key assertion is that the method completes
   186→	// without error and accesses the store correctly.
   187→}
   188→
   189→func TestSendProjectNotifications_NoSubscriptions(t *testing.T) {
   190→	projectID := "proj-1"
   191→
   192→	store := &mockOrchestratorStore{
   193→		project:              &models.Project{ID: projectID, Name: "My Project"},
   194→		projectSubscriptions: []models.Subscription{}, // no subscriptions
   195→		channels:             map[string]*models.NotificationChannel{},
   196→	}
   197→
   198→	orch := &Orchestrator{store: store, llmConfig: LLMConfig{GoogleAPIKey: "test-key"}}
   199→
   200→	run := &models.AgentRun{ID: "run-1", ProjectID: projectID}
   201→	result := &agentResult{
   202→		semanticReleaseID: "sr-1",
   203→		version:           "v1.0.0",
   204→		reportText:        "Initial release.",
   205→		projectName:       "My Project",
   206→	}
   207→
   208→	// Should return immediately without attempting to send anything.
   209→	orch.sendProjectNotifications(context.Background(), run, result)
   210→}

   233→func TestSendProjectNotifications_ChannelNotFound(t *testing.T) {
   234→	projectID := "proj-1"
   235→
   236→	store := &mockOrchestratorStore{
   237→		project: &models.Project{ID: projectID, Name: "My Project"},
   238→		projectSubscriptions: []models.Subscription{

   244→	orch := &Orchestrator{store: store, llmConfig: LLMConfig{GoogleAPIKey: "test-key"}}
   245→
   246→	run := &models.AgentRun{ID: "run-1", ProjectID: projectID}
   247→	result := &agentResult{
   248→		semanticReleaseID: "sr-1",
   249→		version:           "v1.0.0",
   250→		reportText:        "Some report.",
   251→		projectName:       "My Project",
   252→	}
   253→
   254→	// Should log the channel error and continue without panicking.
   255→	orch.sendProjectNotifications(context.Background(), run, result)
   256→}

   323→func TestVersionPlaceholderSubstitution(t *testing.T) {
   324→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
   325→	if !strings.Contains(instruction, "{{VERSION}}") {
   326→		t.Fatal("DefaultInstruction must contain {{VERSION}} placeholder")
   327→	}
   328→
   329→	replaced := strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", "v1.10.15")
   330→	if strings.Contains(replaced, "{{VERSION}}") {
   331→		t.Fatal("replacement failed: still contains {{VERSION}}")
   332→	}
   333→	if !strings.Contains(replaced, "v1.10.15") {
   334→		t.Fatal("replacement failed: does not contain v1.10.15")
   335→	}
   336→}
   337→
   338→func TestCheckAllSourcesReady(t *testing.T) {
   339→	tests := []struct {
   340→		name     string
   341→		sources  []models.Source
   342→		hasMap   map[string]bool
   343→		version  string
   344→		expected bool
   345→	}{
   346→		{
   347→			name:     "no sources",
   348→			sources:  nil,
   349→			version:  "v1.0.0",
   350→			expected: true,
   351→		},
   352→		{
   353→			name: "all sources have version",
   354→			sources: []models.Source{
   355→				{ID: "s1"}, {ID: "s2"},
   356→			},
   357→			hasMap:   map[string]bool{"s1": true, "s2": true},
   358→			version:  "v1.0.0",
   359→			expected: true,
   360→		},
   361→		{
   362→			name: "one source missing",
   363→			sources: []models.Source{
   364→				{ID: "s1"}, {ID: "s2"},
   365→			},
   366→			hasMap:   map[string]bool{"s1": true, "s2": false},
   367→			version:  "v1.0.0",
   368→			expected: false,
   369→		},
   370→	}
   371→
   372→	for _, tt := range tests {
   373→		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
   374→			store := &mockOrchestratorStore{
   375→				sources:       tt.sources,
   376→				hasReleaseMap: tt.hasMap,
   377→			}
   378→			o := &Orchestrator{store: store}
   379→			got, err := o.checkAllSourcesReady(context.Background(), "proj-1", tt.version)
   380→			if err != nil {
   381→				t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
   382→			}
   383→			if got != tt.expected {
   384→				t.Errorf("got %v, want %v", got, tt.expected)
   385→			}
   386→		})
   387→	}
   388→}
   389→
   390→func TestParseReport_NewFormat(t *testing.T) {
   391→	input := `{
   392→		"subject": "Ready to Deploy: Geth v1.10.15",
   393→		"risk_level": "CRITICAL",
   394→		"risk_reason": "Hard Fork detected",
   395→		"status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified"],
   396→		"changelog_summary": "Fixes sync bug",
   397→		"availability": "GA",
   398→		"adoption": "12% updated",
   399→		"urgency": "Critical",
   400→		"recommendation": "Wait for 25% adoption.",
   401→		"download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"],
   402→		"download_links": ["https://example.com/release"]
   403→	}`
   404→
   405→	report, err := parseReport(input)
   406→	if err != nil {
   407→		t.Fatalf("parseReport: %v", err)
   408→	}
   409→	if report.Subject != "Ready to Deploy: Geth v1.10.15" {
   410→		t.Errorf("subject: got %q", report.Subject)
   411→	}
   412→	if report.RiskLevel != "CRITICAL" {
   413→		t.Errorf("risk_level: got %q", report.RiskLevel)
   414→	}
   415→	if len(report.StatusChecks) != 1 {
   416→		t.Errorf("status_checks: got %d", len(report.StatusChecks))
   417→	}
   418→}

    13→// limitations under the License.
    14→
    15→// Package geminitool provides access to Gemini native tools. You can use any
    16→// tool from genai with geminitool.New().
    17→//
    18→// For example, to create a Gemini retrieval tool:
    19→//
    20→//	geminitool.New("data_retrieval", &genai.Tool{
    21→//		Retrieval: &genai.Retrieval{
    22→//			ExternalAPI: &genai.ExternalAPI{
    23→//				Endpoint: ,
    24→//				AuthConfig:
    25→//			},
    26→//		},
    27→//	})
    28→//
    29→// Package also provides default tools like GoogleSearch.
    30→package geminitool
    31→
    32→import (
    33→	"fmt"
    34→
    35→	"google.golang.org/genai"
    36→
    37→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
    38→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
    39→)
    40→
    41→// New creates  gemini API tool.
    42→func New(name string, t *genai.Tool) tool.Tool {
    43→	return &geminiTool{
    44→		name:  name,
    45→		value: t,
    46→	}
    47→}
    48→
    49→// geminiTool is a wrapper around a genai.Tool.
    50→type geminiTool struct {
    51→	name  string
    52→	value *genai.Tool
    53→}
    54→
    55→// ProcessRequest adds the Gemini tool to the LLM request.
    56→func (t *geminiTool) ProcessRequest(ctx tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
    57→	return setTool(req, t.value)
    58→}
    59→
    60→// Name implements tool.Tool.
    61→func (t *geminiTool) Name() string {
    62→	return t.name
    63→}
    64→
    65→// Description implements tool.Tool.
    66→func (t *geminiTool) Description() string {
    67→	return "Performs a Google search to retrieve information from the web."
    68→}
    69→
    70→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
    71→func (t *geminiTool) IsLongRunning() bool {
    72→	return false
    73→}
    74→
    75→func setTool(req *model.LLMRequest, t *genai.Tool) error {
    76→	if req == nil {
    77→		return fmt.Errorf("llm request is nil")
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	if req.Config == nil {
    81→		req.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}
    82→	}
    83→
    84→	req.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, t)
    85→	return nil
    86→}
    87→

    24→// GoogleSearch is a built-in tool that is automatically invoked by Gemini 2
    25→// models to retrieve search results from Google Search.
    26→// The tool operates internally within the model and does not require or
    27→// perform local code execution.
    28→type GoogleSearch struct{}
    29→
    30→// Name implements tool.Tool.
    31→func (s GoogleSearch) Name() string {
    32→	return "google_search"
    33→}
    34→
    35→// Description implements tool.Tool.
    36→func (s GoogleSearch) Description() string {
    37→	return "Performs a Google search to retrieve information from the web."
    38→}
    39→
    40→// ProcessRequest adds the GoogleSearch tool to the LLM request.
    41→func (s GoogleSearch) ProcessRequest(ctx tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
    42→	return setTool(req, &genai.Tool{
    43→		GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{},
    44→	})
    45→}
    46→
    47→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
    48→func (t GoogleSearch) IsLongRunning() bool {
    49→	return false
    50→}
