     1→// Copyright 2025 Google LLC
     2→//
     3→// Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
     4→// you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
     5→// You may obtain a copy of the License at
     6→//
     7→//     http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
     8→//
     9→// Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
    10→// distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
    11→// WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
    12→// See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
    13→// limitations under the License.
    14→
    15→// Package tool defines the interfaces for tools that can be called by an agent.
    16→// A tool is a piece of code that performs a specific task. You can either define
    17→// your own custom tools or use built-in ones, for example, GoogleSearch.
    18→package tool
    19→
    20→import (
    21→	"context"
    22→
    23→	"google.golang.org/adk/agent"
    24→	"google.golang.org/adk/memory"
    25→	"google.golang.org/adk/session"
    26→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool/toolconfirmation"
    27→)
    28→
    29→// Tool defines the interface for a callable tool.
    30→type Tool interface {
    31→	// Name returns the name of the tool.
    32→	Name() string
    33→	// Description returns a description of the tool.
    34→	Description() string
    35→	// IsLongRunning indicates whether the tool is a long-running operation,
    36→	// which typically returns a resource id first and finishes the operation later.
    37→	IsLongRunning() bool
    38→}
    39→
    40→// Context defines the interface for the context passed to a tool when it's
    41→// called. It provides access to invocation-specific information and allows
    42→// the tool to interact with the agent's state and memory.
    43→type Context interface {
    44→	agent.CallbackContext
    45→	// FunctionCallID returns the unique identifier of the function call
    46→	// that triggered this tool execution.
    47→	FunctionCallID() string
    48→
    49→	// Actions returns the EventActions for the current event. This can be
    50→	// used by the tool to modify the agent's state, transfer to another
    51→	// agent, or perform other actions.
    52→	Actions() *session.EventActions
    53→	// SearchMemory performs a semantic search on the agent's memory.
    54→	SearchMemory(context.Context, string) (*memory.SearchResponse, error)
    55→
    56→	// ToolConfirmation returns a handler for checking the Human-in-the-Loop
    57→	// confirmation status for the current tool context. This should be used within a tool's logic
    58→	// *before* performing any sensitive operations that require user approval.
    59→	//
    60→	// Example Usage:
    61→	// if confirmation := ctx.ToolConfirmation(); confirmation == nil {
    62→	//     // Confirmation required, create confirmation or handle appropriately
    63→	//     ctx.RequestConfirmation("hint", payload)
    64→	// }
    65→	//
    66→	// The returned *toolconfirmation.ToolConfirmation object provides methods to check the actual
    67→	// confirmation state.
    68→	ToolConfirmation() *toolconfirmation.ToolConfirmation
    69→
    70→	// RequestConfirmation initiates the Human-in-the-Loop (HITL) process to ask the user for approval
    71→	// before the tool proceeds with a specific action. Call this method when a tool needs
    72→	// explicit user consent.
    73→	//
    74→	// This will typically result in the ADK emitting a special event
    75→	// (e.g., a FunctionCall like "adk_request_confirmation") to the client application/UI,
    76→	// prompting the user for a decision.
    77→	//
    78→	// Args:
    79→	//   - hint: A human-readable string explaining why confirmation is needed. This is usually
    80→	//     displayed to the user in the confirmation prompt.
    81→	//   - payload: Any additional data or context about the action requiring confirmation.
    82→	//
    83→	// Returns:
    84→	//   - nil: If the confirmation request was successfully enqueued or initiated within the ADK.
    85→	//     This indicates that the process of asking the user has begun. It does NOT mean the action
    86→	//     is approved. The tool's execution will likely pause or be suspended until the user responds.
    87→	//   - error: If there was a failure in initiating the confirmation process itself (e.g., invalid
    88→	//     arguments, issue with the event system). The request to ask the user has not been sent.
    89→	RequestConfirmation(hint string, payload any) error
    90→}
    91→
    92→// Toolset is an interface for a collection of tools. It allows grouping
    93→// related tools together and providing them to an agent.
    94→type Toolset interface {
    95→	// Name returns the name of the toolset.
    96→	Name() string
    97→	// Tools returns a list of tools in the toolset. The provided
    98→	// ReadonlyContext can be used to dynamically determine which tools
    99→	// to return based on the current invocation state.
   100→	Tools(ctx agent.ReadonlyContext) ([]Tool, error)
   101→}
   102→
   103→// Predicate is a function which decides whether a tool should be exposed to LLM.
   104→type Predicate func(ctx agent.ReadonlyContext, tool Tool) bool
   105→
   106→// StringPredicate is a helper that creates a Predicate from a string slice.
   107→func StringPredicate(allowedTools []string) Predicate {
   108→	m := make(map[string]bool)
   109→	for _, t := range allowedTools {
   110→		m[t] = true
   111→	}
   112→
   113→	return func(ctx agent.ReadonlyContext, tool Tool) bool {
   114→		return m[tool.Name()]
   115→	}
   116→}
   117→
   118→// FilterToolset returns a Toolset that filters the tools in the given Toolset
   119→// using the given predicate.
   120→func FilterToolset(toolset Toolset, predicate Predicate) Toolset {
   121→	if toolset == nil {
   122→		panic("toolset must not be nil")
   123→	}
   124→	if predicate == nil {
   125→		panic("predicate must not be nil")
   126→	}
   127→
   128→	return &filteredToolset{
   129→		toolset:   toolset,
   130→		predicate: predicate,
   131→	}
   132→}
   133→
   134→type filteredToolset struct {
   135→	toolset   Toolset
   136→	predicate Predicate
   137→}
   138→
   139→func (f *filteredToolset) Name() string {
   140→	return f.toolset.Name()
   141→}
   142→
   143→func (f *filteredToolset) Tools(ctx agent.ReadonlyContext) ([]Tool, error) {
   144→	tools, err := f.toolset.Tools(ctx)
   145→	if err != nil {
   146→		return nil, err
   147→	}
   148→	var filtered []Tool
   149→	for _, tool := range tools {
   150→		if f.predicate(ctx, tool) {
   151→			filtered = append(filtered, tool)
   152→		}
   153→	}
   154→	return filtered, nil
   155→}
   156→

     1→# Unified Agent Architecture Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Unify the Gemini and OpenAI agent flows to use the same sub-agent architecture via `agenttool`, with provider-specific search tools.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** Both providers use a 3-agent pattern: root → data sub-agent + search sub-agent. The search sub-agent uses `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` for Gemini and a new `openai.WebSearch{}` for OpenAI. The OpenAI wrapper translates the `GoogleSearch` marker on `genai.Tool` into `web_search_options: {}` in the HTTP body.
     8→

   211→func convertTools(req *model.LLMRequest) []chatTool {
   212→	if req.Config == nil || len(req.Config.Tools) == 0 {
   213→		return nil
   214→	}
   215→
   216→	var tools []chatTool
   217→	for _, t := range req.Config.Tools {
   218→		// Skip GoogleSearch markers — handled via web_search_options.
   219→		if t.GoogleSearch != nil {
   220→			continue
   221→		}
   222→		for _, fd := range t.FunctionDeclarations {
   223→			ct := chatTool{
   224→				Type: "function",
   225→				Function: chatFunction{
   226→					Name:        fd.Name,
   227→					Description: fd.Description,
   228→				},
   229→			}
   230→			if fd.Parameters != nil {
   231→				ct.Function.Parameters = schemaToMap(fd.Parameters)
   232→			}
   233→			tools = append(tools, ct)
   234→		}
   235→	}
   236→	return tools
   237→}
   238→```

   330→**Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

   332→Create `internal/agent/openai/websearch.go`:
   333→
   334→```go
   335→package openai
   336→
   337→import (
   338→	"fmt"
   339→
   340→	"google.golang.org/genai"
   341→
   342→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
   343→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
   344→)
   345→
   346→// WebSearch is a tool that enables OpenAI's built-in web search via
   347→// web_search_options. It mirrors geminitool.GoogleSearch by adding a
   348→// GoogleSearch marker to the request config, which the OpenAI wrapper
   349→// translates into the web_search_options HTTP body field.
   350→type WebSearch struct{}
   351→
   352→// Name implements tool.Tool.
   353→func (WebSearch) Name() string { return "web_search" }
   354→
   355→// Description implements tool.Tool.
   356→func (WebSearch) Description() string {
   357→	return "Search the web for additional context about a release."
   358→}
   359→
   360→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
   361→func (WebSearch) IsLongRunning() bool { return false }
   362→
   363→// ProcessRequest implements toolinternal.RequestProcessor by adding a
   364→// GoogleSearch marker to the LLM request. The OpenAI model wrapper
   365→// detects this marker and emits web_search_options in the HTTP body.
   366→func (WebSearch) ProcessRequest(_ tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
   367→	if req == nil {
   368→		return fmt.Errorf("llm request is nil")
   369→	}
   370→	if req.Config == nil {
   371→		req.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}
   372→	}
   373→	req.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, &genai.Tool{
   374→		GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{},
   375→	})
   376→	return nil
   377→}
   378→```

   464→### Task 5: Unify BuildAgent to use sub-agent pattern for both providers

   470→**Step 1: Rewrite BuildAgent**
   471→
   472→Replace the entire `BuildAgent` function (lines 77-147) with unified logic:
   473→
   474→```go
   475→func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {
   476→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
   477→	if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
   478→		instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
   479→	}
   480→	instruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", version)
   481→
   482→	llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig)
   483→	if err != nil {
   484→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create LLM model: %w", err)
   485→	}
   486→
   487→	// Create project-scoped function tools.
   488→	functionTools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)
   489→	if err != nil {
   490→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create agent tools: %w", err)
   491→	}
   492→
   493→	// Data sub-agent: handles DB queries for releases and context sources.
   494→	dataAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   495→		Name:        "data_agent",
   496→		Description: "Query project releases and context sources from the database. Use this to fetch release lists, release details, and context sources like runbooks and documentation.",
   497→		Model:       llmModel,
   498→		Tools:       functionTools,
   499→	})
   500→	if err != nil {
   501→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create data sub-agent: %w", err)
   502→	}
   503→
   504→	// Search sub-agent: provider-specific web search.
   505→	var searchTool tool.Tool
   506→	var searchModel model.LLM
   507→	switch llmConfig.Provider {
   508→	case "openai":
   509→		searchTool = oaimodel.WebSearch{}
   510→		// OpenAI web search requires a search-capable model.
   511→		searchModelName := llmConfig.OpenAISearchModel
   512→		if searchModelName == "" {
   513→			searchModelName = "gpt-5-search-api"
   514→		}
   515→		searchModel, err = NewLLMModel(ctx, LLMConfig{
   516→			Provider:      "openai",
   517→			Model:         searchModelName,
   518→			OpenAIAPIKey:  llmConfig.OpenAIAPIKey,
   519→			OpenAIBaseURL: llmConfig.OpenAIBaseURL,
   520→		})
   521→		if err != nil {
   522→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("create OpenAI search model: %w", err)
   523→		}
   524→	default: // gemini
   525→		searchTool = geminitool.GoogleSearch{}
   526→		searchModel = llmModel
   527→	}
   528→
   529→	searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   530→		Name:        "search_agent",
   531→		Description: "Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues.",
   532→		Model:       searchModel,
   533→		Tools:       []tool.Tool{searchTool},
   534→	})
   535→	if err != nil {
   536→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create search sub-agent: %w", err)
   537→	}
   538→
   539→	// Root agent orchestrates data lookup and web search via sub-agents.
   540→	return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   541→		Name:        "release_analyst",
   542→		Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   543→		Model:       llmModel,
   544→		Instruction: instruction,
   545→		Tools: []tool.Tool{
   546→			agenttool.New(dataAgent, nil),
   547→			agenttool.New(searchAgent, nil),
   548→		},
   549→	})
   550→}
   551→```
   552→
   553→Update imports — add the `oaimodel` import and `"google.golang.org/adk/model"`:
   554→

   557→	// ... existing imports ...

{
  "description": "Implement Task 1: schema casing",
  "prompt": "You are implementing Task 1 of a plan. Follow TDD strictly.\n\n## Context\nThe project is a Go application at /Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon. The `internal/agent/openai/openai.go` file contains a custom OpenAI wrapper for ADK-Go. The `schemaToMap()` function (around line 301) converts `genai.Schema` to JSON Schema maps for OpenAI. Currently it passes through the `Type` field as-is, but `genai.Schema` uses uppercase (`\"OBJECT\"`, `\"STRING\"`) while OpenAI expects lowercase (`\"object\"`, `\"string\"`).\n\n## Task\nFix schema type casing in OpenAI wrapper using TDD.\n\n## Steps\n\n### Step 1: Create test file `internal/agent/openai/openai_test.go`:\n\n```go\npackage openai\n\nimport (\n\t\"testing\"\n\n\t\"google.golang.org/genai\"\n)\n\nfunc TestSchemaToMap_LowercasesType(t *testing.T) {\n\ts := &genai.Schema{\n\t\tType: \"OBJECT\",\n\t\tProperties: map[string]*genai.Schema{\n\t\t\t\"request\": {Type: \"STRING\"},\n\t\t},\n\t\tRequired: []string{\"request\"},\n\t}\n\tm := schemaToMap(s)\n\n\ttyp, ok := m[\"type\"].(string)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"type field missing or wrong type\")\n\t}\n\tif typ != \"object\" {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"expected type 'object', got %q\", typ)\n\t}\n\n\tprops := m[\"properties\"].(map[string]any)\n\treqProp := props[\"request\"].(map[string]any)\n\treqType := reqProp[\"type\"].(string)\n\tif reqType != \"string\" {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"expected nested type 'string', got %q\", reqType)\n\t}\n}\n```\n\n### Step 2: Run test to verify it fails\nRun: `go test -v -run TestSchemaToMap_LowercasesType ./internal/agent/openai/...`\n\n### Step 3: Fix `schemaToMap()` in `internal/agent/openai/openai.go`\n- Add `\"strings\"` to imports\n- Change `m[\"type\"] = string(s.Type)` to `m[\"type\"] = strings.ToLower(string(s.Type))`\n\n### Step 4: Run test to verify it passes\nRun: `go test -v -run TestSchemaToMap_LowercasesType ./internal/agent/openai/...`\n\n### Step 5: Commit\n```bash\ngit add internal/agent/openai/openai.go internal/agent/openai/openai_test.go\ngit commit -m \"fix(openai): lowercase schema types for OpenAI compatibility\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>\"\n```\n\nIMPORTANT: Follow each step in order. Run the test BEFORE implementing to verify it fails. Then implement and verify it passes. Then commit.",

}

Task 1 is complete. Here is a summary of what was done:

## Summary

**Problem:** The `schemaToMap()` function in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go` was passing `genai.Schema.Type` values directly to OpenAI. Google's `genai.Schema` uses uppercase type names (`"OBJECT"`, `"STRING"`, etc.) while OpenAI's API expects lowercase JSON Schema types (`"object"`, `"string"`, etc.).

**Fix (2 files changed):**

1. **Test file created** -- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai_test.go`
   - `TestSchemaToMap_LowercasesType` verifies that both top-level and nested schema types are lowercased.
   - Confirmed the test failed (RED) before the fix was applied.

2. **Source fix** -- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go`
   - Added `"strings"` to imports (line 11).
   - Changed line 305 from `m["type"] = string(s.Type)` to `m["type"] = strings.ToLower(string(s.Type))`.
   - Since `schemaToMap` is called recursively for nested `Properties` and `Items`, the fix automatically applies to all nested schemas as well.

3. **Verified** the test passes (GREEN) after the fix.

4. **Committed** as `aa9a8ae fix(openai): lowercase schema types for OpenAI compatibility`.

The `internal/agent/openai/openai.go` file contains a custom OpenAI wrapper for ADK-Go. We need to add support for OpenAI's `web_search_options` feature, which is triggered when a `genai.Tool` has `GoogleSearch` set. The OpenAI wrapper should detect this marker and emit `\"web_search_options\": {}` in the HTTP request body instead of trying to convert it to a function tool.\n\nA test file already exists at `internal/agent/openai/openai_test.go` — ADD to it, don't overwrite.\n\n## Steps\n\n### Step 1: Add failing tests to `internal/agent/openai/openai_test.go`\n\nAdd these tests (you'll need to add `\"google.golang.org/adk/model\"` to the imports):\n\n```go\nfunc TestConvertTools_SkipsGoogleSearchMarker(t *testing.T) {\n\treq := &model.LLMRequest{\n\t\tConfig: &genai.GenerateContentConfig{\n\t\t\tTools: []*genai.Tool{\n\t\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\t\tFunctionDeclarations: []*genai.FunctionDeclaration{\n\t\t\t\t\t\t{Name: \"get_releases\", Description: \"Fetch releases\"},\n\t\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\t\tGoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{},\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\n\ttools := convertTools(req)\n\n\tif len(tools) != 1 {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"expected 1 tool, got %d\", len(tools))\n\t}\n\tif tools[0].Function.Name != \"get_releases\" {\n\t\tt.Errorf(\"expected tool name 'get_releases', got %q\", tools[0].Function.Name)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc TestHasWebSearch(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Run(\"with GoogleSearch marker\", func(t *testing.T) {\n\t\treq := &model.LLMRequest{\n\t\t\tConfig: &genai.GenerateContentConfig{\n\t\t\t\tTools: []*genai.Tool{\n\t\t\t\t\t{GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{}},\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !hasWebSearch(req) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"expected hasWebSearch to return true\")\n\t\t}\n\t})\n\n\tt.Run(\"without GoogleSearch marker\", func(t *testing.T) {\n\t\treq := &model.LLMRequest{\n\t\t\tConfig: &genai.GenerateContentConfig{\n\t\t\t\tTools: []*genai.Tool{\n\t\t\t\t\t{FunctionDeclarations: []*genai.FunctionDeclaration{{Name: \"test\"}}},\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t}\n\t\tif hasWebSearch(req) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"expected hasWebSearch to return false\")\n\t\t}\n\t})\n\n\tt.Run(\"nil config\", func(t *testing.T) {\n\t\treq := &model.LLMRequest{}\n\t\tif hasWebSearch(req) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"expected hasWebSearch to return false for nil ---

## Summary

### TDD Red Phase (failing tests)

Added two new test functions to `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai_test.go`:

- **`TestConvertTools_SkipsGoogleSearchMarker`** -- verifies that when a `genai.Tool` contains a `GoogleSearch` marker alongside a regular function declaration tool, `convertTools` returns only the function tool (1 item, not 2) and ignores the GoogleSearch entry.

- **`TestHasWebSearch`** -- three sub-tests verifying the `hasWebSearch` helper returns `true` when a `GoogleSearch` marker is present, `false` when only function tools exist, and `false` when `Config` is nil.

Both tests failed as expected (compilation errors: `hasWebSearch` undefined).

### TDD Green Phase (implementation)

Four changes in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/openai.go`:

1. **Added `webSearchOptions` type** -- empty struct used as the JSON value for `"web_search_options": {}`.

2. **Updated `chatRequest` struct** -- added `WebSearchOptions *webSearchOptions` field with JSON tag `"web_search_options,omitempty"`.

3. **Added `hasWebSearch` helper** -- iterates over `req.Config.Tools` and returns `true` if any tool has a non-nil `GoogleSearch` field.

4. **Updated `convertTools`** -- added `if t.GoogleSearch != nil { continue }` at the top of the tool iteration loop to skip GoogleSearch markers.

5. **Updated `doRequest`** -- after building config fields and before `json.Marshal`, checks `hasWebSearch(req)` and sets `chatReq.WebSearchOptions = &webSearchOptions{}` so the serialized HTTP body includes `"web_search_options": {}`.

### Verification

All 3 test functions (4 total sub-tests) pass:
- `TestSchemaToMap_LowercasesType` (pre-existing)
- `TestConvertTools_SkipsGoogleSearchMarker` (new)
- `TestHasWebSearch/with_GoogleSearch_marker` (new)
- `TestHasWebSearch/without_GoogleSearch_marker` (new)
- `TestHasWebSearch/nil_config` (new)

### Commit

     1→# Unified Agent Architecture Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Unify the Gemini and OpenAI agent flows to use the same sub-agent architecture via `agenttool`, with provider-specific search tools.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** Both providers use a 3-agent pattern: root → data sub-agent + search sub-agent. The search sub-agent uses `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` for Gemini and a new `openai.WebSearch{}` for OpenAI. The OpenAI wrapper translates the `GoogleSearch` marker on `genai.Tool` into `web_search_options: {}` in the HTTP body.
     8→

   330→**Step 3: Write minimal implementation**
   331→
   332→Create `internal/agent/openai/websearch.go`:
   333→
   334→```go
   335→package openai
   336→
   337→import (
   338→	"fmt"
   339→
   340→	"google.golang.org/genai"
   341→
   342→	"google.golang.org/adk/model"
   343→	"google.golang.org/adk/tool"
   344→)
   345→
   346→// WebSearch is a tool that enables OpenAI's built-in web search via
   347→// web_search_options. It mirrors geminitool.GoogleSearch by adding a
   348→// GoogleSearch marker to the request config, which the OpenAI wrapper
   349→// translates into the web_search_options HTTP body field.
   350→type WebSearch struct{}
   351→
   352→// Name implements tool.Tool.
   353→func (WebSearch) Name() string { return "web_search" }
   354→
   355→// Description implements tool.Tool.
   356→func (WebSearch) Description() string {
   357→	return "Search the web for additional context about a release."
   358→}
   359→
   360→// IsLongRunning implements tool.Tool.
   361→func (WebSearch) IsLongRunning() bool { return false }
   362→
   363→// ProcessRequest implements toolinternal.RequestProcessor by adding a
   364→// GoogleSearch marker to the LLM request. The OpenAI model wrapper
   365→// detects this marker and emits web_search_options in the HTTP body.
   366→func (WebSearch) ProcessRequest(_ tool.Context, req *model.LLMRequest) error {
   367→	if req == nil {
   368→		return fmt.Errorf("llm request is nil")
   369→	}
   370→	if req.Config == nil {
   371→		req.Config = &genai.GenerateContentConfig{}
   372→	}
   373→	req.Config.Tools = append(req.Config.Tools, &genai.Tool{
   374→		GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{},
   375→	})
   376→	return nil
   377→}
   378→```

---

## Report

### What I implemented

Created the `WebSearch` tool in the `internal/agent/openai` package. This tool mirrors `geminitool.GoogleSearch` from ADK-Go but is purpose-built for the OpenAI code path. It implements the `tool.Tool` interface plus the `toolinternal.RequestProcessor` interface (via `ProcessRequest`).

The mechanism: `WebSearch.ProcessRequest()` adds a `GoogleSearch` marker (`&genai.Tool{GoogleSearch: &genai.GoogleSearch{}}`) to the LLM request's config. The existing OpenAI model wrapper (already modified in Tasks 1 & 2) detects this marker via `hasWebSearch()` and translates it into `"web_search_options": {}` in the HTTP request body, while `convertTools()` skips it when building the function tool list.

### What I tested and test results

Followed TDD -- wrote failing tests first, then implementation.

4 tests, all passing:
- `TestWebSearch_Name` -- verifies name is `"web_search"`
- `TestWebSearch_IsLongRunning` -- verifies it returns `false`
- `TestWebSearch_ProcessRequest` -- verifies it initializes `Config`, appends a `GoogleSearch` marker tool
- `TestWebSearch_ProcessRequest_NilRequest` -- verifies it returns an error for nil request

Full package test suite (7 tests including 3 pre-existing) passes with no regressions. `go vet` clean.

### Files changed

- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch.go` -- **Created** -- `WebSearch` struct implementing `tool.Tool` + `ProcessRequest`
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch_test.go` -- **Created** -- 4 test functions

### Self-review findings

No issues found. The implementation:
- Exactly matches the spec
- Follows the same pattern as `geminitool.GoogleSearch` (value receiver on empty struct, same method signatures)
- Integrates cleanly with the existing `hasWebSearch()` and `convertTools()` functions in `openai.go`
- No overbuilding -- only what was requested

### Issues or concerns

None. The tool is ready for use in Task 5 (Unify BuildAgent) where it will be wired into the OpenAI search sub-agent as the counterpart to Gemini's `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}`.

I now have a complete picture. Here is my review.

---

## Code Review: Commit a858734 -- `feat(openai): add WebSearch tool for OpenAI web search`

### Files Reviewed

- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch.go` (new, 43 lines)
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch_test.go` (new, 48 lines)

---

### What Was Done Well

1. **Faithful mirroring of the upstream pattern.** The `ProcessRequest` method is nearly line-for-line identical to ADK's own `geminitool.GoogleSearch` and its `setTool` helper. The nil check, config initialization, and `Tools = append(...)` logic are correct.

2. **Good separation of concerns.** The tool is a standalone type with no dependencies on the rest of the openai package. It only marks the request; the actual translation of the `GoogleSearch` marker to `web_search_options` is handled by the OpenAI wrapper (commit d769f14), which is the right layering.

3. **Defensive nil-request guard.** The `ProcessRequest` method returns a clear error for nil `req`, matching the upstream ADK pattern.

4. **Tests pass and vet is clean.** All 4 test functions pass; `go vet` reports nothing.

5. **Clear, accurate documentation.** The godoc comment on the struct explains the full chain (marker -> wrapper -> HTTP body) in a single coherent sentence.

---

### Issues

#### Important (should fix)

**1. Missing compile-time interface assertion for `toolinternal.RequestProcessor`**

The `WebSearch` type is designed to implement `toolinternal.RequestProcessor` (the method comment on `ProcessRequest` explicitly says so), and the ADK runner discovers this interface via a type assertion at runtime. However, there is no compile-time check that the struct satisfies it. If the ADK ever changes the `RequestProcessor` signature, this would break silently at runtime.

The upstream `geminitool` package itself does not include this (`google_search.go` relies on `geminitool/tool_test.go` to verify it via runtime type assertion in tests). But since this project owns the tool, it should be explicit.

Recommended addition in `websearch.go`:

```go
import toolinternal "google.golang.org/adk/internal/toolinternal"

var _ toolinternal.RequestProcessor = WebSearch{}
```

**However** -- `toolinternal` is an `internal` package of the `adk` module, so it likely **cannot** be imported from outside the module. This is actually how ADK is designed: external consumers satisfy the interface by duck typing (matching the method signature). This means a compile-time assertion is **not possible** from outside the module.

Given that constraint, the next best thing is a **test-time assertion** that verifies the method signature matches. This is what ADK's own tests do. The test file should include something like:

```go
func TestWebSearch_ImplementsRequestProcessor(t *testing.T) {
    // Verify ProcessRequest method exists with the expected signature.
    // This catches drift if ADK changes the RequestProcessor interface.
    ws := WebSearch{}
    var req model.LLMRequest
    if err := ws.ProcessRequest(nil, &req); err != nil {
        t.Fatalf("ProcessRequest: %v", err)
    }
}
```

The existing `TestWebSearch_ProcessRequest` already covers this path, so the risk is low. I am downgrading this to a **suggestion** given the `internal` package constraint.

#### Suggestions (nice to have)

**2. Test for idempotency / pre-existing Config.Tools**

The current `TestWebSearch_ProcessRequest` starts with a fresh `&model.LLMRequest{}` (nil Config). It does not test the case where `req.Config` is already set and `req.Config.Tools` already has entries. This is the production path since `BuildAgent` will register function tools first, then the WebSearch tool.

Recommended additional test case:

```go
func TestWebSearch_ProcessRequest_PreExistingTools(t *testing.T) {
    ws := WebSearch{}
    req := &model.LLMRequest{
        Config: &genai.GenerateContentConfig{
            Tools: []*genai.Tool{
                {FunctionDeclarations: []*genai.FunctionDeclaration{{Name: "existing"}}},
            },
        },
    }
    if err := ws.ProcessRequest(nil, req); err != nil {
        t.Fatalf("ProcessRequest error: %v", err)
    }
    if len(req.Config.Tools) != 2 {
        t.Fatalf("expected 2 tools, got %d", len(req.Config.Tools))
    }
    if req.Config.Tools[1].GoogleSearch == nil {
        t.Error("expected GoogleSearch marker on appended tool")
    }
}
```

**3. The `fmt` import could be replaced with `errors`**

The `ProcessRequest` method uses `fmt.Errorf("llm request is nil")` with no format verbs. This works fine, but `errors.New` would be the idiomatic choice for a static error string. That said, the upstream `geminitool` uses `fmt.Errorf` for the same purpose, so consistency with the upstream pattern is a valid reason to keep it as-is.

**4. Tool name should match `geminitool.GoogleSearch` for symmetry (`google_search` vs `web_search`)**

The upstream `geminitool.GoogleSearch` returns `"google_search"` from `Name()`. This tool returns `"web_search"`. The name difference is intentional (reflecting the OpenAI feature name), but worth noting that any code doing tool-name-based lookups (e.g., filtering, logging) would need to handle both names. This is fine as currently written since the tool's `Name()` is never used for matching in the codebase -- the `hasWebSearch` function in `openai.go` checks `GoogleSearch != nil` on the marker, not the tool name.

---

### Plan Alignment

The implementation aligns with the stated plan: "Create WebSearch tool for OpenAI" that mirrors `geminitool.GoogleSearch`. The approach (GoogleSearch marker reuse, letting the OpenAI wrapper translate it) is architecturally sound and avoids duplicating the detection logic. No deviations from the plan.

---

### Verdict: **Approved**

The implementation is clean, minimal, and correctly mirrors the ADK pattern. All tests pass. The two suggestions above (pre-existing-tools test case, `errors.New` nit) are minor improvements that do not block merging. No critical or blocking issues.

## Task 4: Add OpenAISearchModel to LLMConfig -- Complete

### Changes Made

**1. `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/model.go`** -- Added `OpenAISearchModel` field to the `LLMConfig` struct:

```go
type LLMConfig struct {
	Provider string // "gemini" or "openai"
	Model    string // e.g. "gemini-2.5-flash", "gpt-5.2"

	// Gemini
	GoogleAPIKey string

	// OpenAI
	OpenAIAPIKey      string
	OpenAIBaseURL     string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
	OpenAISearchModel string // search-capable model for web search sub-agent
}
```

**2. `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go`** -- Wired the new field via the `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` env var with a default of `gpt-5-search-api`:

```go
llmConfig := agentpkg.LLMConfig{
	Provider:          llmProvider,
	Model:             envOr("LLM_MODEL", llmModelDefault),
	GoogleAPIKey:      os.Getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY"),
	OpenAIAPIKey:      os.Getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"),
	OpenAIBaseURL:     os.Getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL"),
	OpenAISearchModel: envOr("OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL", "gpt-5-search-api"),
}
```

**3. `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/agent/main.go`** -- Same change as the server entrypoint, adding `OpenAISearchModel` with the same env var and default.

   465→
   466→**Files:**

   470→**Step 1: Rewrite BuildAgent**
   471→
   472→Replace the entire `BuildAgent` function (lines 77-147) with unified logic:
   473→
   474→```go
   475→func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {
   476→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
   477→	if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
   478→		instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
   479→	}
   480→	instruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", version)
   481→
   482→	llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig)
   483→	if err != nil {
   484→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create LLM model: %w", err)
   485→	}
   486→
   487→	// Create project-scoped function tools.
   488→	functionTools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)
   489→	if err != nil {
   490→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create agent tools: %w", err)
   491→	}
   492→
   493→	// Data sub-agent: handles DB queries for releases and context sources.
   494→	dataAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   495→		Name:        "data_agent",
   496→		Description: "Query project releases and context sources from the database. Use this to fetch release lists, release details, and context sources like runbooks and documentation.",
   497→		Model:       llmModel,
   498→		Tools:       functionTools,
   499→	})
   500→	if err != nil {
   501→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create data sub-agent: %w", err)
   502→	}
   503→
   504→	// Search sub-agent: provider-specific web search.
   505→	var searchTool tool.Tool
   506→	var searchModel model.LLM
   507→	switch llmConfig.Provider {
   508→	case "openai":
   509→		searchTool = oaimodel.WebSearch{}
   510→		// OpenAI web search requires a search-capable model.
   511→		searchModelName := llmConfig.OpenAISearchModel
   512→		if searchModelName == "" {
   513→			searchModelName = "gpt-5-search-api"
   514→		}
   515→		searchModel, err = NewLLMModel(ctx, LLMConfig{
   516→			Provider:      "openai",
   517→			Model:         searchModelName,
   518→			OpenAIAPIKey:  llmConfig.OpenAIAPIKey,
   519→			OpenAIBaseURL: llmConfig.OpenAIBaseURL,
   520→		})
   521→		if err != nil {
   522→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("create OpenAI search model: %w", err)
   523→		}
   524→	default: // gemini
   525→		searchTool = geminitool.GoogleSearch{}
   526→		searchModel = llmModel
   527→	}
   528→
   529→	searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   530→		Name:        "search_agent",
   531→		Description: "Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues.",
   532→		Model:       searchModel,
   533→		Tools:       []tool.Tool{searchTool},
   534→	})
   535→	if err != nil {
   536→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create search sub-agent: %w", err)
   537→	}
   538→
   539→	// Root agent orchestrates data lookup and web search via sub-agents.
   540→	return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   541→		Name:        "release_analyst",
   542→		Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   543→		Model:       llmModel,
   544→		Instruction: instruction,
   545→		Tools: []tool.Tool{
   546→			agenttool.New(dataAgent, nil),
   547→			agenttool.New(searchAgent, nil),
   548→		},
   549→	})
   550→}
   551→```

    28→// OrchestratorStore defines all data access methods required by the agent
    29→// orchestrator to load project configuration, run the agent, and persist
    30→// the resulting semantic release.
    31→type OrchestratorStore interface {
    32→	AgentDataStore
    33→	GetProject(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.Project, error)
    34→	GetAgentRun(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.AgentRun, error)
    35→	UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx context.Context, id, status string) error
    36→	CreateSemanticRelease(ctx context.Context, sr *models.SemanticRelease, releaseIDs []string) error
    37→	UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx context.Context, id string, semanticReleaseID string) error
    38→	ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx context.Context, projectID string) ([]models.Subscription, error)
    39→	GetChannel(ctx context.Context, id string) (*models.NotificationChannel, error)
    40→	ListSourcesByProject(ctx context.Context, projectID string, page, perPage int) ([]models.Source, int, error)
    41→	HasReleaseForVersion(ctx context.Context, sourceID, version string) (bool, error)
    42→}
    43→
    44→const DefaultInstruction = `You are a release intelligence agent analyzing version {{VERSION}} of a software project.
    45→
    46→Focus ONLY on version {{VERSION}}. Cross-check this version across all available sources.
    47→
    48→Use the available tools to:
    49→1. Fetch releases and find the one matching {{VERSION}} from each source.
    50→2. Inspect release details (changelogs, commit data, raw payloads) for {{VERSION}} only.
    51→3. Check binary/image availability directly from the source data.
    52→4. Review the project's context sources (runbooks, documentation) for relevant background.
    53→5. Use web search ONLY when you need additional context not available from sources
    54→   (e.g., community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption stats, known issues).
    55→
    56→CRITICAL: Your final response MUST be a single JSON object and nothing else.
    57→Do not include any explanation, commentary, or markdown formatting — just the raw JSON.
    58→
    59→For download_links: prefer direct binary/artifact URLs for specific platforms (e.g., linux-amd64, darwin-arm64, windows-amd64 .tar.gz/.zip files) over generic release pages. Include the general release page URL as well, but prioritize direct download links when available.
    60→
    61→The JSON object must have exactly these fields:
    62→{
    63→  "subject": "Ready to Deploy: <Project> <Version> (<Risk Summary>)",
    64→  "risk_level": "CRITICAL|HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW",
    65→  "risk_reason": "Why this risk level (e.g., 'Hard Fork detected in Discord #announcements')",
    66→  "status_checks": ["Docker Image Verified", "Binaries Available"],
    67→  "changelog_summary": "One-line summary of key changes (e.g., 'Fixes sync bug in block 14,000,000')",
    68→  "availability": "GA|RC|Beta",
    69→  "adoption": "Percentage or recommendation (e.g., '12% of network updated (Wait recommended if not urgent)')",
    70→  "urgency": "Critical|High|Medium|Low",
    71→  "recommendation": "Actionable 1-2 sentence recommendation for the SRE team",
    72→  "download_commands": ["docker pull ethereum/client-go:v1.10.15"],
    73→  "download_links": ["https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-linux-amd64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://gethstore.blob.core.windows.net/builds/geth-darwin-arm64-1.10.15-8be800ff.tar.gz", "https://github.com/ethereum/go-ethereum/releases/tag/v1.10.15"]
    74→}`
    75→
    76→// BuildAgent creates the ADK-Go LLM agent for a given project. This is used
    77→// by both the production orchestrator and the dev entrypoint to ensure the
    78→// same agent configuration is used everywhere.
    79→func BuildAgent(ctx context.Context, store AgentDataStore, project *models.Project, llmConfig LLMConfig, version string) (agent.Agent, error) {
    80→	instruction := DefaultInstruction
    81→	if project.AgentPrompt != "" {
    82→		instruction = project.AgentPrompt + "\n\n" + instruction
    83→	}
    84→	// Substitute version placeholder
    85→	instruction = strings.ReplaceAll(instruction, "{{VERSION}}", version)
    86→
    87→	llmModel, err := NewLLMModel(ctx, llmConfig)
    88→	if err != nil {
    89→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create LLM model: %w", err)
    90→	}
    91→
    92→	// Create project-scoped function tools.
    93→	functionTools, err := NewTools(store, project.ID)
    94→	if err != nil {
    95→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create agent tools: %w", err)
    96→	}
    97→
    98→	// For Gemini: use sub-agent pattern (required because Gemini can't mix
    99→	// grounding tools like GoogleSearch with function tools on the same agent).
   100→	// For OpenAI: flat architecture — give function tools directly to root agent
   101→	// (OpenAI doesn't support the agenttool schema).
   102→	if llmConfig.Provider == "openai" {
   103→		return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   104→			Name:        "release_analyst",
   105→			Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   106→			Model:       llmModel,
   107→			Instruction: instruction,
   108→			Tools:       functionTools,
   109→		})
   110→	}
   111→
   112→	// Gemini path: sub-agent architecture.
   113→
   114→	// Data sub-agent: handles DB queries for releases and context sources.
   115→	dataAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   116→		Name:        "data_agent",
   117→		Description: "Query project releases and context sources from the database. Use this to fetch release lists, release details, and context sources like runbooks and documentation.",
   118→		Model:       llmModel,
   119→		Tools:       functionTools,
   120→	})
   121→	if err != nil {
   122→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create data sub-agent: %w", err)
   123→	}
   124→
   125→	// Search sub-agent: Google Search grounding.
   126→	searchAgent, err := llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   127→		Name:        "search_agent",
   128→		Description: "Search the web for additional context about a release. Use this ONLY when you need information not available from the project's sources, such as community sentiment, security advisories, network adoption statistics, or known issues.",
   129→		Model:       llmModel,
   130→		Tools:       []tool.Tool{geminitool.GoogleSearch{}},
   131→	})
   132→	if err != nil {
   133→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create search sub-agent: %w", err)
   134→	}
   135→
   136→	// Root agent orchestrates data lookup and web search via sub-agents.
   137→	return llmagent.New(llmagent.Config{
   138→		Name:        "release_analyst",
   139→		Description: "Analyzes upstream releases and produces semantic release reports.",
   140→		Model:       llmModel,
   141→		Instruction: instruction,
   142→		Tools: []tool.Tool{
   143→			agenttool.New(dataAgent, nil),
   144→			agenttool.New(searchAgent, nil),
   145→		},
   146→	})
   147→}
   148→
   149→// checkAllSourcesReady returns true if every source in the project has a
   150→// release matching the target version.
   151→func (o *Orchestrator) checkAllSourcesReady(ctx context.Context, projectID, version string) (bool, error) {
   152→	// Use a high limit to fetch all sources; projects rarely exceed a handful.
   153→	sources, _, err := o.store.ListSourcesByProject(ctx, projectID, 1, 1000)
   154→	if err != nil {
   155→		return false, fmt.Errorf("list sources: %w", err)
   156→	}
   157→	if len(sources) == 0 {
   158→		return true, nil
   159→	}
   160→
   161→	for _, src := range sources {
   162→		has, err := o.store.HasReleaseForVersion(ctx, src.ID, version)
   163→		if err != nil {
   164→			return false, fmt.Errorf("check source %s: %w", src.ID, err)
   165→		}
   166→		if !has {
   167→			slog.Info("agent: source not ready for version",
   168→				"project_id", projectID,
   169→				"source_id", src.ID,
   170→				"version", version,
   171→			)
   172→			return false, nil
   173→		}
   174→	}
   175→	return true, nil
   176→}
   177→
   178→// Orchestrator manages the lifecycle of an agent run: loading project config,
   179→// creating an ADK-Go agent with project-specific tools and instructions,
   180→// running the agent, and persisting the result as a semantic release.
   181→type Orchestrator struct {
   182→	store     OrchestratorStore
   183→	llmConfig LLMConfig
   184→}
   185→
   186→// NewOrchestrator creates a new Orchestrator. It validates that the provided
   187→// LLMConfig has the required API key for the configured provider. If the key
   188→// is missing, it returns an error so the caller can degrade gracefully.
   189→func NewOrchestrator(store OrchestratorStore, cfg LLMConfig) (*Orchestrator, error) {
   190→	switch cfg.Provider {
   191→	case "openai":
   192→		if cfg.OpenAIAPIKey == "" {
   193→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("OPENAI_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   194→		}
   195→	default: // gemini
   196→		if cfg.GoogleAPIKey == "" {
   197→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("GOOGLE_API_KEY is not set; agent orchestrator requires an LLM API key")
   198→		}
   199→	}
   200→	return &Orchestrator{
   201→		store:     store,
   202→		llmConfig: cfg,
   203→	}, nil
   204→}
   205→
   206→// RunAgent executes the full agent lifecycle for a given agent run:
   207→//  1. Marks the run as "running".
   208→//  2. Loads the project and builds the agent prompt.
   209→//  3. Creates an ADK-Go LLM agent with project-scoped tools.
   210→//  4. Runs the agent and captures its final text response.
   211→//  5. Parses the response into a SemanticReport.
   212→//  6. Creates a SemanticRelease and links it to the agent run.
   213→//  7. Marks the run as "completed" (or "failed" on error).
   214→func (o *Orchestrator) RunAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) error {
   215→	// Mark as running.
   216→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "running"); err != nil {
   217→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to running: %w", err)
   218→	}
   219→
   220→	// Execute the agent; capture any error to mark the run as failed.
   221→	result, err := o.executeAgent(ctx, run)
   222→	if err != nil {
   223→		slog.Error("agent run failed", "run_id", run.ID, "err", err)
   224→		// Best-effort: mark the run as failed.
   225→		if statusErr := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "failed"); statusErr != nil {
   226→			slog.Error("agent: failed to mark run as failed",
   227→				"run_id", run.ID, "status_err", statusErr)
   228→		}
   229→		return err
   230→	}
   231→
   232→	// Link the semantic release to the agent run and mark completed.
   233→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunResult(ctx, run.ID, result.semanticReleaseID); err != nil {
   234→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run result: %w", err)
   235→	}
   236→	if err := o.store.UpdateAgentRunStatus(ctx, run.ID, "completed"); err != nil {
   237→		return fmt.Errorf("update agent run status to completed: %w", err)
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	// Send project-level notifications (best-effort; errors are logged, not returned).
   241→	o.sendProjectNotifications(ctx, run, result)
   242→
   243→	return nil
   244→}
   245→
   246→// agentResult holds the output of a successful agent execution, used to
   247→// construct notifications after the semantic release is persisted.
   248→type agentResult struct {
   249→	semanticReleaseID string
   250→	version           string
   251→	reportText        string
   252→	projectName       string
   253→}
   254→
   255→// executeAgent performs the actual LLM agent interaction and returns the
   256→// agentResult containing the semantic release ID and metadata for notifications.
   257→func (o *Orchestrator) executeAgent(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun) (*agentResult, error) {
   258→	// Load project.
   259→	slog.Info("agent: loading project", "run_id", run.ID, "project_id", run.ProjectID)
   260→	project, err := o.store.GetProject(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   261→	if err != nil {
   262→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("get project: %w", err)
   263→	}
   264→	slog.Info("agent: project loaded", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name)
   265→
   266→	// Extract target version from the agent run.
   267→	version := run.Version
   268→	if version == "" {
   269→		// Fallback: parse from trigger "auto:version:v1.10.15"
   270→		if strings.HasPrefix(run.Trigger, "auto:version:") {
   271→			version = strings.TrimPrefix(run.Trigger, "auto:version:")
   272→		}
   273→	}
   274→
   275→	// Build the agent using the shared constructor.
   276→	slog.Info("agent: building agent", "run_id", run.ID,
   277→		"provider", o.llmConfig.Provider, "model", o.llmConfig.Model,
   278→		"version", version)
   279→	agentInstance, err := BuildAgent(ctx, o.store, project, o.llmConfig, version)
   280→	if err != nil {
   281→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("build agent: %w", err)
   282→	}
   283→
   284→	// Create in-memory session service and a new session.
   285→	sessionService := session.InMemoryService()
   286→	createResp, err := sessionService.Create(ctx, &session.CreateRequest{
   287→		AppName: "changelogue",
   288→		UserID:  "system",
   289→	})
   290→	if err != nil {
   291→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create session: %w", err)
   292→	}
   293→	sess := createResp.Session
   294→
   295→	// Create the runner with logging plugin for agent trace visibility.
   296→	r, err := runner.New(runner.Config{
   297→		AppName:        "changelogue",
   298→		Agent:          agentInstance,
   299→		SessionService: sessionService,
   300→		PluginConfig: runner.PluginConfig{
   301→			Plugins:      []*plugin.Plugin{loggingplugin.MustNew("agent_trace")},
   302→			CloseTimeout: 5 * time.Second,
   303→		},
   304→	})
   305→	if err != nil {
   306→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create runner: %w", err)
   307→	}
   308→
   309→	// Run the agent with a prompt requesting analysis.
   310→	slog.Info("agent: starting LLM run", "run_id", run.ID, "project", project.Name, "version", version)
   311→	var userPrompt string
   312→	if version != "" {
   313→		userPrompt = fmt.Sprintf("Analyze version %s for this project. Cross-check all sources and produce a semantic release report.", version)
   314→	} else {
   315→		userPrompt = "Analyze the recent releases for this project and produce a semantic release report."
   316→	}
   317→	userMsg := genai.NewContentFromText(userPrompt, "user")
   318→
   319→	var finalText string
   320→	eventCount := 0
   321→	for event, err := range r.Run(ctx, "system", sess.ID(), userMsg, agent.RunConfig{}) {
   322→		if err != nil {
   323→			slog.Error("agent: run event error",
   324→				"run_id", run.ID,
   325→				"event_count", eventCount,
   326→				"err", err,
   327→			)
   328→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent run event error: %w", err)
   329→		}
   330→		eventCount++
   331→		if event != nil && event.IsFinalResponse() && event.Content != nil {
   332→			for _, part := range event.Content.Parts {
   333→				if part.Text != "" {
   334→					finalText += part.Text
   335→				}
   336→			}
   337→		}
   338→	}
   339→	slog.Info("agent: LLM run finished",
   340→		"run_id", run.ID,
   341→		"event_count", eventCount,
   342→		"output_length", len(finalText),
   343→	)
   344→
   345→	if finalText == "" {
   346→		slog.Error("agent: produced no output",
   347→			"run_id", run.ID,
   348→			"project_id", run.ProjectID,
   349→			"event_count", eventCount,
   350→		)
   351→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("agent produced no output")
   352→	}
   353→
   354→	// Parse the agent's response into a SemanticReport.
   355→	report, err := parseReport(finalText)
   356→	if err != nil {
   357→		slog.Warn("agent output was not valid JSON report, storing raw",
   358→			"run_id", run.ID, "parse_err", err, "raw_output", finalText)
   359→		// Fall back to storing the raw text as the summary.
   360→		report = &models.SemanticReport{
   361→			Summary: finalText,
   362→		}
   363→	}
   364→
   365→	reportJSON, err := json.Marshal(report)
   366→	if err != nil {
   367→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("marshal report: %w", err)
   368→	}
   369→
   370→	// Gather release IDs for the semantic_release_sources join table.
   371→	// Only link releases that match the target version.
   372→	releases, _, err := o.store.ListReleasesByProject(ctx, run.ProjectID, 1, 200)
   373→	if err != nil {
   374→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("list releases for semantic release: %w", err)
   375→	}
   376→	releaseIDs := make([]string, 0, len(releases))
   377→	for _, r := range releases {
   378→		if r.Version == version {
   379→			releaseIDs = append(releaseIDs, r.ID)
   380→		}
   381→	}
   382→
   383→	// Use the target version for the semantic release.
   384→	srVersion := version
   385→	if srVersion == "" {
   386→		srVersion = "unknown"
   387→		if len(releases) > 0 {
   388→			srVersion = releases[0].Version
   389→		}
   390→	}
   391→
   392→	now := time.Now()
   393→	sr := &models.SemanticRelease{
   394→		ProjectID:   run.ProjectID,
   395→		Version:     srVersion,
   396→		Report:      reportJSON,
   397→		Status:      "completed",
   398→		CompletedAt: &now,
   399→	}
   400→
   401→	if err := o.store.CreateSemanticRelease(ctx, sr, releaseIDs); err != nil {
   402→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("create semantic release: %w", err)
   403→	}
   404→
   405→	slog.Info("agent run produced semantic release",
   406→		"run_id", run.ID,
   407→		"semantic_release_id", sr.ID,
   408→		"version", sr.Version,
   409→	)
   410→
   411→	return &agentResult{
   412→		semanticReleaseID: sr.ID,
   413→		version:           srVersion,
   414→		reportText:        finalText,
   415→		projectName:       project.Name,
   416→	}, nil
   417→}
   418→
   419→// defaultSenders returns a map of channel type to Sender for the supported
   420→// notification channel types (webhook, slack, discord).
   421→func defaultSenders() map[string]routing.Sender {
   422→	httpClient := &http.Client{Timeout: 10 * time.Second}
   423→	return map[string]routing.Sender{
   424→		"webhook": &routing.WebhookSender{Client: httpClient},
   425→		"slack":   &routing.SlackSender{Client: httpClient},
   426→		"discord": &routing.DiscordSender{Client: httpClient},
   427→	}
   428→}
   429→
   430→// sendProjectNotifications looks up project-level subscriptions and sends a
   431→// notification to each channel with the semantic release report. This is
   432→// best-effort: errors are logged but do not propagate to the caller.
   433→func (o *Orchestrator) sendProjectNotifications(ctx context.Context, run *models.AgentRun, result *agentResult) {
   434→	subs, err := o.store.ListProjectSubscriptions(ctx, run.ProjectID)
   435→	if err != nil {
   436→		slog.Error("list project subscriptions for notification",
   437→			"project_id", run.ProjectID, "err", err)
   438→		return
   439→	}
   440→	if len(subs) == 0 {
   441→		slog.Debug("agent: no project subscriptions configured",
   442→			"project_id", run.ProjectID)
   443→		return
   444→	}
   445→
   446→	senders := defaultSenders()
   447→
   448→	msg := routing.Notification{
   449→		Title:   fmt.Sprintf("Semantic Release Report: %s %s", result.projectName, result.version),
   450→		Body:    result.reportText,
   451→		Version: result.version,
   452→	}
   453→
   454→	for _, sub := range subs {
   455→		ch, err := o.store.GetChannel(ctx, sub.ChannelID)
   456→		if err != nil {
   457→			slog.Error("get channel for project notification",
   458→				"channel_id", sub.ChannelID, "err", err)
   459→			continue
   460→		}
   461→
   462→		sender, ok := senders[ch.Type]
   463→		if !ok {
   464→			slog.Warn("unknown channel type for project notification", "type", ch.Type)
   465→			continue
   466→		}
   467→
   468→		if err := sender.Send(ctx, ch, msg); err != nil {
   469→			slog.Error("send project notification failed",
   470→				"channel", ch.Name, "type", ch.Type, "err", err)
   471→		} else {
   472→			slog.Info("project notification sent",
   473→				"channel", ch.Name, "project_id", run.ProjectID, "version", result.version)
   474→		}
   475→	}
   476→}
   477→
   478→// parseReport attempts to parse the agent's text output as a SemanticReport JSON.
   479→// It handles cases where the agent wraps the JSON in markdown code blocks.
   480→func parseReport(text string) (*models.SemanticReport, error) {
   481→	// Strip markdown code fences if present.
   482→	cleaned := strings.TrimSpace(text)
   483→	if strings.HasPrefix(cleaned, "```") {
   484→		// Remove opening fence (with optional language tag).
   485→		if idx := strings.Index(cleaned, "\n"); idx != -1 {
   486→			cleaned = cleaned[idx+1:]
   487→		}
   488→		// Remove closing fence.
   489→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(cleaned, "```"); idx != -1 {
   490→			cleaned = cleaned[:idx]
   491→		}
   492→		cleaned = strings.TrimSpace(cleaned)
   493→	}
   494→
   495→	var report models.SemanticReport
   496→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(cleaned), &report); err != nil {
   497→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parse report JSON: %w", err)
   498→	}
   499→
   500→	if report.Subject == "" && report.Summary == "" {
   501→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("report is missing required 'subject' or 'summary' field")
   502→	}
   503→
   504→	return &report, nil
   505→}
   506→

## Summary

**File modified:** `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go`

### What changed

The `BuildAgent` function previously had two separate code paths:

1. **OpenAI path** -- a flat architecture where function tools were given directly to a single root agent (no sub-agents, no web search capability).
2. **Gemini path** -- a 3-agent hierarchy: root agent orchestrating a `data_agent` (DB tools) and `search_agent` (Google Search grounding) via `agenttool`.

After this change, **both providers use the same 3-agent hierarchy**:

- **`data_agent`** -- handles DB queries for releases and context sources using the project-scoped function tools.
- **`search_agent`** -- performs web search. The search tool and model vary by provider:
  - **Gemini**: uses `geminitool.GoogleSearch{}` with the main LLM model.
  - **OpenAI**: uses `oaimodel.WebSearch{}` (the tool created in Task 3) with a dedicated search-capable model (defaults to `gpt-5-search-api`, configurable via `LLMConfig.OpenAISearchModel`).
- **`release_analyst`** (root) -- orchestrates both sub-agents via `agenttool.New()`.

### Import additions

Two new imports were added to `orchestrator.go`:

```go
"google.golang.org/adk/model"                                      // for model.LLM type
oaimodel "github.com/sentioxyz/changelogue/internal/agent/openai"   // for oaimodel.WebSearch{}
```

### Verification

- `go build ./...` -- compiles successfully.
- `go test ./internal/agent/...` -- all tests pass in both `agent` and `agent/openai` packages.

---

## Code Review: Commit `34774d0` -- Unify BuildAgent for Both Providers

**Commit:** `feat(agent): unify BuildAgent to use sub-agent pattern for both providers`

### What Was Done Well

1. **Correct architectural decision.** Eliminating the branching code path (flat for OpenAI, sub-agents for Gemini) in favor of a single 3-agent hierarchy is a clear improvement. It reduces the surface area for provider-specific bugs and ensures both providers exercise the same orchestration logic.

2. **Clean diff.** The change is focused -- 16 lines removed, 24 lines added. It touches exactly one function (`BuildAgent`) in one file. No unrelated changes were bundled in.

3. **Proper error handling.** The new OpenAI search model creation has its own error wrapping (`"create OpenAI search model: %w"`) that is distinct from the root model error, which will aid debugging.

4. **Config wiring is consistent.** The `OpenAISearchModel` field is populated identically in both entrypoints (`cmd/server/main.go:77`, `cmd/agent/main.go:73`) using `envOr("OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL", "gpt-5-search-api")`.

5. **Tests pass.** `go vet` and `go test` both succeed for `./internal/agent/...` and `./internal/agent/openai/...`.

---

### Issues

#### Important (Should Fix)

**1. Duplicated default value for `OpenAISearchModel` across three locations.**

The fallback `"gpt-5-search-api"` appears in three places:
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go` line 77
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/agent/main.go` line 73
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` line 119

The first two are acceptable (environment-level defaults at config construction time), but the third one in `BuildAgent` creates a second layer of defaulting. If someone changes the default in the entrypoints but not in `orchestrator.go`, or vice versa, behavior will silently differ depending on whether `OpenAISearchModel` was set to empty string vs. never set. Since the entrypoints always populate the field via `envOr`, the check on line 118-119 is defensive but introduces a split source of truth.

**Recommendation:** Extract the default as a package-level constant:

```go
// in model.go or orchestrator.go
const DefaultOpenAISearchModel = "gpt-5-search-api"
```

Then reference it in all three locations. This is a minor but worthwhile consistency improvement.

---

#### Suggestions (Nice to Have)

**2. The removed comment `// Substitute version placeholder` was useful context.**

Line 86 in the diff shows that the comment `// Substitute version placeholder` was removed. While the `strings.ReplaceAll` call is self-documenting enough, the comment served as a section delimiter in a long function. This is purely stylistic and not blocking.

**3. No unit test exercises the new `BuildAgent` code path for `provider == "openai"` with sub-agents.**

The existing test `TestBuildAgentSignature` at `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator_test.go` line 450 only verifies the function signature compiles -- it does not actually call `BuildAgent` (because that requires a real LLM model). This is understandable since `BuildAgent` calls `NewLLMModel` which requires real API credentials. However, now that the OpenAI path creates a *second* model for the search sub-agent, there is more logic that is only tested via integration. Consider adding a test that validates the switch/case logic by mocking `NewLLMModel` or by extracting the search tool/model selection into a smaller testable function.

**4. The `WebSearch` tool reuses Gemini's `GoogleSearch` marker internally.**

In `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch.go` line 39-41, the `ProcessRequest` method appends `genai.GoogleSearch{}` as a marker. This works because the OpenAI model wrapper translates it, but it couples the OpenAI search tool to a Gemini-specific type name. This is an existing design choice from a prior commit, not introduced by this commit, so it is out of scope for this review -- but worth noting for future refactoring.

---

### Plan Alignment

The commit matches the described intent precisely:
- Single unified code path: **Yes** -- the `if llmConfig.Provider == "openai"` early return is fully removed.
- 3-agent hierarchy for both providers: **Yes** -- `data_agent`, `search_agent`, and `release_analyst` (root) are always created.
- Provider difference isolated to search tool and model: **Yes** -- the only branching is the `switch llmConfig.Provider` block at lines 113-133 that selects the search tool and model.

---

### Verdict: **Approved**

The change is clean, well-scoped, and architecturally sound. The one **Important** item (duplicated default constant) is a minor maintainability concern, not a correctness issue. No critical issues found.

Now I have all the information needed for a thorough review. Here is my assessment.

---

## Final Code Review: Unified Agent Architecture

### Verification Results

| Check | Status |
|-------|--------|
| `go test ./...` | All packages pass |
| `go vet ./...` | Clean - no issues |
| `go build ./cmd/server` | Builds successfully |

---

### Plan Alignment Analysis

The stated implementation goal has six items. Here is how each maps to the actual commits:

| Planned Item | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Schema type lowercasing in `schemaToMap` | Already present at d769f14 | Not part of this diff range -- was done in a prior commit (`aa9a8ae`). |
| 2. `web_search_options` / `hasWebSearch` / `convertTools` skipping GoogleSearch | Already present at d769f14 | Committed as `d769f14` itself. |
| 3. New `WebSearch` tool | Implemented in `a858734` | Clean implementation. |
| 4. `OpenAISearchModel` field + env var wiring | Implemented in `6e5cbe5` | Both entrypoints (`cmd/server` and `cmd/agent`) updated. |
| 5. Unified `BuildAgent` function | Implemented in `34774d0` | Replaces provider-branching with shared sub-agent pattern. |
| 6. README env var update | Implemented in `594143f` | Added `OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL` row. |

**Plan deviation -- extra changes found in this range:**

- **Commit `c7f384f`**: Notification format alignment across Slack, Discord, and webhook. This is outside the stated scope of "unified agent architecture." It modifies 4 files (`discord.go`, `slack.go`, `webhook.go`, `semantic-release-detail.tsx`) and `lessons.md`.
- **`cmd/server/main.go` line 114-117**: Frontend static asset serving was added. This snippet registers `api.RegisterFrontend(mux, "web/out")` and is unrelated to the agent unification.
- **`semantic-release-detail.tsx`**: Removed the `recommendation` pull-quote block from the web UI.
- **`slack.go`**: Removed the `Recommendation` section from Slack blocks.

These are meaningful, coherent changes on their own, but they are not part of the described plan for "unified agent architecture." They should be noted but are not problematic.

---

### What Was Done Well

1. **Clean abstraction**: The `WebSearch` tool in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch.go` correctly mirrors the `geminitool.GoogleSearch` pattern by adding a `GoogleSearch` marker to the request config, keeping the integration surface minimal and the OpenAI wrapper's `hasWebSearch` function working for both paths.

2. **Provider-agnostic architecture**: The unified `BuildAgent` in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go` (lines 81-156) elegantly handles both providers with a single sub-agent structure. The `switch` at line 113-133 is well-scoped -- only the search tool and search model vary by provider.

3. **Defensive programming**: The `WebSearch.ProcessRequest` method correctly handles nil request with an explicit error. The `OpenAISearchModel` fallback default is applied both in the entrypoints (`envOr("OPENAI_SEARCH_MODEL", "gpt-5-search-api")`) and in the orchestrator itself (line 118-119), providing defense in depth.

4. **Test coverage**: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/openai/websearch_test.go` covers name, long-running flag, happy path process request, and nil request error path. All four cases are relevant.

5. **Consistent env var wiring**: Both `cmd/server/main.go` and `cmd/agent/main.go` wire `OpenAISearchModel` identically, preventing configuration drift.

6. **Discord notification upgrade**: The `buildSemanticEmbed` function in `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/discord.go` is well-structured with proper truncation handling at the embed description limit, risk-based color coding, and graceful fallback for non-semantic-report messages.

---

### Issues

#### Important (should fix)

**1. Blank line left behind in frontend component**

File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/components/semantic-releases/semantic-release-detail.tsx`, lines 598-599

The recommendation block was removed but left a blank line (`\n\n`) where it used to be. This is a cosmetic leftover:

```tsx
          )}


        </div>
```

Should be cleaned to a single blank line or none, to match the rest of the file's formatting.

**2. Recommendation removed from Slack and web UI but still in the data model and agent prompt**

The `Recommendation` field is still defined in:
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/models/semantic_release.go:23` -- `Recommendation string json:"recommendation"`
- `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/agent/orchestrator.go:73` -- the agent prompt still instructs the LLM to generate `"recommendation": "..."` as part of its JSON output

Yet the Slack blocks no longer render it (removed in this diff), and the web UI no longer renders it (removed in this diff). Discord's `buildSemanticEmbed` also does not render it. The webhook's `webhookSemanticPayload` passes the full `SemanticReport` struct, so it will include recommendation in the JSON payload -- creating an inconsistency: webhook consumers see it, but Slack/Discord/web UI users do not.

This is a half-removal. Either:
- Keep `Recommendation` and display it in all channels, or
- Remove it from the model, agent prompt, and all test fixtures to complete the deprecation.

**3. Unrelated frontend serving change snuck in**

File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/cmd/server/main.go`, lines 114-117:

```go
if info, err := os.Stat("web/out"); err == nil && info.IsDir() {
    api.RegisterFrontend(mux, "web/out")
    slog.Info("serving frontend from web/out")
}
```

This is functional and harmless, but it was not part of the stated plan. It should be committed separately with its own commit message for traceability.

#### Suggestions (nice to have)

**4. `WebSearch` does not implement the full `tool.Tool` interface's `Schema` or `Run` methods**

The `WebSearch` struct only implements `Name()`, `Description()`, `IsLongRunning()`, and `ProcessRequest()`. It appears to work because ADK-Go's `tool.Tool` interface is satisfied, and the search is triggered via the `ProcessRequest` hook rather than via `Run`. However, there is no documentation comment on `WebSearch` explaining that this tool is intentionally a "marker tool" that never gets called via `Run()`. A brief comment would help future maintainers understand this pattern.

**5. Discord `CRITICAL` color is near-black (`0x111113`)**

File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/discord.go`, line 48:

```go
case "CRITICAL":
    return 0x111113 // near-black
```

Near-black is very subtle on Discord's dark theme where the sidebar/background is already dark. A high-contrast color (e.g., deep purple `0x7B2D8B` or bright red-black `0x8B0000`) would be more visible. This is purely aesthetic and subjective.

**6. `buildSemanticEmbed` receives `version` parameter but does not use it**

File: `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/internal/routing/discord.go`, line 61:

```go
func buildSemanticEmbed(title string, version string, report *models.SemanticReport) discordEmbed {
```

The `version` parameter is never referenced in the function body. It should either be used (e.g., added as a field in the embed) or removed from the signature.

---

### Summary

The core implementation is solid. The unified `BuildAgent` function successfully collapses the Gemini and OpenAI paths into a single sub-agent architecture with provider-specific search tools. The `WebSearch` tool cleanly mirrors the Gemini `GoogleSearch` pattern without requiring OpenAI-specific changes to the runner framework. All tests pass, the binary builds, and the code follows the project's established patterns.

The main area needing attention is the inconsistent treatment of the `Recommendation` field (issue #2) -- it was removed from display channels but lives on in the model and agent prompt, creating a mismatch that will confuse future readers. The unrelated changes (frontend serving, notification formatting) should ideally have been in separate commits for cleaner history, but they are not harmful.

    17→const defaultBaseURL = "https://api.openai.com/v1"
    18→
    19→// Config holds connection parameters for an OpenAI-compatible API.
    20→type Config struct {
    21→	APIKey  string
    22→	BaseURL string // defaults to https://api.openai.com/v1
    23→}
    24→
    25→// openaiModel implements model.LLM for OpenAI-compatible chat completion APIs.
    26→type openaiModel struct {
    27→	name    string
    28→	apiKey  string
    29→	baseURL string
    30→	client  *http.Client
    31→}
    32→
    33→// NewModel creates an OpenAI-backed model.LLM.
    34→func NewModel(_ context.Context, modelName string, cfg Config) (model.LLM, error) {
    35→	if cfg.APIKey == "" {
    36→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("openai: API key is required")
    37→	}
    38→	baseURL := cfg.BaseURL
    39→	if baseURL == "" {
    40→		baseURL = defaultBaseURL
    41→	}
    42→	return &openaiModel{
    43→		name:    modelName,
    44→		apiKey:  cfg.APIKey,
    45→		baseURL: baseURL,
    46→		client:  &http.Client{},
    47→	}, nil
    48→}
    49→
    50→func (m *openaiModel) Name() string { return m.name }
    51→
    52→// GenerateContent converts the ADK request to an OpenAI chat completion request,
    53→// calls the API, and converts the response back to an LLMResponse.
    54→// Streaming is not implemented; the iterator always yields a single response.
    55→func (m *openaiModel) GenerateContent(ctx context.Context, req *model.LLMRequest, _ bool) iter.Seq2[*model.LLMResponse, error] {
    56→	return func(yield func(*model.LLMResponse, error) bool) {
    57→		resp, err := m.doRequest(ctx, req)
    58→		if err != nil {
    59→			yield(nil, err)
    60→			return
    61→		}
    62→		yield(resp, nil)
    63→	}
    64→}
    65→
    66→// Close is a no-op; the HTTP client has no persistent state.
    67→func (m *openaiModel) Close() {}
    68→
    69→// --- OpenAI API types ---
    70→
    71→type webSearchOptions struct{}
    72→

   112→}

   128→	TotalTokens      int32 `json:"total_tokens"`
   129→}
